# Category : Web Enhancements

> Source: [https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Category:Web_Enhancements](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Category:Web_Enhancements) · Retrieved 2026-10-09T08:03:31Z.

During the publication of *Saga Edition*, *Wizards of the Coast* released a number of web articles online that provided additional characters, species, planets, and Gamemaster resources. For convenience sake, these Web Enhancements have been compiled together. Additionally, in collaboration with the *Star Wars Miniatures*, some additional Heroes, Villains, and specialized Equipment were also made available.

## About the Authors

**Eric Cagle** cut his teeth at *Wizards of the Coast* but now lives the extravagant freelancer lifestyle. Look for his name on everything from *Dungeons & Dragons* to *d20 Modern* and *Star Wars* products. Recent credits include the *Grimm* roleplaying game, *Tome of Salvation*, and *Hollow Earth Expedition*. Eric is currently the Managing Editor for *No Quarter Magazine*. He lives in Seattle, where the coffee is dark and bitter- like his goddesses.

**Matthew Grau** has been a professional writer and intellectual property developer since the age of 17. He began his career in the adventure games industry, writing books and designing game rules for such popular titles and licenses as *Dungeons & Dragons*, *Marvel Super Heroes*, *Frank Herbert's Dune*, and *Star Trek*. In the late 90s, Matthew moved into the film and television arena. He has written scripts and directed productions for clients such as Microsoft and Nintendo, and he created and wrote the *Curiosity Cafe* television show and kick-off feature film on a first-look agreement with the Jim Henson Company. He is an award-winning screenwriter with projects currently in the Hollywood studio system.

**Sterling Hershey** is an architect and freelance game designer. He regularly creates *Star Wars Miniatures* and *Starship Battles* scenarios and previews for the *Wizards of the Coast* website. He also wrote the *Scenario Book* for the *Attack on Endor* Scenario Pack. Previously, he authored and contributed to many *Star Wars The Roleplaying Game* products from West End Games, as well as *Star Wars Gamer* Magazine. Gaming in a galaxy far, far away is the focus of his starwars.com blog, *Delusions of Grandeur*. Sterling lives in the Midwest with his wife, Mary.

**Jack Irons** is an artist and graphic designer. He has been fan of *Star Wars* since he was four, when his dad took his sister and him to a matinee on the second day of its release. He posts regularly on the *Star Wars Miniatures* Game boards under the name "ironlightsaber" and often works with other board members on projects such as the Lost Twenty fan set. Jack lives in the middle of the United States with his wife Ruth and their son.

**Gary M. Sarli** is a freelance designer, developer, and editor whose credits include the *Saga Edition* rulebook and *Ultimate Adversaries* (*Star Wars Roleplaying Game*) and *Power of Faerûn* and *Fiendish Codex II: Tyrants of the Nine Hells* (*Dungeons & Dragons*). He also moderates on the Wizards.COMmunity message boards as WizO the Hutt, cheerfully feeding Code of Conduct violators to the Sarlacc. Gary recently opened GMSarli Games, an online retailer specializing in non-random miniatures packs designed for the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

**Owen Kirker Clifford Stephens** was born in 1970 in Norman, Oklahoma. He was introduced to gaming by his uncle in 1978 and was instantly hooked. He later enrolled in creative writing courses taught by *Forgotten Realms* novel author Mel Odom. In 1997, he attended a TSR Writer's Workshop, and later that year, his first professional work saw the light of day: an article on elven names in issue 251 of *Dragon* magazine.

In 2000, Owen moved to the Seattle area with his wife and three cats after accepting a job as a Game Designer at *Wizards of the Coast*. There, he worked on numerous *Star Wars Roleplaying Game* products and the *Wheel of Time Roleplaying Game*. Fourteen months later, he returned to Oklahoma and picked up his freelance career, writing for numerous companies, including Green Ronin, Paizo, White Wolf, and Steve Jackson Games. Owen co-authored the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition [Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* and has contributed to numerous licensed RPG products, including *The Wheel of Time*, *EverQuest*, *Black Company*, and *Thieves' World*.

**Patrick Stutzman** is a freelance game designer whose credits include *[Threats of the Galaxy](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Threats_of_the_Galaxy "Threats of the Galaxy")* and the upcoming *[Clone Wars Campaign Guide](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Clone_Wars_Campaign_Guide "Clone Wars Campaign Guide")* for the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition*. He also maintains his Web site, *Star Wars: The Forgotten Tales*, and works on SWRPGNetwork under the name of Jan Tolbara. Currently, he lives in Kansas City with his wife, two children, and an annoying Force spirit that follows him wherever he goes. If you have any tips on how to get rid of this spirit, let him know.

## [25 to Rescue](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/25_to_Rescue "25 to Rescue")

*By Sterling Hershey*

In the midst of [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars"), Separatist invaders lay siege to the planet [Christophsis](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Christophsis "Christophsis"), pounding Republic defenders throughout the planet's crystalline cities. During the fighting, clone forces learn that the enemy has captured 25 technicians and other civilians. In a hastily arranged rescue mission, nearby heroes must rush to save as many prisoners as possible before a [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") arrives to transport them offworld. With the majority of clone troops and Jedi engaged elsewhere, it's up to the heroes to carry the day on their own.

## [Point-Blank RPG](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Point-Blank_RPG "Point-Blank RPG")

*By Sterling Hershey*

Late in [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars"), the Republic dislodges the Separatists from their bases and holdings in [The Core Worlds](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Core_Worlds?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Core Worlds (page does not exist)"). Damaged but not defeated, Separatist forces withdraw and regroup in fortified regions in [The Outer Rim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Outer_Rim?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Outer Rim (page does not exist)"), pursued by determined Republic fleets. Along the way, the Separatists attempt to divert the Republic forces into spending time and resources engaging lesser targets.

One of these targets is a Separatist base on the planet [Garia](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Garia?action=edit&redlink=1 "Garia (page does not exist)") in the Brak Sector, located close to the Corellian Run. An active Separatist base so near the important hyperspace trade route could threaten Republic fleets, disrupt their supply lines, and target vulnerable civilian vessels. In one of many such actions, clone units detach from the fleet to attack the base directly. After a brief orbital and air bombardment is cut short by the activation of an unexpected shield, Republic walkers and ground forces blast the base and its outer defenses.

In the midst of the battle, an [AT-TE](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/AT-TE "AT-TE") leads the assault on the base's [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") control and command center. A direct hit from a volley of missiles disables the armored beast at the fortified entrance. Immobile, but still armed, the [AT-TE](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/AT-TE "AT-TE") faces down the [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") at point blank range. The heroes must rush to its aid and sustain the momentum of the attack. However, the [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") have a surprise of their own- agile Commando Droids are en route to deal with the heavily armed clones. The success of the attack hinges greatly on this pivotal moment.

## [Iridonian Darkness](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Iridonian_Darkness "Iridonian Darkness")

*By Sterling Hershey*

*Iridonian Darkness* is a full-length adventure for the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition*. It uses the *[Knights of the Old Republic Campaign Guide](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knights_of_the_Old_Republic_Campaign_Guide "Knights of the Old Republic Campaign Guide")* and the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")*. Both books are required to use all of the elements in this adventure. *Iridonian Darkness* is designed for four to six 5th-level characters, but you can adapt it for characters of other levels. The adventure takes place near the beginning of [The Jedi Civil War](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi_Civil_War "The Jedi Civil War"), but you can adapt it to other time frames and locations in [The Old Republic Era](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Old_Republic_Era "The Old Republic Era").

## [The Betrayal of Darth Revan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Betrayal_of_Darth_Revan "The Betrayal of Darth Revan")

*By Patrick Stutzman*

It is two years into [The Jedi Civil War](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi_Civil_War "The Jedi Civil War") and [The Old Republic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Old_Republic "The Old Republic"), already recovering from the war against [The Mandalorians](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Mandalorians "The Mandalorians"), is struggling against the might of the fleets led by the former Jedi: [Revan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Revan "Revan") and [Malak](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Malak "Malak"). The pair, heroes of [The Mandalorian Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Mandalorian_Wars "The Mandalorian Wars") along with the other Jedi that had flocked to their banner, have now become the scourge of the galaxy. [Telos](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Telos "Telos") still burns from their surprise attack, and numerous other planets have fallen to the Sith forces.

Now, a plan has been put into motion by [The Old Republic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Old_Republic "The Old Republic") and [The Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi "The Jedi") to trap [Revan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Revan "Revan") and [Malak](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Malak "Malak"). A six-being strike team has been assembled to- under the leadership of Jedi [Bastila Shan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bastila_Shan "Bastila Shan") and other Jedi- go into Revan's flagship itself and confront the Sith.

## Web Enhancements

The Tech SpecialistEncounter Design 101DevaroniansThe Pau’ansThe Nazren

*By Rodney Thompson & Gary M. Sarli*

A favored archetype of long-time *Star Wars* gamers, the tech specialist is a character that focuses on modifying and enhanced fantastic technology to fit his or her needs. Tech specialists range from brilliant engineers to prodigious outlaw techs, though all share a love for the guns, gear, and gadgets found in the galaxy far, far away.

This web enhancement includes a few rules (cut from the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* for space) that allow heroes to take on the role of the tech specialist. Additionally, you will find sample heroic characters- a [Zabrak Engineer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Zabrak_Engineer "Zabrak Engineer") and a [Mon Calamari Outlaw Tech](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mon_Calamari_Outlaw_Tech "Mon Calamari Outlaw Tech")- that illustrate two ways to build tech-oriented characters for your own campaign. Of course, these are just two examples, and a hero who wants to tinker with machines and electronics has a number of options available across many different classes.

Additionally, these rules will be featured in an upcoming sourcebook for reference at the gaming table, and will be expanded upon with more [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and rules to further enhance your game.

- New [Outlaw Tech Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Outlaw_Tech_Talent_Tree "Outlaw Tech Talent Tree")
- New [Tech Specialist](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tech_Specialist "Tech Specialist") Feat
- New [Zabrak Engineer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Zabrak_Engineer "Zabrak Engineer") (CL 7) and a [Mon Calamari Outlaw Tech](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mon_Calamari_Outlaw_Tech "Mon Calamari Outlaw Tech") (CL 7)

## The Outlaw Tech Talent Tree

The [Outlaw Tech Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Outlaw_Tech_Talent_Tree "Outlaw Tech Talent Tree") fills some niches left by the existing [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") rules and options for repairing broken pieces of technology. Here's a behind-the-screen look at the design decisions that went into creating these [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents").

- **[Fast Repairs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fast_Repairs "Fast Repairs")** is designed to make [Jury-Rigging](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jury-Rigging "Jury-Rigging") more appealing to tech-oriented characters. As it stands now, [Jury-Rigging](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jury-Rigging "Jury-Rigging") an object or [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") moves it up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") but does not provide temporary [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"). If your ship is at 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") and you jury-rig it, it's still [Disabled](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Disabled "Disabled") from [Hit Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Point "Hit Point") loss. This new [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") alleviates that problem, while still making [Jury-Rigging](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jury-Rigging "Jury-Rigging") useful for ships pushed down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") by other means (such as [Ion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion "Ion") damage).
- **[Quick Fix](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Quick_Fix "Quick Fix")** builds on **[Fast Repairs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fast_Repairs "Fast Repairs")** and gives a techie character a way to restore some [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") to a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") that's still in the fight, but brings with it the threat of becoming [Disabled](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Disabled "Disabled") at the end of the encounter. This new [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") also gives ship [Engineers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Engineers "Engineers") something else to do in combat, especially in desperate situations, and it can bring a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") back up to full fighting form very quickly.
- **[Hot Wire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hot_Wire "Hot Wire")** is similar to the other [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill")-substitution [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and makes sense for a techie who may not be the best slicer around.
- **[Personalized Modifications](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Personalized_Modifications "Personalized Modifications")** is designed to give a [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") another means of activating some of their other [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"). For example, many of the Scoundrel's [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") from the [Misfortune Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Misfortune_Talent_Tree "Misfortune Talent Tree") rely on a successful attack roll to activate. Combined with the [Tech Specialist](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tech_Specialist "Tech Specialist") Feat, this new [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") gets the [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") a small boost to attacks that can compensate for the difference between low [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") progression and [Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense "Defense") progression, even at high levels. The [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") might still need to work closely with their friends against tougher foes, but this [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") is designed to capture the feel of tweaking your own weapon while still synergizing with many of the [Misfortune Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Misfortune_Talents "Misfortune Talents").

*By Rodney Thompson*

Designing fresh, exciting, and (most importantly) fair encounters for *Star Wars* Roleplaying Game heroes can be one of the greatest challenges for a Gamemaster. In many ways, encounter design and balance are more art than science. Despite the organized and numeric nature of the rules, sometimes factors outside of the raw statistics can alter the difficulty or fun of the encounter. For example, a Gamemaster might design an encounter that would be perfectly appropriate for most parties, but since their players have just the right mixture of [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), and tactics, the encounter is over too quickly- either in the heroes' favor, or to their dismay.

The *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* presents some very basic guidelines on encounter design (see [Building an Encounter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Building_an_Encounter "Building an Encounter")). However, to encourage Gamemasters to create more dynamic and exciting encounters, this article walks through the steps for building an encounter from start to finish. The guidelines in this Web Enhancement should help you create interesting and fair encounters, though of course each GM must consider the composition of the adventuring party.

## Step 1: Determine Opponents

The most significant and most difficult part of designing an encounter is choosing the enemies that the heroes will face. Every character, creature, [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid"), and [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") includes a Challenge Level (CL) that indicates how tough that challenge can be. In many cases, CL is not assigned through any specific formula; determining CL can often be a matter of testing the challenge to gauge its difficulty. However, CL gives a good starting point for designing an encounter. An opponent of a given CL is considered to be a challenge for four heroic characters of that level; thus, a CL 1 [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") is a challenge for four 1st-level characters.

As stated in the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")*, encounters with four to six enemies work best for most encounters. This gives the heroes enough targets to feel like each member of the group is participating while not overwhelming them. To remain an appropriate challenge, the main opponents in the fight should have a CL that is 1 or 2 points lower than the average party level. For most encounters, the enemies should be weaker than the heroes but should still be enough of a threat that the heroes will feel challenged by the encounter. *Star Wars* differs from many other roleplaying games, though, in that it's often perfectly acceptable to litter the battlefield with enemies who are far outclassed by the heroes. This will be covered in more detail in Step 3, but when determining encounter difficulty, you should focus only on those enemies whose CL is within 5 levels of the average party level.

To determine whether or not the CLs you have chosen for your encounter are appropriate, add all the CLs together and divide by 3. If this result is within one level of the average party level, and no single enemy is of a higher CL than the average party level minus 1, you've probably created a good challenge. Remember, though, that for single opponents, an appropriate encounter is with an enemy of CL equal to the average party level.

### Mixing Challenge Levels

Not every enemy in an encounter has to be a difficult challenge for the heroes. It is acceptable, and even encouraged in many cases, to have a portion of the opposition be weaker than other opponents. These weaker enemies might act as "support" for the primary opponents, using the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to enhance attack rolls, or affecting the battlefield in some indirect way. Alternatively, they might just be there to slow the heroes down long enough for the main enemies to make their move. Regardless of their function, these lower-CL enemies can still hurt the heroes, but they are secondary concerns after the primary enemies.

Additionally, mixing Challenge Levels is a great way to keep the heroes guessing. If one enemy has a high attack bonus and high damage, the heroes might assume that they all do, encouraging the group to be more cautious (even though that caution may be unwarranted). Similarly, heroes that drop an enemy of a lower CL quickly might become reckless, making them enticing targets for more threatening foes. A good mix of Challenge Levels can add diversity to a fight in a subtle but effective way.

### Choosing Diverse Enemies

The process of picking appropriately interesting enemies is just as important as picking appropriately challenging enemies. When designing an encounter, pull enemies from different [Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Classes "Classes"), different [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species"), and even different types. Mixing characters, creatures, [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids"), and [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") is a great way to create more interesting encounters precisely because they require different tactics. [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") and [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") are immune to [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") damage, creatures can't be negotiated with, [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") move at high rates of speed, and characters have a diverse array of [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"). Though each layer of enemy diversity adds more work for the Gamemaster, they also make for more exciting and tactically engaging encounters.

For example, the classic "squad of [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers")" encounter is a favorite in *Star Wars* adventures. However, so much more can be done with the encounter to make it more exciting. Instead of six [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers"), we might use four [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers"), an [Imperial Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial_Officer "Imperial Officer"), and two [Viper-Series Probe Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Viper-Series_Probe_Droids "Viper-Series Probe Droids"). Instead of five [Human](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Human "Human") [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") starting a cantina fight, the encounter might be three [Human](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Human "Human") thugs, the [Twi'lek](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Twi%27lek "Twi'lek") bartender, and the cantina's "pet," a [Nek Dog](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nek_Dog "Nek Dog"). Even within members of the same [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species"), [Class](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Class "Class") diversity can make a big difference. A single [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") with a few [Soldiers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldiers "Soldiers") makes a potent combination, and one that produces an interesting combat dynamic during the encounter.

Mixing in [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") can be a bit more challenging, due largely to their increased firepower and toughness. For this reason, most [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") are higher on the CL scale. However, [Speeder Bikes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speeder_Bikes "Speeder Bikes") and [Landspeeders](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Landspeeders "Landspeeders") (many of which have weapons comparable to those carried by characters) can liven up the battlefield without tilting the balance of the encounter too much. A group of [Dark Side Adepts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Adepts_(CL_6) "Dark Side Adepts (CL 6)") might be made into a more interesting encounter if it were two [Dark Side Marauders](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Marauders "Dark Side Marauders"), a [Dark Side Adept](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Adept_(CL_6) "Dark Side Adept (CL 6)"), and three of their [Human](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Human "Human") thralls riding around the battlefield on [Speeder Bikes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speeder_Bikes "Speeder Bikes").

Regardless of the exact combinations, diversity is usually more engaging than uniformity when it comes to primary enemies. A good rule of thumb when diversifying opponents is to choose one type of opponent as the base enemy, choose a second type of opponent that is separated from the base enemy by a minor difference (such as [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species") or [Class](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Class "Class")), and then choose a third type of opponent that is separated from the base enemy by a major difference (such as by type: [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid"), creature, or [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle")).

### Altering Challenge Through Gear

One aspect of picking your enemies that can often be overlooked is the effect of [Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapons "Weapons") and [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") on an individual opponent. A CL 1 [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") typically carries a [Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Rifle "Blaster Rifle") and other basic accoutrements. However, if you put an [E-Web Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/E-Web_Repeating_Blaster "E-Web Repeating Blaster") in the hands of that [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper"), they suddenly become a much more potent enemy, even though technically their Challenge Level remains unchanged. Since [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") and wealth are not balancing factors in determining Challenge Level for most enemies, Gamemasters should be careful when handing out powerful [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment"). It can quickly increase the challenge out of proportion with the Challenge Level.

By the same token, adding [Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapons "Weapons") or [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") to an opponent is an excellent way to add diversity to an encounter. If every [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") in the encounter has a [Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Rifle "Blaster Rifle"), they all act the same way and use the same tactics. If, however, one [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") is firing an [E-Web Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/E-Web_Repeating_Blaster "E-Web Repeating Blaster"), this changes that opponent's behavior and encourages the heroes to change theirs as well. Likewise, if the Gamemaster puts a [Grenade Launcher](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grenade_Launcher "Grenade Launcher") or a [Flamethrower](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Flamethrower "Flamethrower") in the hands of one of the [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers"), the fight becomes very different indeed. A standard [Bounty Hunter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bounty_Hunter_(CL_7) "Bounty Hunter (CL 7)") might be tough, but when outfitted with the kinds of weapons and armor like [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett"), the enemy becomes much more challenging.

In general, [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") should not increase the CL of an individual opponent. However, if the [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") significantly raises the enemy's attack bonus, damage, or [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), it might be appropriate to increase the CL as well. As a guideline, if a piece of [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") increases any of these aspects of the character by 50%, increase the opponent's Challenge Level by +1. For example, a normal [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") deals 3d8 points of damage with a [Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Rifle "Blaster Rifle") (an average of 13.5 points). If that [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") begins using an [E-Web Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/E-Web_Repeating_Blaster "E-Web Repeating Blaster") that deals 3d12 points of damage (an average of 19.5 points), that change normally wouldn't justify increasing the CL. However, the weapon creates an [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack") (which not only strikes multiple opponents but also deals half damage even on a miss), and that probably is enough to warrant a higher CL.

## Step 2: Determine Locale

Though the general locale of the encounter will likely be determined by the demands of the adventure, choosing an appropriate setting can go a long way toward excitement. Very few encounters in *Star Wars* take place in an empty room or on a wide-open plain with no visible terrain or obstacles. Instead, they take place in cramped hallways in Imperial detention centers, around massive open-air catwalks and endless chasms, and on assembly lines deep within [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") construction facilities.

Usually, interesting encounter locales are irregular in shape, with alcoves, short hallways, and protruding walls. A locale can encompass several rooms, and a single encounter might be designed to flow from one room to another. Perhaps an [Explosive Charge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Explosive_Charge "Explosive Charge") collapses a wall, allowing reinforcements to arrive from the far side and increasing the challenge. Indoor locales usually have stairs, catwalks, turbolifts, or other means of moving between altitudes, as well as pits and chasms that must be avoided. Similarly, a good encounter locale gives the Gamemaster enough to describe without getting bogged down in details. By itself, a cargo bay isn't very exciting or memorable, but a cargo bay with a massive glass window overlooking a volcano planet, with electricity coursing through power couplings along one wall, is certainly more evocative.

For outdoor locales, terrain plays an even more important role. [Difficult Terrain](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Difficult_Terrain "Difficult Terrain") requires tactical movement and can slow down heroes and enemies alike. Alien flora and fauna, a staple of the *Star Wars* universe, can add spice to any encounter. If your fight takes place on an ice planet, you can stage it on an open, snowy plain, or you can stage it on the edge of a cliff, above the canyon lair of a flesh-eating snow lizard. Likewise, if a battle takes place on a forest planet at night, you can evoke the alien nature of the world by having giant fireflies provide the only illumination. Even better, as the huge insects drift across the battlefield, they bring an interesting dynamic- constantly shifting [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment")- to the fight itself.

### Environmental Hazards

Locales bring with them certain [Hazards](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hazards "Hazards") native to the environment. Most Environmental Hazards can be classified as Conditional, Partial, or Total.

#### Conditional Hazards

A Conditional Hazard is one that presents itself only if certain conditions are met. These conditions might require the heroes or their enemies to trigger the Hazard. For example, anyone who passes through the security sensors triggers an alarm that slams a blast door closed. Alternatively, the [Hazard](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hazard "Hazard") might be triggered by some other environmental effect. Perhaps when the blast door slams shut, it disrupts power to half the room, plunging it into darkness. Similarly, the condition that triggers the Hazard might be a function of time. After 5 rounds, the ice at the edge of the cliff melts, sending anyone in a 2-square-wide line plummeting into the chasm below.

Additionally, Conditional Hazards may not be readily apparent to the heroes, and can come as a surprise in the middle of an encounter.

#### Partial Hazards

Partial Hazards are usually visible to the heroes and are present throughout the duration of the encounter. These [Hazards](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hazards "Hazards") can be avoided and may not affect every combatant. For example, the [Sarlacc Pit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sarlacc_Pit "Sarlacc Pit") in the Great Pit of Carkoon acted as a Partial Hazard because while it was present throughout the fight, the heroes could avoid it by not falling off their skiff. Other examples of Partial Hazards include a room that's half underwater, a large swath of ground that's covered in jagged rocks or metal, or a stretch of floor plating that's charged with electricity.

#### Total Hazards

A Total Hazard is one that is present throughout the encounter and unavoidable, such as a room filled with [Dioxis](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dioxis "Dioxis") or a completely flooded chamber. Total Hazards usually impede (or damage) the heroes every round and can seriously affect the encounter. Gamemasters should be careful with Total Hazards because they can quickly lead to the death of one or more characters- or perhaps the entire party.

#### Using Environmental Hazards

When introducing an Environmental Hazard, adjust the encounter to accommodate the increased challenge. Assuming that the Hazard has attack bonuses and damage outputs on par with enemies of the appropriate CL, adding a Hazard to an encounter is just like adding another opponent. If the Hazard is Conditional, consider it to be another "opponent" with a CL equal to the equivalent enemy CL minus 2. If the Hazard is Partial, consider it to be another "opponent" with a CL equal to the equivalent enemy CL minus 1. If the Hazard is Total, consider it to be another "opponent" with a CL equal to the equivalent enemy CL.

For example, let's say the [Hazard](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hazard "Hazard") is a poison gas cloud. The gas attacks a target's [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") with an attack bonus of +6 (the equivalent of about a CL 5 enemy) and deals 2d10 points of damage each round the gas is inhaled. If the gas is present only when a hero enters an incorrect command code, the Hazard is Conditional; treat it as a CL 3 enemy. If the gas is always in the room, but the cloud covers only the computer stations, the Hazard is Partial; treat it as a CL 4 enemy. If the entire room is filled with gas, the Hazard is Total and would be considered a CL 5 enemy.

In any case, when you're determining the fairness of an encounter, be sure to include an Environmental Hazard's CL in the calculations.

#### Step 3: Determine Obstacles and Other Challenges

Lastly, anything else that can act as an impediment to the heroes should be added to the encounter. Though many obstacles are Environmental Hazards, you can complicate an encounter in other interesting ways. For example, a Construction Droid that swings a hanging girder through the battlefield every other round certainly alters the fight. The [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is not an Environmental Hazard because it can be disabled, but it's not really an opponent, either, so you need not consider its CL when determining the fairness of the encounter.

### Reverse Engineering

One good way to determine if obstacles are needed for an encounter is to use the Reverse Engineering method. First, picture the room where the fight will take place, empty it of obstacles, and imagine the foes inside. Now imagine that the fight begins; what do the heroes do? What are their actions, assuming they win [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative") and act first? Based on those assumptions, find ways to make those actions more difficult. Repeat the process until you're satisfied that the encounter provides enough of a challenge to the heroes.

For example, let's say an encounter pits the heroes against four [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers"), an [Imperial Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial_Officer "Imperial Officer"), and two [Viper-Series Probe Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Viper-Series_Probe_Droids "Viper-Series Probe Droids"). Place the enemies in an empty room and begin the Reverse Engineering process. The [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") hero will likely move into the room and use *[Force Slam](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Slam "Force Slam")* on the squad of [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers"), so you decide to put two of them on a platform that is 3 meters tall, meaning that the *[Force Slam](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Slam "Force Slam")* can only get 2 of them at best. The [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") will probably try to sneak up on the [Imperial Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial_Officer "Imperial Officer"), so give that foe a hovering spotlight [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") that tries to point out any heroes breaking off from the main group. The [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") carries [Ion Grenades](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion_Grenades "Ion Grenades") and [Ion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion "Ion") weapons, so they'll probably try to take out the two [Viper-Series Probe Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Viper-Series_Probe_Droids "Viper-Series Probe Droids"). To complicate matters, spread the two [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") far apart from each other, ensuring that a single [Ion Grenade](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion_Grenade "Ion Grenade") won't take them both out. With just a few small changes, a relatively mundane encounter has become more engaging and challenging for the heroes.

When using Reverse Engineering, it's important to remember that your goal is to challenge your heroes, not to stymie them. *Star Wars* is all about doing amazing, heroic things in the heat of battle, and your players will want to do this as well. Obstacles should encourage them to make choices with their abilities, not keep them from using the abilities completely. Does the [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") go after the [Imperial Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial_Officer "Imperial Officer") or the spotlight [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") first? Which pair of [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") does the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") use *[Force Slam](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Slam "Force Slam")* on? Which [Viper-Series Probe Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Viper-Series_Probe_Droid "Viper-Series Probe Droid") feels the heat of the Soldier's [Ion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion "Ion") barrage? The heroes still use their abilities in fun and exciting ways, but they have choices to make when doing so.

### Low-Challenge Enemies as Hazards

Low-level enemies don't usually provide enough challenge for heroes. However, even at high levels, most players still want to kill [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") and blow up [B1-Series Battle Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/B1-Series_Battle_Droids "B1-Series Battle Droids"). That's part of what makes a *Star Wars* game memorable. Instead of continually increasing the levels of [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers"), try using a handful of [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") as an obstacle. An encounter with a single, high-level enemy might need to be enhanced to present a more cinematic scene, and adding a bunch of low-level goons to the fight does just that. The heroes will usually take time to stop and dispatch low-level enemies, even if they see them as little threat, and there's always a chance that a Natural 20 will allow a [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") to get off a lucky shot at a hero.

For example, a party of 10th-level heroes comes up against the [Sith Apprentice](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Apprentice_(CL_13) "Sith Apprentice (CL 13)") they have been pursuing for several adventures. The Gamemaster wants the [Sith Apprentice](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Apprentice_(CL_13) "Sith Apprentice (CL 13)") to be the focus of the encounter, but a four-on-one fight, even against a Sith, might be anticlimactic. To enhance the encounter, the Gamemaster adds a dozen low-level [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers"). They pose little direct threat to the heroes, but they're an obstacle that must be overcome on the way to defeating the real enemy. In addition, they give non-[Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") party members something to do while the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") engages the [Sith Apprentice](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Apprentice_(CL_13) "Sith Apprentice (CL 13)") in a climactic duel. A similar situation occurred in the Battle of [Geonosis](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Geonosis "Geonosis"), when [Mace Windu](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mace_Windu "Mace Windu") ran straight for [Jango Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jango_Fett "Jango Fett") while others focused on taking out the [B2-Series Super Battle Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/B2-Series_Super_Battle_Droids "B2-Series Super Battle Droids").

### Providing Cover Spots

One important aspect of encounter design is providing enough spots for heroes and enemies to take [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") during an encounter. Not only is [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") important to many characters in the game, it also provides more *Star Wars*-like combat experiences. Objects that provide [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover")- such as crates, computers, and low walls- also act as obstacles, encouraging creative movement throughout the terrain.

### Spreading Out the Enemies

One of the fastest ways to get a group of opponents killed is to have them all bunched together. A quick [Grenade](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grenade "Grenade") or a *[Force Slam](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Slam "Force Slam")*, and they'll be wounded, dead, or dying. Instead, try to spread at least some of the enemies out to avoid [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks"). It's fine (and encouraged) to have a group of Battle Droids march in formation so they can be taken out quickly, but if you want certain enemies to present a significant challenge, place them throughout the encounter area so that individual [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") will be less effective- at least in the first round or two.

## Step 4: Putting It All Together

Having walked through the process of designing an encounter, it's time to make up an encounter of our own. Let's assume that we have a party of 7th-level heroes playing in [The Rebellion Era](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Rebellion_Era "The Rebellion Era"), and we want to challenge the heroes while still making sure that they can survive the encounter. Since the heroes will be infiltrating an Imperial Detention Facility as a part of the adventure, we already know our setting. For our primary enemies, we pick two CL 5 [ISB Agents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/ISB_Agents "ISB Agents") ([Nonheroic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonheroic "Nonheroic") 6/[Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") 3) and one CL 5 [Imperial Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial_Officer "Imperial Officer") ([Nonheroic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonheroic "Nonheroic") 3/[Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") 3/[Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Officer "Officer") 1) to represent the interrogation team. Additionally, the facility has a small complement of [Crab Droid Scouts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crab_Droid_Scouts "Crab Droid Scouts") (CL 3, [Nonheroic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonheroic "Nonheroic") 9) leftover from [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars"), one of which is guarding the area the heroes are attempting to infiltrate. Adding all the CLs together and dividing by 3, we get an encounter CL of 6, meaning we have the beginnings of an appropriate encounter.

However, we really want to challenge the party, so we spice up the battlefield with terrain effects and other [Hazards](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hazards "Hazards"). The encounter will take place in a detention block, since the heroes are there to rescue an NPC that has helped them in the past. We decide that the detention block is equipped with emergency systems that spray a numbing gas into the room. We give the numbing gas an attack bonus of +5 against a character's [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") (the equivalent of a CL 4 enemy), dealing 3d8 points of [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") damage. But we also determine that the gas shoots out of the walls only around the edges of the room, making it a Partial Hazard and reducing the CL to 3. Added to the other CLs in the encounter, this gives us a new encounter CL of 7, which should be more challenging for the heroes.

Knowing that our heroes are fond of [Stunning](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stunning "Stunning") enemies, the party is likely to charge in and try to get close to the [Imperial Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial_Officer "Imperial Officer") and the [ISB Agents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/ISB_Agents "ISB Agents") to use the [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") settings on their blasters. To keep this from happening too quickly, we decide that barricades pop up to block the middle of the room, spaced at intervals of 3 squares. Thus, if the heroes want to get in close with the [ISB Agents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/ISB_Agents "ISB Agents"), they'll have to move down the sides of the room, where the numbing gas is spraying. The [Crab Droid Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crab_Droid_Scout "Crab Droid Scout"), unaffected by the gas or [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") weapons, will range out toward the heroes, while the [Imperial Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial_Officer "Imperial Officer") and [ISB Agents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/ISB_Agents "ISB Agents") make attacks against the party from the back of the room.

In a matter of minutes, we've created an encounter that will challenge the heroes and produce interesting combat situations. All that's left to do is determine the DC of the [Use Computer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") check or [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") check needed to shut down the gas (giving tech-oriented heroes another way to contribute), and the encounter is ready to run.

*By Rodney Thompson*

During the development of the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition [Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")*, some material had to be cut for space. One such section was the [Devaronian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Devaronian "Devaronian") Species.

- New [Devaronian Drifter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Devaronian_Drifter "Devaronian Drifter")

| SPECIES | ABILITY MODIFIERS | IMAGE |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Devaronian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Devaronian "Devaronian") | +2 Dex, -2 Wis, -2 Cha (Males); +2 Int, +2 Wis, -2 Dex (Females) |  |

| PLANET | LOCATION | CLIMATE | NATIVE SPECIES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Devaron](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Devaron "Devaron") | [The Colonies](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Colonies?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Colonies (page does not exist)") | Temperate | [Devaronians](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Devaronians "Devaronians") |

*By Rodney Thompson*

The [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are a tall, gray-skinned species of humanoids with sunken eyes and wrinkled skin. Gaunt and with sharp teeth that clearly indicate carnivorous tendencies, [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") move with a deliberate pace and have soft, gravelly voices that are calming to hear. [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are taller than most [Humans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Humans "Humans") (though not as tall as [Wookiees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiees "Wookiees")) and have long, slender fingers that end in nails that closely resemble talons.

Xenobiologists from the University of Sanbra have uncovered connections between the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") and the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai"), the smaller Species with whom they share the planet of [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau"). Genetic sampling has confirmed a few tenuous biological links, though the exact evolutionary process by which the [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") Species developed separately from the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") is still a mystery. Xenoanthropologists date the first [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") civilizations back almost 57,000 years before the Battle of [Yavin](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yavin "Yavin"), making theirs one of the youngest known civilizations. Though no written history of this time exists, scientists currently theorize that the long-lived [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are genetically closer to their progenitor Species, and that as a result of their longevity the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") have likely only evolved a very little since the planet was settled.

Some time in the last ten thousand years, [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") underwent a climate change that caused winds on the surface of the world to reach and maintain constant bursts at destructive speeds. Experts from the University of Sanbra speculate that the climate change was likely very sudden (at least, sudden in geological terms) given the fact that the [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") Species did not evolve or adapt to the high winds as many other [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species") did. The long gap between [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") generations caused by their extremely long lives was probably responsible for this evolutionary stumbling block, and were it not for the migration of [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") civilization underground they might not have survived the climate change.

The shift to life underground did not change the Species' eating habits, as they maintained many of the biological signs of a carnivorous diet. The ecology of [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau"), particularly with regard to an abundance of animal life in sinkholes and below ground, allowed the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") to retain their carnivorous ways, which persist into modern times.

## Temperament

Though many beings (particularly [Humans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Humans "Humans")) are frightened by the appearance of the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans"), in truth they possess a temperament far kinder than that of many other [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species"), particularly in regard to outlanders. Living in a symbiotic relationship with the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai"), the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") have learned over the years how to interact with members of other [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species"), and as a result they are friendly and welcoming to outsiders. Having been thrust into a position of authority, they know that such authority is best wielded with a kind and sympathetic hand. For the most part, [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are amicable and open to outsiders, and they mesh well with almost any group.

Nearly every [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") has been in some kind of leadership position at least once in his or her life. From an early age, [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are expected to take on a leadership role. For example, many [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") start out as managers of [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") laborers, even if they are just directing a small team of [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") in a menial task. This is done to teach young [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") how to lead and earn respect and trust. This regimen has worked for thousands of years, and never once has there been any significant conflict between the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") and the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai").

As one might expect, the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are exceptionally good at taking on leadership roles anywhere in the galaxy. The few [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") that leave [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") fall naturally into managerial roles, whether at major corporations or in positions of government. In [The Corporate Sector](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Corporate_Sector?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Corporate Sector (page does not exist)"), [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are highly sought after for their natural inclination toward wise and even-handed leadership. Many corporations have discovered that divisions run by [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") administrators have higher than average productivity and employee retention rates, even in sectors where the quality of life is low. At one point, so many corporations were sending delegations to [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") to recruit the natives that the governments of the world had to request that [The Corporate Sector Authority](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Corporate_Sector_Authority "The Corporate Sector Authority") intervene on their behalf. Since that time, [The Corporate Sector Authority](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Corporate_Sector_Authority "The Corporate Sector Authority") has sent a delegation to [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") once a year to recruit administrators from the planet's populace, though some corporations still send their own representatives in clandestine recruiting attempts.

## History and Culture

Almost no records exist of the time before the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") moved underground and into sinkholes. Only verbal histories tell of an age when clusters of [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") civilization dotted the planet's surface. Before that, however, the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") were actually members of another [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species") entirely. Tales of the settling of [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") are vague and can be only tangentially corroborated by records from other civilizations of the time period. Still, it is clear that the Pau'ans' progenitors came to [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") to make it a permanent settlement, possibly following the destruction of their original homeworld. At least some of these settlers sought shelter in the planet's natural sinkholes, and xenoanthropologists believe that this is when the genetic split occurred. The settlers who went underground became the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai"), and those who remained on the surface became the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans"). Additionally, shortly after the world was settled, the first rumors began to spread that [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") was the original home planet of [The Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi "The Jedi").

For millennia the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") lived in small villages and cities on the surface of [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau"), believing themselves to be the dominant [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species"). When climate changes created powerful and destructive wind storms that blew unabated across the surface, the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") were forced to find safer environments. They descended into the planet's natural sinkholes and reestablished their civilization underground. During this period of transition, the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") encountered the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") once again, who were more than willing to assist the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") in their relocation efforts. Very quickly, the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") and the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") formed a symbiotic relationship, and the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") drifted naturally into a position of authority. The [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") seemed to accept this change easily, and the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") became the civilization's administrators and leaders.

The relocation of [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") civilization underground led to the creation of several large city-states that were, on the whole, self sustaining and self governing. However, the relationship between the city-states was a bit rockier than that between the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") and the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai"). For the most part, the city-states tended to avoid contact with one another because philosophical differences were likely to lead them into conflict. Such conflicts were usually the result of cultural differences more than malice or wrongdoing, and few of the clashes came to violence- and even those that did were little more than small fights or skirmishes. Still, the differences among the city-states were enough to keep them in relative isolation except for their representatives to the planetary committee (see "Politics," below). The only time the city-states worked together was to fend off outside threats, and when they united in this manner, they showed remarkable solidarity for a civilization with so much internal isolationism. However, once the external threat was dealt with, each city-state went back to its old habits of ignoring the others.

Much of that changed as a result of [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars") and the eventual rise of the Empire. When the battles of [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars") came to [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau"), the planet's citizens found themselves unable to resist the might of the Confederacy, and for a time the world was under Separatist control. When the Republic liberated the planet, the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") rose up in celebration for their freedom. Sadly, these celebrations were short lived. Soon the Empire replaced the Republic, and [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") remained under Imperial control. The planet's resources were harvested without any thought of the repercussions to the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") and the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai"), and the forceful hand of the Empire supplanted the peaceful leadership of the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans"). During this time, the city-states began to work together more closely, and the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") and the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") formed underground resistance groups. Just as the planet was never more unified than in the face of external danger, so was the planet never more united than under the threat of the Empire.

## Politics

The [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") act as administrators and bureaucrats of the individual city-states, and as a result they are heavily involved in the politics of government. Each city-state has its own government and administration, and each is overseen by a single leader (known as the Master of Port Administration) who has an advisory council to assist in making important decisions. Different city-states have different specific laws, though by and large they stick to a set of general guidelines that keeps the laws from being too diverse from place to place.

Governing the world as a whole is the Utapauan Committee, which is little more than a group of representatives from the city-states that meet to discuss matters of planetary importance. Since each city-state is capable of governing itself and providing for the health and welfare of its own citizens, there is no real need for a world government except in cases of planetary defense. Thus, an Utapauan Security Force (USF) keeps the planet safe from pirates and raiders, but with a few exceptions, the world has no standing government. Only the USF remains active at all times; the Utapauan Committee meets once every few weeks, or in times of dire need.

As a result of this decentralized governmental system, visitors are more likely to deal with representatives from one of the citystates than with members of the Utapauan Committee. When a ship approaches the planet, a METOSP (Message to Spacers) alerts travelers to contact spaceport control at their destination and provides them with the appropriate [Comlink](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Comlink "Comlink") frequencies. From that point onward, visitors deal exclusively with the city-state's government, and the USF gets involved only when the visitors pose a threat to the entire planet. In fact, a visitor might go his or her entire stay without dealing with a member of the planetary government, which helps enforce the notion that [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") is a sparsely populated world that holds little of interest.

## Technology Level

The combination of the Pau'ans' ingenuity and the Utai's penchant for labor has produced technology on par with that found in the rest of the galaxy. When the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") moved underground, they brought the secrets of generating power by harnessing the wind, and even in modern times over 99% of the planet's energy generation comes from wind power. Massive fields of unobtrusive windmills harness the mighty storms and convert them to clean energy that meets the modest power needs of the world. Each of the city-states has a functional spaceport, but larger ships must send down shuttles- in fact, anything larger than a [Space Transport](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Space_Transport "Space Transport") will find it difficult to maneuver into the sinkholes.

Despite their status as a spacefaring [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species"), the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") retain an affinity for more traditional forms of transportation, particularly that of mounted [Beasts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Beasts "Beasts"). Many [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") still ride [Varactyls](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Varactyls "Varactyls") and [Dactillions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dactillions "Dactillions") in lieu of using [Speeders](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speeders "Speeders") or other [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles"), and much of the architecture in their cities is quite classical. However, the prosperity that resulted from their relationship with the [Utai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utai "Utai") has allowed them to progress technologically as well, and most city-states have all of the same amenities as cities on any other Outer Rim world.

## Trade and Technology

Although the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are welcoming to outsiders, they rarely venture out into the galaxy to let others know it. In fact, very few people have even heard of [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau"), and those who have consider the planet to be little more than a legend. As such, the arrival of traders is usually a cause for celebration on the world, and the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") are even more welcoming to merchants than they are to normal visitors. Though the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") have little need for basic supplies such as foodstuffs or raw materials, they pay well for luxury items and pieces of high technology not available on their planet. Moreover, many [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") pay well for authentic pieces of art from throughout the galaxy, particularly for sculptures (which they often study so they can integrate the style into their own architecture).

Before [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars"), [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") fell under a restrictive trade embargo put in place by [The Trade Federation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Trade_Federation "The Trade Federation"). During this period, the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") could not obtain much weaponry or defensive hardware and had to rely on ships of their own design or weapons of inferior quality. The [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") have never been aggressive in spreading out into the galaxy for fear that they might draw too much attention to their lightly defended world. In the days leading up to [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars"), several Rendili StarDrive Dreadnaughts served as a planetary defense fleet for [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau"), but those ships were destroyed when the Separatists captured the planet. Later, [The Galactic Empire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Galactic_Empire "The Galactic Empire") seized all [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") military assets and shut down their [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter") production facilities, not even bothering to convert their meager resources into [TIE Fighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/TIE_Fighter "TIE Fighter") factories.

About thirty years before the Rise of the Empire, [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") gained a bit of notoriety following the discovery of supposed healing powers in the waters of the planet. Though the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") were reluctant to draw much attention to the world, their natural friendliness led them to allow an offworld water mining company to harvest and sell water drawn from lakes deep underground. The miners marketed the water as coming from "[Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau"), lost home of [The Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi "The Jedi")!" and claimed that it had miraculous healing powers, attracting attention from around the galaxy. The lucrative sales dried up when people realized that the water had no special abilities whatsoever. However, some [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") later speculated that this series of events drew the planet to the attention of [General Grievous](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/General_Grievous "General Grievous") and the Separatists, setting the stage for their invasion during [The Clone Wars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Clone_Wars "The Clone Wars").

## Pau'ans in the Galaxy

Though few [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") venture off-world, it is far from uncommon. [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") can be found in almost any part of the galaxy (especially in [The Corporate Sector](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Corporate_Sector?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Corporate Sector (page does not exist)")) in a variety of roles, but unsurprisingly they gravitate toward leadership positions. Some governments hire [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") in advisory roles, and more than one Republic Senator has sought the assistance of a [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") aide when making major decisions. Among the nobility in [The Core Worlds](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Core_Worlds?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Core Worlds (page does not exist)"), having a [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") advisor is usually seen as a status symbol, though [Caamasi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Caamasi "Caamasi") advisors often bring just as much respect and notoriety.

In the days of the Republic, many nobles kept [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") advisors on hand as trusted allies, but once the Empire came to power, this relationship became twisted by evil. Imperial nobles treat [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") advisors as slaves or sometimes even as pets, requesting their counsel only to laugh it off as the foolish insights of an Outer Rim dweller.

Adventuring [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") drift toward the [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") Heroic Class in particular and the [Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Officer "Officer") Prestige Class by extension. Their ability to improve [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") such as **[Inspire Confidence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Inspire_Confidence "Inspire Confidence")** and to gain [Skill Focus (Persuasion)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Persuasion) "Skill Focus (Persuasion)") allow [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") to take advantage of a variety of [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") abilities without needing to invest quite as many resources. [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans") also make excellent [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi"), particularly those that follow the path of the [Jedi Consular](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Consular_Talent_Tree "Jedi Consular Talent Tree"). If the rumors of [Utapau](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Utapau "Utapau") as the ancestral home of [The Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi "The Jedi") are true, perhaps the bloodlines of [The Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi "The Jedi") still run strong in the [Pau'ans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27ans "Pau'ans"). Their bonuses to [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom") and [Charisma](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charisma "Charisma") help them to [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") and gain [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers"), and [Pau'an](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pau%27an "Pau'an") Jedi who are [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in the [Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persuasion "Persuasion") Skill (and gain the bonus [Skill Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus "Skill Focus") Feat) have a significant advantage when using some [Jedi Consular](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Consular_Talent_Tree "Jedi Consular Talent Tree") Talents.

*By Eric Cragle*

The [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") are a tall Species, similar in height to [Wookiees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiees "Wookiees"). Their homeworld of [Nizon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nizon "Nizon") is a harsh dustbowl of a planet, with driving winds and fierce sandstorms that can scour the skin from an unprotected creature's bones. Until relatively recently, [Nizon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nizon "Nizon") had little to offer the galaxy. That changed when it was discovered that [Nizon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nizon "Nizon") possessed a few rich veins of minerals and ore. When the Empire arrived on the planet, however, the most useful resource proved to be the Nazren people themselves. They were quickly rounded up for slave labor and sent to the nearby planet of [Centares](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Centares?action=edit&redlink=1 "Centares (page does not exist)") to work in the mines.

Xenobiologists from the University of Sanbra have conducted studies of the Species and their planet at two points in their history. The first occurred about a hundred years prior to the establishment of the Empire, when a team of scientists from the University was sent along with some early mining expeditions. They began to investigate and catalog the [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") and found them to be a shy, reclusive Species that shunned warfare and were content to live alongside other species that made [Nizon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nizon "Nizon") their home. The Species is truly ancient and has changed little over the course of perhaps a million years or so. Once the Empire was successfully routed from the planet with the help of insurgents working for [The Alderaanian Resistance](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Alderaanian_Resistance?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Alderaanian Resistance (page does not exist)"), another team of xenobiologists was sent back to [Nizon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nizon "Nizon") to assess the damage done to the population.

The wholesale enslavement of the population had two interesting effects. First, the population was drastically reduced, with entire clans having been whisked away on Imperial slave ships. The leadership of the [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren"), mainly the elderly, was similarly turned on its head as most of the elder statesmen were killed during the resistance or died in internment camps on or off the planet. Xenobiologists have deduced that only a few more years of such treatment would have put an end to the [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren").

However, there appears to be a silver lining to this catastrophe. As different tribes were enslaved and moved around the planet (and off the planet entirely), genetic diversity received a big boost when disparate tribes came into contact with each other. Also, the troubles stirred the spirit of the [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") people, which had grown conservative and rather stagnant after untold millennia. After their successful resistance against the Empire, the [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") were no longer content with the status quo, and young [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") embarked on journeys outside their home system or began new projects to restore their planet's infrastructure. Social conventions were turned on their head, meaning that all [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") had the chance to prove and better themselves.

## Temperament

The immense stature of the [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") is enough to put many beings off guard. They move in a languid grace as their long arms sway to compensate for their relatively short legs. [Nizon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nizon "Nizon") is a harsh planet, with frequent sandstorms and a constant howling wind that wears down objects in short order. The [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") have survived by coming to terms with their homeworld's unforgiving nature, which has instilled patience in the race as well as an acceptance that nothing truly lasts. Some may perceive this as nihilism, but this is not correct. The [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") simply do not concern themselves as much with the material world.

[Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") hold their family ties in high regard and allow every adult an equal voice in making decisions that affect the rest of the tribe. On the positive side, this means that once a decision is made, the rest of the tribe goes along with it without question. On the downside, this makes the process exceedingly slow, as [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") debate the merits and flaws of any given challenge. They also lack initiative, and if someone doesn't take up the banner for some cause or concern, then it probably languishes in debate.

The [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") are not a passionate people, but they relish their freedom. When the Empire began enslaving them, this dichotomy became readily apparent, as relatively few of the [Nazren](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nazren "Nazren") put up resistance or were quickly cowed when they witnessed the brutality of the Empire firsthand.
