Ubiquitous in *Star Wars* is the Trickster- the comic relief. The Trickster's antics bring about change, as well as providing the much needed relief from the otherwise near-constant dramatic tension. Their comedy makes the serious parts of the story more meaningful. R2-D2 and C-3PO are perhaps the most famous examples of Tricksters, spanning two eras of *Star Wars*. Even in the most tense of situations, C-3PO's finicky nature comes through, most often in humorous complaints- or at least humorous to us. R2-D2's comedy is of a more physical nature. Whether it be being swallowed by a swamp beast on [Dagobah](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dagobah "Dagobah") and spit back out, spraying Battle Droids with oil and lighting them on fire as they slip and slide about, or simply revving up his partner in crime, R2-D2 is almost always amusing. Of course, both characters are heart-felt and invested in the drama that surrounds them.

Tricksters can be catalysts for needed change in others. Jar-Jar Binks, though a bumbling buffoon most of the time, had a profound influence in the fate of [Naboo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Naboo "Naboo"). It is he that warned his people of the Trade Federation's invasion, who guided the Jedi to the palace to rescue Princess Amidala, and who ultimately arranged the meeting that resulted in alliance between the [Humans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Humans "Humans") and the [Gungans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gungans "Gungans"). His antics put him in the right places at the right times to cause changes that were for the good of all. HK-47, though dark and bloodthirsty, sometimes played the role of the Trickster for Revan and their companions. The Droid's condescending and humorous attitude towards organic life was not only funny, it was a reminder of who Revan once was. He helped Revan stay the course of redemption, reinforcing that change.

## Using Archetypes

Archetypes are tools. They are designed to be functions that you can assign to characters in your story that will help expand the drama- and resonate with the monomyth. Each of them is useful and each of them is appropriate for nearly any kind of story. However, they are not something to which to be enslaved.

When you are designing your *Star Wars* game, you will be dealing with a situation in which you have many Heroes. Every character portrayed by a player is one. It would be difficult and redundant to have separate characters for each of the archetypes for each of the Heroes. As you are creating the key NPC's for your game, choose only one or two of the characters who will have Mentors. It should be obvious which are the most appropriate- Jedi or Sith are the easy targets.

The Shadow archetype is the easiest- it will be your villain. The thing to keep in mind is that Shadows have certain traits that you will want to incorporate into the design of your primary antagonists. There is something humanizing about them, so resist the urge to make them nothing but monsters. They also contain something in them which your Heroes (one or several) will not want to face, because it is an aspect of themselves with which they cannot deal.

Threshold Guardians are also fairly easy. They are all those who somehow oppose your Heroes along the way. They are those that are minor hurdles all the way up to the minions of the primary antagonist. These types of characters are naturally designed into roleplaying adventures. Heralds are likewise a built-in roleplaying concept, being the person who inspires the characters to undertake their adventure.

The Shapeshifter is one you may not even need to assign. As your story goes on, players will undoubtedly portray their characters as inconsistent and you will do the same, so the dramatic tension will increase naturally.

Some of the Heroes may take on one or two of the archetypes. There is often someone who desires to play a character with comic tendencies, so you might wish to have a conversation with him about taking on the role of the Trickster. Remember that the Trickster is more than just a clown- it is a character that lightens things at the appropriate moment, and also forces or inspires needed change. Furthermore, one or two of the characters might take on the role of the Herald, moving the group to form or at least to take action.

So, as you can see, it's mostly likely not going to be very difficult for you to incorporate archetypes into your *Star Wars* game. However, the magic really happens when you put archetypes into the structure of the monomyth.

At the heart of the monomyth is the structure of the story. Each Hero, regardless of who he is and the setting in which they live, follows a similar path. This structure has existed in mythology for generations and it speaks to us. It resonates with the human condition and what it takes to truly overcome and persevere. When we experience a story that utilizes the structure of the monomyth, it occurs to us as meaningful and epic. We identify with the Heroes and join them on what is most like the adventure of a lifetime.

The monomyth certainly resonates in *Star Wars*. How else could a 1970's medium-budget space opera have become such a household name (aside from the ground-breaking effects)? George Lucas studied the monomyth and used it to form his story. By adding the elements of the monomyth to his space opera, the story of *Star Wars* took on an eternal characters, speaking to the audience on a deep level. Even people who had no love for "sci-fi" were able to watch *Star Wars*, enjoy it, and be touched by it.

In this article, we will explore the stages of the monomyth as they apply to the first half of a story, encompassing the first act and first-half of the second act. The important thing to keep in mind is to not take the titles of the stages too literally- instead, study the spirit of what they represent. When you are creating your *Star Wars* roleplaying story, knowing how to broadly implement these stages will allow you to use the monomyth over and over again, with it never appearing the same way twice.

## Act I

The purpose of Act I is to set the stage for the real action of the story. During Act I, you should answer the where (setting), the who (main characters), and the what (plot) of your story, as well as have introduced the beginnings of any subplots. You should also have introduced the antagonists' main goal, or at least evidence of it. At the end of the Act, something moves the Hero into the real meat of the story.

### The Call to Adventure

Every story starts in the ordinary world of the Hero. They are living their life, doing the everyday sorts of things they do everyday. It is important to take a moment to experience the world of the Hero as it occurs to them in their routine. This makes the special world of the story, into which he will soon be venturing, that much more impactful.

However, something intrudes on the Hero's ordinary world. Whatever it is, it is something that they cannot ignore, something that intrudes into their life, and speaks to him. This is the Call to Adventure, a thing or event that draws the Hero onto the path of the story.

For example, Luke Skywalker is a simple farm boy, born and raised on [Tatooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tatooine "Tatooine"). His everyday life involves little excitement, aside from racing and shooting wildlife. However, one day his uncle Ben Lars picks up new Droids for the farm. As part of his ordinary world, Luke is sent to clean them and get them ready for use. The cleaning process sets off a holographic transmission inside the little Droid R2-D2- a princess calling for help, imploring a man with a name similar to a hermit Luke knows. Luke is compelled by the damsel in distress and now wants to find "Ben" Kenobi. This pushes him onto the path of the greater adventure that is *A New Hope*

Ben Kenobi was once a young Padawan known as Obi-Wan Kenobi. His ordinary world as a boy was a more exciting place than Luke's, traveling the galaxy as the apprentice of Jedi Knight Qui-Gon Jinn. One day, he is asked to go with his master to enter into negotiations with the Trade Federation, who has implemented a trade blockade on the planet of [Naboo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Naboo "Naboo"). This is all routine for young Obi-Wan. However, shortly after he and his master arrive, the Trade Federation attempts to assassinate them. This one moment starts a chain reaction in which Obi-Wan is called into the greater adventure that is *The Phantom Menace*.

Thousands of years before either Skywalker or Kenobi were born, the amnesiac Revan believes themselves to be a part of [The Old Republic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Old_Republic "The Old Republic"). Fighting against [The Sith Empire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Sith_Empire "The Sith Empire") is part of their everyday world. However, the Sith attack The *Endar Spire*, forcing Revan to flee the ship with Carth Onasi. This one event changes everything for Revan, who starts on the path of *Knights of the Old Republic* while marooned on the planet [Taris](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Taris "Taris").

In each of these cases, you can see that something enters the Hero's normal life which he cannot deny. In *Star Wars*, that thing is most often a matter of life and death.

### Refusal of the Call

Heroes don't just leap onto the path of adventure without some trepidation. After all, they are meant to be characters with which we can empathize, and any sane person would give pause when facing what the Heroes most definitely will face. In the monomyth, this moment is called the Refusal of the Call.

Oftentimes, the Hero simply feels that what is ahead is out of their league. They feel that they are not up to the challenge, that such things are better left to more qualified people. It could also be that they are afraid of the consequences, which are often death or worse. However, the refusal doesn't have to be an outright retreat from the call. It can sometimes simply be the Hero's acknowledgement that what is ahead is dangerous, as well as possibly scary or beyond them. It just has to be a moment where the magnitude of what the Hero is about to embark on weighs on them.

The thing about the Refusal of the Call is that when the Hero looks back on their ordinary world, what they have already experienced makes it feel banal. The excitement about what has already happened makes their old life seem somehow insubstantial- something that often drives them forward on the path of the story.

When Ben asks Luke to come with him to [Alderaan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Alderaan "Alderaan"), the boy balks. He uses the excuse that he has to stay and help his uncle, when really the world Ben is proposing is much too big for the simple farm boy.

Obi-Wan and Qui-Gon are not interested in getting into the politics of the blockade or the war the Trade Federation is threatening. They just want to survive [Naboo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Naboo "Naboo"), rescue the captive Queen Amidala, and get back to [Coruscant](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Coruscant "Coruscant"). The pair refuses to get drawn in any more than that.

Revan only wants to rescue the captive Jedi Bastila Shan and get off of [Taris](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Taris "Taris"). Their only desire is to survive and get back to the Republic.

### Supernatural Aid

Before the Hero sets out on the path of the story, they are usually counseled by a mentor. This character shows up at exactly the right time to give the Hero an object or special advice that is designed to prepare them for the road ahead. Whatever it is that the mentor gives them, it is crucial for the Hero's ultimate success- though at the time it may not make much sense.

Luke Skywalker unknowingly seeks his mentor, Ben Kenobi. Luke's only intention is to bring Ben his Droids and find out what R2-D2's mysterious message is about. However, once the two are together, Ben enchants Luke with stories of the Jedi Knights, gives the boy his father's [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"), and gives him his first lessons in [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"). As we know, this pivotal point is exactly what Luke needs to survive what lays in store for him.

Obi-Wan's aid is less dramatic. His mentor, Qui-Gon Jinn travels with him and the Padawan spends much time checking in with his master for guidance. It is, however, Qui-Gon's teachings that allow him to survive the rocky road of *The Phantom Menace*.

Once Revan escaped from [Taris](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Taris "Taris"), they were brought to [Dantooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dantooine "Dantooine"). The Jedi Enclave there took Revan in, teaching them in the ways of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") (or reawakening the teachings). The lessons that Revan learned from the enclave are the only thing that could have prepared them for their ultimate confrontation with Darth Malak.

You'll notice that the Refusal of the Call and Supernatural Aid may not always occur one after the other. These two points can be used flexibly in your story- sometimes they are reversed.

### The Crossing of the First Threshold

Eventually, the Hero is ready (or is forced) to go beyond their ordinary world into the special world of the story. This moment is often marked and protected by a Threshold Guardian, who must be overcome in order for the Hero to be committed to and worthy of the adventure ahead. Overcoming the Threshold Guardian doesn't always need to be violent, but often is in *Star Wars*.

Luke, having lost everything he's known, must still pass through the hornet's nest that is the seedier side of Mos Eisley and survive a Stormtrooper attack to leave the planet. By proving that he can survive and handle himself in the face of imminent danger, Luke is worthy of joining Ben on his adventure.

Obi-Wan and Qui-Gon don't just get to leave [Naboo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Naboo "Naboo") with ease. They are forced to infiltrate the city and fight their out past the forces of the invading Trade Federation. By facing an army, rescuing the Queen Amidala, and surviving, Obi-Wan proves that his ready for the coming of [The Sith](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Sith "The Sith").

Revan must fight against ancient [Rakatan Guardian Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rakatan_Guardian_Droids?action=edit&redlink=1 "Rakatan Guardian Droids (page does not exist)") in the crypt on [Dantooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dantooine "Dantooine"). By destroying these deadly Droids, they prove that they are strong enough to learn the secret of the Star Maps- and ultimately the one to walk the path of destroying [The Star Forge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Star_Forge "The Star Forge").

### Belly of the Whale

Just as Jonah was transformed by surviving his ordeal with the whale, the Hero is transformed into something new by crossing the first threshold. The Hero enters the special world of the story changed.

- Luke takes his first steps towards becoming a Jedi and a man of action.
- Obi-Wan is transformed into a man worthy of being a Jedi Knight.
- Revan becomes a Jedi Knight, growing from being a lost amnesiac (and former Sith Lord).

## Act II

Things really start to happen in the first half of Act II. The Hero begins to uncover pieces of the puzzle (or come closer to the ultimate goal by steps) by overcoming hurdles one at a time. Every step along the way builds the drama of the story.

### The Road of Trials

The Hero has now entered the special world of the story and things start to heat up. Nothing is going to be easy from here on out. They must now face a series of obstacles and challenges. While each has consequences, they also hold rewards- they amplify their consciousness and awareness of the greater world and cause them to grown in ability in order to more effectively face what lies ahead.

Luke Skywalker, after his escape from [Tatooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tatooine "Tatooine"), is trained by Obi-Wan is the ways of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") and [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") combat. Hurdle one is learning new skills while mastering himself. As that is happening, they travel to [Alderaan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Alderaan "Alderaan"), as directed by Princess Leia. Hurdle two is getting there. He arrives to find the planet destroyed and is promptly captured by the Empire. Hurdle three is survival and escape. Then, he discovers that Princess Leia is being held captive on [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"). Hurdle four is pushing deep into the lion's den. Finally, he and his companions must stage a daring escape now that enemy knows they are there. Hurdle five, escape and survive again.

Obi-Wan and Qui-Gon must take their damaged ship to [Tatooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tatooine "Tatooine"), deal with unfriendly locals who will not accept their currency, help the young Anakin Skywalker win the Podraces, repair their ship, and ultimately face the new threat of [The Sith](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Sith "The Sith") through Darth Maul.

Revan's journey is long and arduous. They must endure the trials of three planets before things really heat up, including the hostile sand people of [Tatooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tatooine "Tatooine"), the corporate greed and oppression of [The Czerka Corporation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Czerka_Corporation "The Czerka Corporation") on [Kashyyyk](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Kashyyyk "Kashyyyk"), and the litigious détente of Republic and Sith on [Manaan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Manaan "Manaan"). Each planet functions as its own Road of Trials, each building to a conclusion that catapults Revan on the path to the next.

As you can see, the obstacles seem to ratchet up in intensity with every success. These challenges are designed to build towards the Midpoint- the middle of the movie, where everything changes.

#### Subplots

As the Hero journeys into the special world of the story, smaller personal plots begin to grow. These subplots highlight the human element of the story, taking focus away from the grand plot at hand to show that those involved are just people. These humanizing elements are important and allow us to identify more fully with the Heroes.

In *A New Hope*, Luke and Ben's personal relationship as apprentice and master grows. In *The Phantom Menace*, the rescue and training of young Anakin Skywalker become an important element. In *The Empire Strikes Back*, the love affair between Leia and Han blossoms. In *Knights of the Old Republic*, Revan's memories begin to return as visions, driving them towards the revelation of their identity and the cover up that went with it.

### Midpoint

Halfway through such a tale, something always happens to change things so that the second half is fundamentally different from the first. It is an exciting change, where we discover that the story wasn't exactly what we thought it was and that the drama is even more than we expected. It is to this point that the Road of Trials builds. Sometimes the Midpoint is a false relief and sometimes it brings further darkness to the story.

- Luke Skywalker and his companions finally believe that they have escaped Darth Vader and [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"). It is false comfort, however, as the Empire planted a homing beacon on [The *Millennium Falcon*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Millennium_Falcon "The Millennium Falcon") and is now tracking them to a primary rebel base.
- Obi-Wan and Qui-Gon discover that the Trade Federation blockade is hardly the real threat. [The Sith](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Sith "The Sith") have returned to the galaxy.
- Revan, while on their travels, discovers that [The Sith Empire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Sith_Empire "The Sith Empire") have destroyed the Jedi Enclave on [Dantooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dantooine "Dantooine"), the birthplace of the new Revan. Their light has been extinguished from the galaxy.

To review, at the heart of the monomyth is the structure of the story. Each Hero, regardless of who they are and the setting in which they live, follows a similar path. This structure has existed in mythology for generations and it speaks to us. It resonates with the human condition and what it takes to truly overcome and persevere. When we experience a story that utilizes the structure of the monomyth, it occurs to us as meaningful and epic. We identify with the Heroes and join them on what is most like the adventure of a lifetime.

Again, the important thing to keep in mind is to not take the titles of the stages too literally- instead, study the spirit of what they represent. When you are creating your *Star Wars* roleplaying story, knowing how to broadly implement these stages will allow you to use the monomyth over and over again, with it never appearing the same way twice.

## Act II

Things really start to happen in the first half of Act II. The Hero begins to uncover pieces of the puzzle (or come closer to the ultimate goal by steps) by overcoming hurdles one at a time. Every step along the way builds the drama of the story.

### Mother as Goddess

Once conditions have changed (the Midpoint), the Hero has a moment where one of two things might happen. They may experience some kind of connection with an opposite sex figure that allows them to unite their masculine and feminine sides, or to simply mature. They may also experience a kind of total love that alters their world, similar to the kind of love an infant might feel from their mother.

- In *A New Hope*, Luke Skywalker has a crush on Princess Leia that intensifies when they meet face to face in The Death Star's detention block. This forces him to man up ways he had not previously. Fortunately, he does not know she's his sister yet.
- In *The Phantom Menace*, Obi-Wan Kenobi experiences the love Shmi Skywalker has for her young son, Anakin. She is willing to let him go off into the galaxy to have a better life and make a difference, even though she can't come with and will most likely never see him again.
- In *Knights of the Old Republic*, Revan experiences the love of Elora for her Jedi husband Sunry. She continues to love him, even though he was having an affair with a Dark Jedi and was also guilty of killing her in cold blood. If love like this can exist, then Revan can be redeemed.

### Woman as Temptress

The special world is not content to leave the Hero to their experience of union or love. Temptations arrive that attempt to lead the Hero from their path, to a path that might destroy them or remove them from the equation. In *Star Wars*, as many Heroes are Jedi, this is often represented by the lure of [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force").

- Luke Skywalker must deal with his first experience with [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") on [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"), through Darth Vader. Though it does not immediately tempt him, it is the shades of things to come.
- Obi-Wan Kenobi has his first true experience with [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side"), as Darth Maul attacks on [Tatooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tatooine "Tatooine"). Though Obi-Wan isn't tempted immediately, Maul represents the thing against which he must be wary.
- Revan travels to the Sith homeworld of [Korriban](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Korriban "Korriban") and experiences the pull of [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") through the Sith Academy and in the tombs of the Dark Lords. It is a world of their own creation, calling for them to return.

### Atonement with the Father

Finally, the Hero meets the point to which the entire journey has been building. This is where the Hero must confront the ultimate power of their journey in the special world. This ultimate power doesn't always need to be a person- it can also be a thing or some kind of event.

- On [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"), Luke must confront the overwhelming forces of the Empire as they try to escape. He witnesses the confrontation between Ben Kenobi and Darth Vader, as the former apprentice exacts a decades-old vendetta.
- As Obi-Wan and Qui-Gon finally overcome the trials of [Tatooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tatooine "Tatooine") in order to get back into the real story afoot, Darth Maul appears without warning and attacks. He is the most dangerous foe they have ever fought, and also represents a much bigger threat to the galaxy
- Revan finally finishes facing the shades and tombs of the Dark Lords of [Korriban](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Korriban "Korriban") to find the final Star Map. They now knows the secret location of [The Star Forge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Star_Forge "The Star Forge").

### Apotheosis

Because of their experience confronting the ultimate power, death enters the picture. Sometimes the Hero is transformed by the threat of death or the experience of another's death. Sometimes someone close to them transcends death to move onto the world of the spirit. Sometimes, death is represented by a lull, the eye of the storm, where there is a period of rest. Sometimes it is several elements of the above.

- Luke quickly becomes no stranger to death. He is transformed by the death of Ben Kenobi (Though he is transformed into spirit) and by the threat of his own death. He is no longer the brash farm boy- he is becoming something else.
- Obi-Wan is confronted by a manifestation of death- Darth Maul. His experience with the Sith changes him.
- Revan is confronted by their own death, or at least the death of their identity as a Dark Lord of the Sith. They finally make a choice- be who they were or who they have become.

### The Ultimate Boon

Through their trials, confronting the ultimate power, and apotheosis, the Hero has earned the thing that brought them to the special world in the first place. They have attained the Holy Grail of their adventure, the thing that makes all the difference.

- R2-D2 and Princess Leia in hand, Luke heads back with the plans to [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star") to save the day.
- All the pieces fall into place for Obi-Wan. All that is left is to help Queen Amidala return to [Naboo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Naboo "Naboo") to rescue her people from the predations of the Trade Federation.
- Revan discovers the location of [The Star Forge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Star_Forge "The Star Forge") and how to get there.

## Act III

This act is for building to the ultimate point of the story and for wrapping up loose ends. The Heroes should experience increased tribulations, to the point at which they are not sure how they are supposed to survive or succeed, leading up to the climax- the most important or exciting part of your story. From the climax, they move into the denouement, which should tie up all loose ends and answer most, if not all, questions. In general, everything after the climax should be quick.

### Refusal of the Return

Now that the Hero has been transformed by their journey, there may a couple of reasons why they wouldn't want to return. They may be excited by their adventure in the special world that they do not want to go back to the ordinary world. Alternatively, they may wonder if they should bring their hard-won ultimate boon back to the ordinary world, who may not be ready for or deserve it.

In *Star Wars*, this typically manifests a little differently. The Hero usually does not have to go back to the ordinary world, but instead make a choice to return to a version of the special world that will become their new ordinary world. What was is gone, but there will be routine in what is to come.

- Luke Skywalker joins [The Rebel Alliance](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Rebel_Alliance "The Rebel Alliance") as a fighter pilot. This is a far cry from the farm boy he recently was, but he is also no longer a special hero as he was on [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star").
- Obi-Wan is no longer just the Padawan, nor is the intrepid adventurer that has travelled with Qui-Gon. He is now being relied upon to behave as a Jedi Knight and a full-blown warrior.
- Revan is no longer an amnesiac soldier, nor are they the heroic adventurer. They are now a Jedi Knight, being relied upon by the Jedi Council to destroy everything they built as a Dark Lord.

### The Magic Flight

Oftentimes, the journey to return with the boon is as perilous is the journey to get it. Sometimes the Hero has stirred up the hornet's nest by stealing the ultimate boon and the forces guarding it chase them to get it back. This is often the part of the story where an exciting life-or-death chase occurs.

- Luke and his friends flee [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"), but they are chased by [TIE Fighters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/TIE_Fighters "TIE Fighters"). They must fend off the [TIE Fighters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/TIE_Fighters "TIE Fighters") until they can get clear and plot a Hyperspace route.
- Obi-Wan and his friends must break through the Trade Federation blockade in order to get to [Naboo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Naboo "Naboo").
- Revan and their companions must break through the Sith lines guarding [The Star Forge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Star_Forge "The Star Forge") and the planet [Lehon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lehon "Lehon").

### Rescue from Without

When the Hero began their journey, they needed the guidance of a Mentor in order to prepare them properly to survive and succeed. Sometimes the Hero needs powerful guidance or the help of rescuers to get back from their journey. The forces that oppose them may be too great, now that they have stirred up the hornet's nest, or they may have been weakened by their experience in the special world.

- Strangely, Darth Vader is actually the thing that rescues Luke and his friends. He lets them go, for he has planted a tracking device on [The *Millennium Falcon*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Millennium_Falcon "The Millennium Falcon") that will lead him to the Rebel's base.
- The [Gungans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gungans "Gungans") are Obi-Wan's rescue. In order for the small band of [Naboo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Naboo "Naboo") security volunteers and the Jedi to infiltrate the palace and capture Nute Gunray, they need the Gungan Army to draw out the Trade Federation's army.
- Revan receives their rescue from the [Rakatans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rakatans?action=edit&redlink=1 "Rakatans (page does not exist)") that still hold the old ways. They help Revan disarm the field that prevents ships from reaching [The Star Forge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Star_Forge "The Star Forge").

### Crossing of the Return Threshold

Just as they crossed a threshold to get to the special world, the Hero must also cross a threshold to get back. This can often be difficult for the Hero, as they are returning to the ordinary world and must accept it as the place they belong.

Again, in *Star Wars*, this often manifests a little differently. The Hero is returning to a new ordinary world, distinct from the world in which they began, and they must accept their place in it- which may not be as prominent as the place they occupied in the special world.

Similar to the Refusal of the Call, Luke Skywalker becomes a run-of-the-mill fighter pilot for [The Rebel Alliance](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Rebel_Alliance "The Rebel Alliance"), Obi-Wan becomes an agent of the Jedi Council, and Revan becomes a Jedi Knight in service to the Council, not even a general.

### Master of Two Worlds

The Hero ultimately triumphs because they can master both the world inside of them and the world outside of them, the special world and the ordinary world, the material world and the spiritual world. During the climax, they reach a new understanding of themselves and the things around them.

- Luke begins to use [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") in the trenches of [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star").
- Obi-Wan masters his fear while facing Darth Maul.
- Revan masters themselves, balancing [The Light Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Light_Side "The Light Side") they are with [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") they were.

### Freedom to Live

The Hero's new understanding and mastery leads them to freedom. They now have the freedom to live as they are, no longer afraid of failure or death. Instead of being the Hero of what once was, they become the Hero of now and what is to come.

- Luke uses [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") to fire the proton torpedo that destroys [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"). By doing so, he also begins to resurrect [The Jedi Order](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi_Order "The Jedi Order").
- Obi-Wan conquers his fear and baits Darth Maul into thinking he is defeated- he then kills his assailant. In doing so, he cripples the Sith for the moment and becomes the first guardian against their return.
- Revan rejects their old ways to destroy Darth Malak and [The Star Forge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Star_Forge "The Star Forge"). They bring an end to [The Sith Empire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Sith_Empire "The Sith Empire"), the very thing they built.

## Tips & Tactics

*By Owen K.C. Stephens*

Welcome back to Tips and Tactics, the column of *Star Wars* gaming advice. (This is actually the third edition of Tips and Tactics. The first edition covered the previous version of the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*. The second edition focused more on the *Star Wars Miniatures Game*.)

This new incarnation of Tips and Tactics will cover both the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition* and the *Star Wars Miniatures Game*. Approximately every two weeks, Tips and Tactics will give hints, ideas, advice, or alternative rule ideas. Anything from covering the basics for new players to sneaky strategies for experienced participants is fair game, and from time to time, we'll even discuss how to combine the two games.

The Starfighter ShuffleMultiple AttacksSynergy

The new *Saga Edition* has been extensively streamlined to make it easier for new players to learn and to keep the pace as fast as the movies on which the game is based. Most players who have experience with previous editions of the game have little difficulty getting into Saga Edition, but a few nuances can create potential stumbling blocks for players used to how things worked before.

In particular, some have questions about the new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system. Players want their characters to be truly exceptional at certain roles, but they don't know what to do after training their heroes in the relevant [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). For example, if Carl wants to make his character Gilac an ace [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter") pilot, obviously he takes [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") as a [Trained Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained_Skill "Trained Skill"). But how does he set Gilac apart from other characters who also have [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") as a [Trained Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained_Skill "Trained Skill") but who aren't dedicated [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter") pilots? Is there a way to excel at that role without reducing the character's ability to be useful in other situations?

For [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter") pilots in particular, there are two ways to set a character apart as an expert, rather than someone who is merely "Trained." First, [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship")-specific [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") can set you apart from other heroes [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot"). The [Vehicular Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicular_Combat "Vehicular Combat") Feat makes you much more dangerous in [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat"), especially if you have a good [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") check. Combined with [Skill Focus (Pilot)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Pilot) "Skill Focus (Pilot)"), you can build a character who can avoid attacks in combat even when flying a slow junker. Of course, these [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") have few applications outside [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat"), and you might want to wait to take them until you're of a higher level (unless the campaign is based around [Starship Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Combat "Starship Combat"), in which case these [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") are hard to beat as early options). Similarly, anything from the Scoundrel's [Spacer Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Spacer_Talent_Tree "Spacer Talent Tree") gives you an edge in [Starship Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Combat "Starship Combat").

The other option is to take combat [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and talents that are useful both in [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat") and in [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat"). For example, the [Point-Blank Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Point-Blank_Shot "Point-Blank Shot") Feat applies to both hand-held ranged weapons and vehicular weapons, which also have a Point-Blank [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range"). Taking this Feat increases your effectiveness in [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat"), while still being useful in [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat") situations. Other [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") that fit that description include [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire"), [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot"), [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot"), and [Precise Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Precise_Shot "Precise Shot") (which is useful for firing into [Dogfights](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dogfights "Dogfights")). Numerous other [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") can serve double duty if taken with [Heavy Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Weapons "Heavy Weapons") (such as [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus")), as can the Soldier [Weapon Specialist Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Specialist_Talent_Tree "Weapon Specialist Talent Tree").

Once you reach 7th level, if you truly wish to be an ace fighter pilot, you should move to the [Ace Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ace_Pilot "Ace Pilot") Prestige Class. Although you don't gain the Bonus Feats available to a [Heroic Class](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Class "Heroic Class"), the [Vehicle Dodge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Dodge "Vehicle Dodge") ability gives you defensive capability in a [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter") that members of others classes can't manage. There's no rule preventing you from taking multiple [Prestige Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prestige_Classes "Prestige Classes"), and some characters might also want to take a few levels of [Bounty Hunter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bounty_Hunter "Bounty Hunter") or [Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Officer "Officer"), depending on their style of fighting.

Another popular [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship")-related option is to forgo the *Saga Edition* rules and play out combat using the *Star Wars Miniatures Starship Battles* game. Why? First, players who are already familiar with and enjoy the miniatures game might want to incorporate those rules just for the fun of it. Also, the Starship Battles rules currently have statistics for more [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") (especially [Starfighters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighters "Starfighters") and [Capital Ships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Capital_Ships "Capital Ships")), making it easier to run battles with numerous different classes of craft. Furthermore, while the miniatures rules are tactically more complex, as is fitting for a combat game, they handle large fleet engagements more easily than the roleplaying game rules. So, you gain more tactical options and the ability to play out large conflicts with ease. However, the drawback to incorporating Starship Battles rules is that [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat") is largely ignored.

For most roleplaying game scenarios, the Gamemaster will decide what ships are available without worrying too much about point cost. Of course, looking at point cost is a good way to see whether the two sides are balanced (though consider discussions of victory conditions and the heroic element to learn why the fleets still might not be balanced). Since the idea is to use the miniatures rules to tell part of a roleplaying game's story, the GM can set the victory conditions rather than simply requiring one side to destroy the other. For example, let's say that an escape sequence is being played out, such as [The *Millennium Falcon*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Millennium_Falcon "The Millennium Falcon") trying to get free of [Hoth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hoth?action=edit&redlink=1 "Hoth (page does not exist)"). One player might be massively outmatched, but perhaps in order to win, he needs only to get off the stellar grid.

Of course, the Starship Battles rules have no options for modifying [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") when heroic characters are piloting them. The miniatures are static, designed to represent typical ships and a few famous specific vehicles, not to detail the exact abilities of a particular hero's [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter"). If none of the heroic characters are exceptional pilots, typical [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") might be fine for their characters. Otherwise, a few changes need to be made.

The simplest way to represent heroic characters is to pick ships that are close representations of the character's ship and abilities. For example, if most nonheroic pilots are in X-Wings, you can give the heroic pilot an X-Wing Starfighter Ace, a Rogue Squadron X-Wing, or even Luke Skywalker's X-Wing.

However, if that doesn't quite balance the relative skill of heroic characters, it's easy to boost ships' attack values until they're more in scale to the heroes piloting them. To keep things simple, defense, damage, and hull ratings are unchanged. It's best to stay with the miniatures game rules for movement, so that a class 1 ship moves 1 square per turn, a class 2 ship moves 2 squares per turn, and so on. While this doesn't map perfectly to the roleplaying game rules, there's no point in using miniature rules for their simplicity, and then making them more complex!

Many heroic characters also have [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), and abilities that make them better than a typical pilot. Rather than try to rewrite these for the miniatures game, just use existing miniatures abilities to emulate their functions. The following list provides examples of how to emulate *Saga Edition* (SE) roleplaying game elements with abilities from the Starship Battles rules (SB).

- Any character with **[Force Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Pilot "Force Pilot")** (SE) should be given the Force Sense (SB) ability.
- Any [Vehicle Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Pilot "Vehicle Pilot") with [Vehicular Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicular_Combat "Vehicular Combat") (SE) should gain the Evasions (SB) ability.
- Any heroic character who takes the [System Operator](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/System_Operator "System Operator") position (SE) on a ship gives it the Sensor Array (SB) ability.
- Any heroic character with levels in the [Bounty Hunter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bounty_Hunter "Bounty Hunter") Prestige Class (SE) gains the Bounty Hunter (SB) ability.
- Heroic characters with multiple levels of the [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") Heroic Class or [Officer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Officer "Officer") Prestige Class (SE) should gain either the Advanced Communication Array (SB) or Sith Command +2 (SB) abilities.

Using the miniatures rules also lets you give characters who aren't pilots something to do. For example, if a heroic ace pilot is handed Luke Skywalker's X-Wing to represent his character, other players can assume that their heroes are in the background and temporarily play typical pilots flying typical X-Wings. Thus, while [Leia Organa](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Leia_Organa "Leia Organa"), [R2-D2](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-D2 "R2-D2"), and [C-3PO](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/C-3PO "C-3PO") watch from the Rebel Base, their players can run typical pilots who help Luke take on [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star").

In previous versions of the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*, characters automatically gained access to multiple attacks per round as their [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") increased. This approach had two drawbacks. First, you had to take a [Full Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full_Attack "Full Attack") Action to use the multiple attacks, which encouraged characters to stand still during combat, making fights less dynamic. Second, every attack after the first was made at an increasingly large penalty, so additional attacks were less likely to affect the outcome. As a result, too often combat was slowed by die rolls that had little chance of changing anything, and sometimes whoever won initiative in the first round of combat had an advantage out of balance for a single roll.

Of course, it's important for more experienced characters to be better at combat, but multiple attacks per round is just one solution to that issue. In *Saga Edition*, a single attack is the norm, and more experienced characters deal more damage. This keeps combatants mobile (since you generally have a [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action") free every round), takes up less time (since there are fewer attack rolls), and makes higher-level characters more effective (since their single shot deals more damage than that of a lower-level character). In addition, [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") such as [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") and [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike") represent multiple attacks by adding another damage multiple, which helps keep the action fast-paced and interesting.

However, you still can make multiple attacks in a round, generally through [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") selection. [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") and [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack") let you take two or three shots, respectively, using weapons from a given class. The Dual Weapon Mastery [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") also grant access to multiple attacks and are available earlier in a character's development. Though it would be a major investment of feats, it's possible to use [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack"), [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack"), and [Dual Weapon Mastery I](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dual_Weapon_Mastery_I "Dual Weapon Mastery I"), [Dual Weapon Mastery II](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dual_Weapon_Mastery_II "Dual Weapon Mastery II"), and [Dual Weapon Mastery III](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dual_Weapon_Mastery_III "Dual Weapon Mastery III") together to achieve an amazing number of attacks in a single round. For example, a character with all five [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") using a [Double-Bladed Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double-Bladed_Lightsaber "Double-Bladed Lightsaber") could make four attacks (with a penalty of -10 to each attack roll) as a [Full Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full_Attack "Full Attack") Action.

So, when should you take these [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats")? When should you use them? And for that matter, are you better off with [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") or Dual Weapon Mastery when you first start selecting [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats")? In general, only characters with high attack bonuses (those who mostly have levels in [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") or [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier"), or in [Prestige Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prestige_Classes "Prestige Classes") designed for [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") and [Soldiers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldiers "Soldiers")) should consider these [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"). Other characters can't afford the attack penalty that comes with multiple attacks; they should use [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") to increase combat effectiveness in other ways.

For characters with high attack bonuses, taking [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") to allow multiple attacks makes the most sense at around 6th level. Before that level, the penalties are simply too large for your attack bonus to be useful (and besides, [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") isn't available at lower levels). Usually, the [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") are most useful against multiple weaker foes. While you can deal considerable damage with [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") or by using two weapons, when fighting a foe close to your skill level, the penalties such attacks impose mean that you're less likely to hit. You might actually deal less damage each round.

The advantages of using two weapons over [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") are obvious- the [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") work with any weapons you can use in one hand, and they can be gained earlier. The drawbacks are more subtle. If you're fighting with two weapons, you don't have a free hand to use a [Comlink](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Comlink "Comlink"), throw a [Thermal Detonator](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thermal_Detonator "Thermal Detonator"), punch a command console, or grab a fallen comrade.

Another advantage is the availability of [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack") at higher character levels. A well-built character with a high [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") and [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") dedicated to increasing ranged attack bonuses can challenge numerous lesser foes with [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack"), especially if they can find [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") during the battle. If you want to use [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") with a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"), [Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rifle "Rifle"), or [Heavy Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Weapon "Heavy Weapon"), you should also look at the **Multiattack Proficiency** [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") for the [Elite Trooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Elite_Trooper "Elite Trooper") and the [Jedi Knight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Knight "Jedi Knight") Prestige Classes.

Never risk multiple attacks if doing so leaves you exposed. [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack") should be an option in combat, not a requirement. It's often better to move to a superior position, ready a piece of equipment, or manipulate the battlefield to your advantage than to stand in one spot and make two or three attacks. The only time you should use a [Double-Bladed Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double-Bladed_Lightsaber "Double-Bladed Lightsaber") with Dual Weapon Mastery is if you see no good options other than attacking.

While any group of four characters can be a successful team, giving them different [Heroic Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Classes "Heroic Classes") and roles can produce groups that are more effective and more flexible. Don't worry if that means the only hero is a Force-using [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi"). The other heroes can add more to the group's overall success exactly because they aren't [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")-toting masters of melee.

For example, a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") might have the same attack bonus and [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") as a [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier"), but that doesn't mean a [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") has nothing to offer. In addition to being much better in heavy armor (and the only character for whom [Armor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor "Armor") can still be an excellent choice at mid or high level), a [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") has numerous options that can aid other characters in combat. The [Commando Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Commando_Talent_Tree "Commando Talent Tree") has **[Battle Analysis](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Battle_Analysis "Battle Analysis")**, **[Cover Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover_Fire "Cover Fire")**, **[Draw Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Draw_Fire "Draw Fire")**, and **[Harm's Way](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Harm%27s_Way "Harm's Way")**, all of which can make even a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") more effective in group combat. Combined with [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") that allow a [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") to take amazing amounts of damage and deal similarly heavy wounds at range, the result is an ally who can make it through any combat and harm foes before they get too close.

A [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble"), too, can help to improve the performance of other characters. The [Inspiration Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Inspiration_Talent_Tree "Inspiration Talent Tree") and [Leadership Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Leadership_Talent_Tree "Leadership Talent Tree") are full of abilities that help [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") and [Soldiers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldiers "Soldiers") be even more dangerous in combat, but those [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") have other uses as well. The ability to increase [Skill Checks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Checks "Skill Checks") can mean the difference between dying in a trash compactor and escaping an Imperial space station in a run-down freighter. A [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") can also restore health to allies, allowing them to keep doing what they're good at in longer fights. Finally, while a [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") might not be a master of hand-to-hand combat, their ability to [Intimidate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intimidate "Intimidate") foes and move them down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") can overcome opponents who are physically intimidating but mentally average.

[Scoundrels](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrels "Scoundrels") and [Scouts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scouts "Scouts") can hold their own in a fight, but their real benefit to a group is often outside of combat. Part of the fun of a roleplaying game is facing different situations and working as a team to overcome them. A group that lacks a [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") or a [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") is at a serious disadvantage when flying through an asteroid field, tracking down an escaped foe, negotiating a deal with a [Hutt](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hutt "Hutt"), sneaking up on an encampment, sneaking through the halls of an enemy base, or fixing a broken speeder. If you're playing either of these [Heroic Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Classes "Heroic Classes"), ask the other players what [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") their characters are [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in, and take at least a few [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") that aren't already covered. Chances are that no one else is [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in [Stealth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stealth "Stealth") or [Survival](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Survival "Survival"), and perhaps not [Acrobatics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatics "Acrobatics"), [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb"), [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump"), or [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics"). Being good in areas where other characters are not is the heart of group synergy, and the [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") and [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") are perfect choices for that.

Other important choices have little or nothing to do with class. For example, it's useful to have a trained pilot or someone who can use [Medpacs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medpacs "Medpacs") effectively. Having a mix of melee weapons and ranged weapons often pays off in combat. Similarly, a group that owns a wide variety of equipment is more versatile than one that has six [Fire Extinguishers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fire_Extinguishers "Fire Extinguishers") but no [Electrobinoculars](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Electrobinoculars "Electrobinoculars"). Talk to other players during [Character Creation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Creation "Character Creation") and as their heroes gain levels, and you'll find ways to take advantage of their strengths and cover their weaknesses.

The fringe is ever-present in the *Star Wars* universe. No matter what the era, there are always those who live in the shadows or apart from the galaxy at large. They may serve as enemies or allies for the heroes. This stat pack includes *Saga Edition* roleplaying game statistics for Star Wars Miniatures that are suitable for use during any era and that haven't been presented in other roleplaying products or previews.

*By Sterling Hershey*

[The New Republic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_New_Republic "The New Republic") ruled much of the galaxy from the Emperor's defeat until well into the Yuuzhan Vong Invasion. Some individuals naturally transitioned from a position or job in the Rebellion to a similar role in the new government or armed forces. New species and organizations also came to the fore of galactic events during this time. This stat pack includes *Saga Edition* roleplaying game statistics for Star Wars Miniatures that are suitable for use during this era and that haven't been presented in other roleplaying products or previews. Some of these characters are also usable in or adaptable to other eras.

## Jedi Counseling

*By Gary M. Sarli*

In "Jedi Counseling," Gary M. Sarli answers your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

105106107108109110111112113114115

Welcome to the 105th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

*Saga Edition* is out, and we have our first batch of questions about the new system.

## Area Attacks

**Q:** It seems to me that [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") might be too good. Even if you miss, you're still dealing at least half damage unless the target has **[Evasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Evasion "Evasion")** or [Improved Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Cover "Improved Cover"). Doesn't this make things like [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") (which you can do even with a run-of-the-mill [Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Rifle "Blaster Rifle")) a no-brainer unless your attack bonus greatly exceeds the target's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense")?

**A:** A few details were left out of the [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack") rules. They're in the first set of errata, but they're worth covering here:

First, when making an [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack"), your modified attack roll (after subtracting penalties for [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range"), [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment"), and so on) must be a 10 or higher for the attack to have any effect. If that condition is met, you compare the result to the [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") of everyone in the area for half or full damage. Given this, it's possible to make such a poor attack that no one is affected at all. In these cases, the idea is that your throw lands somewhere harmless, you shot over the target's heads (in the case of [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire")), or maybe you even forgot to arm the [Grenade](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grenade "Grenade") before throwing.

Second, if you make an [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack") against a target that has [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") (not just [Improved Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Cover "Improved Cover")), the target takes no damage if the attack misses. Just as in real life, the best defense against explosives or bursts of [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") is to get in a trench or behind an obstacle. To determine if a target has [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover"), draw a line from the center of the burst or splash radius (for explosives and the like) or from the attacker (for [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire")).

Third, you can't [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") when making an [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack"), so you never get to ignore the target's [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover").

Together, these details make [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") and [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") incredibly effective against a target at relatively close range on open terrain. Such a target is in big, big trouble. In contrast, [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") and [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") are almost worthless against a target that has [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") or [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment") or that is at great [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range"). For example, don't bother trying it against a hidden sniper shooting from hundreds of meters away.

## Deflect and Block vs Area Attacks

**Q:** The **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** Talent can reduce the damage from an [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") attack. Can **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")** reduce the damage from [Whirlwind Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Whirlwind_Attack "Whirlwind Attack") (which is also resolved as an [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack"))?

**A:** **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")** versus [Whirlwind Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Whirlwind_Attack "Whirlwind Attack") (or any other melee [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack") that might exist in the future) is supposed to work the same as **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** versus [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire"). If your [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check equals or exceeds the attack roll, you take half damage if the attack was a hit, and you take no damage if the attack was a miss. If your [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check is less than the attack roll, resolve the [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack") normally.

## Dual Weapon Mastery vs Double Attack

**Q:** I'm curious as to the perceived balance factor of Dual Weapon Mastery versus [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack"). [Dual Weapon Mastery I](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dual_Weapon_Mastery_I "Dual Weapon Mastery I") is available sooner, and you can offset its penalties by taking more [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"). [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") works for only one type of weapon, and you can reduce its penalties only with Prestige Class [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"). Since both grant an extra attack, why would you want [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack")?

**A:** The balancing factor is that Dual Weapon Mastery works only with [One-Handed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/One-Handed "One-Handed") weapons (that is, weapons that do less damage), but [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack")/[Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack") works with [Two-Handed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Two-Handed "Two-Handed") weapons (but only one type of them). For example:

- A Dual Weapon Mastery specialist might use two [Heavy Blaster Pistols](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Blaster_Pistols "Heavy Blaster Pistols"), which deal 3d8 points of damage per weapon, or a potential of 6d8 points from both.
- A [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") specialist might use a [Heavy Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Blaster_Rifle "Heavy Blaster Rifle"), which deals 3d10 points of damage but has a much better [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range"). In addition, the weapon is capable of [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire"), so it can deal up to 5d10 points of damage with [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire").

This also means that [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") can be used with much larger weapons, such as those on a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle"), while a [Vehicle Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Pilot "Vehicle Pilot") or [Gunner](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gunner "Gunner") would have no particular reason to use Dual Weapon Mastery because such weapons can't be operated with one hand.

Finally, don't forget that someone can use both Dual Weapon Mastery and [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack")/[Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack"). So even if Dual Weapon Mastery appears more attractive at first, you might want to take [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack")/[Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack") later.

## Multiple Attacks with Multiple Targets

**Q:** Can you use [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") or [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack") to attack the same target multiple times in the same round, or do you have to attack different targets?

**A:** The extra attacks from [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack"), [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack"), and fighting with multiple weapons can be directed at the same target or at different targets, in any combination and any order that you choose.

## Double and Rapid Attacks

**Q:** It is possible to use [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") with [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike")?

**A:** Yes. [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike"), [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot"), and [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire") all can be used when making multiple attacks, regardless of their source ([Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack"), [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack"), or fighting with multiple weapons).

## Rapid Shot/Strike Prerequisites

**Q:** I think the prerequisites for [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") and [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike") were swapped accidentally. Wouldn't it make more sense for [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike"), a melee [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat"), to have the [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") requirement, and for [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot"), a ranged [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat"), to have the [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") requirement?

**A:** No, this is intentional. For [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot"), you need the higher [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") to control the recoil of firing multiple shots so quickly. For [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike"), you need the higher [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") to have the agility necessary to get two swings for the price of one- catching your target a second time on the backswing, spinning to come around a second time, and so on.

For balance purposes, this also has the effect of giving characters some tough decisions. A ranged specialist won't always want to neglect their [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength"), and a melee specialist won't want to neglect their [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity"). (Sure, such a character could neglect [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") if they have [Weapon Finesse](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Finesse "Weapon Finesse"), but they're spending an extra [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") and restricting themselves to certain weapons for that privilege.)

Welcome to the 106th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Building Lightsabers

**Q:** The [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class table includes "Build Lightsaber" at 7th level. Does this mean that only a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") can build a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")?

**A:** No. As noted in [Lightsaber Construction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Construction "Lightsaber Construction"), a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") can be built by any character who has at least seven Heroic Levels, has the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat, and is proficient with [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers"). The entry in the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class is just meant to be a reminder of this fact.

## Block, Deflect, and Redirect

**Q:** Why are **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")**, **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")**, and **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** three separate [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents")? All Jedi- even the younglings that [Yoda](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yoda "Yoda") trained- seem to be capable of this basic ability, so why isn't it a single [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") (or even a built-in class feature) that you get at 1st level?

**A:** First, there's a difference between cinematically using a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") to deflect a shot or block an incoming blow, and mechanically gaining a specific benefit from deflecting or blocking an attack. All characters, [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") or otherwise, avoid attacks however they can- dodging, ducking, weaving, parrying, blocking, or even deflecting- and all of these things are built into a character's Reflex Defense. If you're proficient with and wielding a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"), at least some of the attacks that miss you are deflected, parried, or blocked in a cinematic sense. You aren't gaining any mechanical benefit from it, though, because you haven't managed to hone that ability enough to make a difference until you take the **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** or **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")** Talents. Once you take those [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), you gain the ability to defend yourself even more effectively, making a [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check to nullify an attack that beats your [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense").

As for why these abilities are separated into multiple [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), we decided that the abilities were so useful that they would be a "no-brainer" choice if they were combined into a single [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent"). In other words, almost every [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") would take this at 1st level, no matter what. No other choice would be competitive unless you were playing a character who was not combat oriented at all. In addition, making them separate [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") also gives each [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") more individuality.

Are you more of a duelist, specializing in one-on-one combat against dark Jedi and Sith apprentices? If so, then **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")** is your best choice.

Are you more of a diplomat or even an infiltrator who'd like a nice defensive ability in the event that you have to make a hasty retreat? **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** is a great choice, allowing you to avoid [Ranged Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attacks "Ranged Attacks") from an ambush or pursuers.

Are you a battlefield commander leading a platoon of [Clone Troopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Clone_Troopers "Clone Troopers") against waves of blaster-wielding Battle Droids? Both **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** and **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** are outstanding choices, allowing you to defend yourself during the battle and add to the ranged offensive capabilities of your unit, turning the enemy's fire against them.

In the end, abilities like these are extremely useful, and, in our opinion, it's worth spending a [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") for any one of them. Certainly, a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") might take all three (and many do), but it isn't necessary to take all three for every character concept, and some [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") may choose to take none at all.

Even without the extra mechanical benefits of these [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), you can still describe your [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") parrying blaster bolts out of the air or locking sabers with a Sith Lord. Game mechanics are meant to enhance the cinematic flair, not restrain it.

## Lightsaber Form Talents

**Q:** Why are the [Lightsaber Form Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talents "Lightsaber Form Talents") unique to the [Jedi Knight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Knight "Jedi Knight") Prestige Class? Various Sith Lords have been described as masters of a particular form, and not all of them are former Jedi Knights. Therefore, many would be unable to take the [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") associated with the form they have mastered (or possibly invented). Is this a mistake?

**A:** No, it's not a mistake.
