A Jedi, Sith, or any other [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")-wielding character can use a form- even be considered a master of that form to in-universe observers- without having the associated [Lightsaber Form Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talent "Lightsaber Form Talent"). After all, being a "master of a form" is a label that's completely subjective, in- universe, and not based on game mechanics. Someone is a "master" if others perceive it to be so. Certainly, someone who takes a [Lightsaber Form Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talent "Lightsaber Form Talent") is probably recognized as a master by others, but that doesn't mean that only someone with a [Lightsaber Form Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talent "Lightsaber Form Talent") can be considered a master.

The [Lightsaber Form Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talents "Lightsaber Form Talents") are meant to represent unique benefits that Jedi- with their greater emphasis on meditative and disciplined study, the sublime emptiness of serenity, and patient reflection on the will of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force")- can gain from devoting themselves to a particular form. Sith, meanwhile, are not as meditative, disciplined, serene, or reflective. Instead, they're about passion, rage, strength, and desire, and their [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") (such as **[Dark Scourge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Scourge "Dark Scourge")** and **[Wicked Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wicked_Strike "Wicked Strike")**) reflect this.

In any case, a [Jedi Knight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Knight "Jedi Knight") and a [Sith Apprentice](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Apprentice "Sith Apprentice") of the same level, both devoting themselves to [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") combat, can learn the same number of [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")- and combat-related [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), but the specific [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") may vary. In other words, [The Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi "The Jedi") aren't better than [The Sith](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Sith "The Sith") when using a particular form; they're just different.

## Jedi Multiclassing

**Q:** When [Multiclassing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Multiclassing "Multiclassing"), do you need to have the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat to take a level in the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class?

**A:** No. You can take [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") as your starting [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") (or, if you wish, you can take a different starting [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat")).

**Q:** Does that mean that you could have a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") who is not Force-sensitive?

**A:** In game mechanical terms, yes, a character can have levels in the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class without having the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat. Obviously, they can't have any [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), such as **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** and **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")**, that require a [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check to function. Furthermore, they can't have any [Jedi Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Talents "Jedi Talents") that must be activated by spending a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point"). Such abilities are meant to reflect their actual connection to [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"), so a character without the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat can't use them.

To an *in-universe observer*, though, such a character would never be described as a "Jedi." They would be regarded as someone who has studied some of the Jedi arts, but few would confuse that with being a member of [The Jedi Order](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi_Order "The Jedi Order"). As an analogy, consider that someone on Earth who studies kung fu would not automatically be thought of as a Shaolin monk.

Remember that the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class isn't only about [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force")- it's broader, encompassing all the Jedi arts. Thus, a non-Force-sensitive duelist might study under a Jedi and take levels in the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class, becoming more skillful with a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") despite having no ability to use [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") (and thus no access to some of the class's best [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), such as **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** and **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")**). In fact, a character like [General Grievous](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/General_Grievous "General Grievous") fits this profile very well, and it would be perfectly valid to create his character with levels in [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") to reflect his years of study under [Count Dooku](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Count_Dooku "Count Dooku").

Alternatively, a character with a close connection to [The Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jedi "The Jedi"), such as an Antarian Ranger, might take levels in the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class, despite being non-Force-sensitive. Such a character might find some Jedi Guardian or Jedi Consular techniques rubbing off on them after working together for so long.

Finally- to borrow from the Shaolin analogy- a martial artist might take levels in [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") to pick up some [Jedi Guardian Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Guardian_Talents "Jedi Guardian Talents"), such as **[Acrobatic Recovery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatic_Recovery "Acrobatic Recovery")** or **[Elusive Target](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Elusive_Target "Elusive Target")**, as part of their studies. In none of these cases, though, would such a character be considered an actual "Jedi" to an in-universe observer.

Nevertheless, remember that the rules on [Multiclassing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Multiclassing "Multiclassing") specifically require that the Gamemaster approve of any [Multiclassing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Multiclassing "Multiclassing") choice. Before you take levels in the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class, the GM might require that you find a Jedi to teach you or that you have the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat (or both, or neither, or any other restriction that seems appropriate). There's absolutely nothing wrong with adding such a restriction if it fits the tone of the GM's campaign.

## Droid Jedi

**Q:** The rules for [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") say that you can't select [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") as your class at 1st level. Given the ruling above, could a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") Multiclass and take levels in [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") after 1st level?

**A:** Yes. In fact, in *Shadows of the Empire*, [Darth Vader](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Darth_Vader "Darth Vader") had some customized [ASP Labor Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/ASP_Labor_Droids "ASP Labor Droids") that were modified to serve as [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")-sparring partners. Such [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") could be represented in the game by having at least some levels in the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class to pick up [Lightsaber Combat Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Combat_Talents "Lightsaber Combat Talents").

Welcome to the 107th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Low Number of Attacks

**Q:** It seems as if the number of attacks available to most characters is very low, especially for [Gunslingers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gunslingers "Gunslingers") or other heroes who charge into battle with blasters blazing. In the *Star Wars* movies, most characters seem to fire multiple shots per second, but in *Saga Edition*, most characters make only one shot every six seconds. Even a high level [Gunslinger](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gunslinger "Gunslinger") using [Triple Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Attack "Triple Attack"), [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot"), and two [Blaster Pistols](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistols "Blaster Pistols") is really making only eight shots (one every 0.75 seconds), which still seems to fall short of the cinematic pace seen in the movies. Was this intentional?

**A:** This is one of those places where you have to separate the cinematic description of a blaster fight from the more abstract game mechanics used to resolve it. For example, in a melee fight, each combatant is assumed to be making multiple swings over the course of a round even though each rolls only one attack. Similarly, two gunslingers in a blaster fight might be pulling the trigger two to three times per second even though they roll only one attack (or more, depending on their [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), and so on).

This might seem straightforward enough, but many players are thrown off by the fact that ranged weapons such as blasters usually have a limited number of shots of ammunition. If the gunslingers are "really" pulling the trigger so many times, why don't they use extra shots?

The answer is that you should think of a ranged weapon's ammunition count as representing the number of attacks it allows before reloading, not the number of times a character physically pulls the trigger. In other words, it's an abstract game mechanic that applies to another abstract game mechanic, not the cinematic interpretation of the fight itself. Either way, the net amount of power used is the same whether you make a flurry of low-powered shots or a single concentrated blast, whichever is appropriate for that character's style and the weapon's design.

Thus, a sniper like the one described earlier would almost certainly make carefully aimed single shots wherein each blast is at full strength. On the other hand, an infantry soldier might make short, controlled bursts with their [Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Rifle "Blaster Rifle") on a single trigger pull. A gunslinger might fill the air with multiple shots as they repeatedly pulls the trigger over a few seconds. You should always feel free to describe your character's style of attack in whatever way seems most appropriate, cinematic, and fun.

## Rapid Strike and Whirlwind Attack

**Q:** Reading the rules, it seems that you can use [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike") in conjunction with [Whirlwind Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Whirlwind_Attack "Whirlwind Attack"), gaining an extra die of damage against all targets. Is this correct?

**A:** No, they don't work together, nor will [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") and [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire"). The text of both [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike") and [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") mention that they work with a single attack roll against a single target. [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") (such as [Whirlwind Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Whirlwind_Attack "Whirlwind Attack")) deal with multiple targets, so they are not eligible.

## Far Shot, Accurate Weapon, and Targeting Scope

**Q:** Let's say I have a sniper with the [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") Feat who [Aims](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aims "Aims") before making a single attack with an [Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate") weapon that has a [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope"). Do any of these effects stack with one another?

**A:** [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot"), [Aiming](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aiming "Aiming") with a [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope"), and using an [Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate") weapon all stack, providing progressively better attacks against distant targets. I can see how the precise interaction might be a bit confusing, so here are the [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") penalties (Point-Blank, Short, Medium, and Long) for each combination:

**Non-[Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate") weapon (such as a [Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Rifle "Blaster Rifle")):**

- With no other modifiers: Point-Blank +0, Short -2, Medium -5, Long -10
- With [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") OR [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") with a [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope"): Point-Blank +0, Short +0, Medium -2, Long -5
- With [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") AND [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") with a [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope"): Point-Blank +0, Short +0, Medium +0, Long -2

**[Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate") weapon (such as a [Sporting Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sporting_Blaster_Rifle "Sporting Blaster Rifle")):**

- With no other modifiers: Point-Blank +0, Short +0, Medium -5, Long -10
- With [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") OR [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") with a [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope"): Point-Blank +0, Short +0, Medium +0, Long -5
- With [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") AND [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") with a [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope"): Point-Blank +0, Short +0, Medium +0, Long +0

Thus, if a trained sniper with the [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") Feat uses a good sniping weapon (anything with the [Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate") quality plus a mounted [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope")) and takes the time to [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim"), that sniper can attack a target at Long [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") without taking a penalty on the attack roll.

## 6-Square Cone

**Q:** What is the exact area of a 6-Square [Cone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cone "Cone") on the battle grid? There's no illustration in the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")*, so I'm not sure how to handle a [Cone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cone "Cone") that doesn't move in a straight line along a row or column of squares.

**A:** For the sake of consistency, a [Cone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cone "Cone") has the same shape and area that it did in the revised rules and in *Dungeons & Dragons*. The [Cone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cone "Cone") starts at an intersection on the battle grid (one of the corners of your space) and then extends outward for 6 squares in a 90-degree arc. Unlike normal [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") calculations, however, count diagonals using the old method- that is, 1 square for the first and all odd diagonals, and 2 squares for the second and all even diagonals. The reason for this is that we assume that many players used the revised rules, and quite a few use the existing [Cone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cone "Cone") templates (including some snazzy wire-frame templates made for use with D&D and other d20 products). We don't want to reinvent the wheel, or the [Cone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cone "Cone"), unless we have to.

Unfortunately, we didn't have room in the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* to provide illustrations for the various shapes and sizes of different area effects. Instead, we described it with a simpler shorthand (6 squares long and 6 squares wide at the base), which is accurate enough for most situations.

For ease of reference, the precise shape of a 6-Square [Cone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cone "Cone") will be included in the upcoming Gamemaster's Screen. Until then, you can refer to the illustration below.

[![](//static.wikitide.net/swsewiki/7/74/Cone.webp)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/File:Cone.webp)

Welcome to the 108th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Attacking and Readying

**Q:** Can you make an [Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack "Attack") ([Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action")) and then [Ready](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready "Ready") a [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action") in the same turn? I can't find any rule that says you can't, but older versions of the game wouldn't allow this.

**A:** No, it's not allowed. [Readying an Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readying_an_Action "Readying an Action") is itself a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") regardless of the type of [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") readied ([Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action"), [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action"), or [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action")). This is apparently missing from the text, so it will be added to the errata.

## Reacting to Readied Actions

**Q:** If you [Ready an Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready_an_Action "Ready an Action") that, when executed, makes it impossible for another character to finish its [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action"), what happens? Is the [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") lost, or does that character get to change [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions")? For example, if I [Ready](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready "Ready") a [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") to be triggered when an enemy makes a [Melee Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attack "Melee Attack"), would that enemy lose its [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action")?

**A:** If something occurs that renders an [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") impossible or moot, that [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") is lost and can't be replaced by another [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action"). This occurs in any such circumstance, such as a Readied [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") as you describe, an [Attack of Opportunity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack_of_Opportunity "Attack of Opportunity") that renders an opponent unable to finish a move or attack, a Readied [Move](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move "Move") to get behind [Total Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Total_Cover "Total Cover") in response to a [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack"), a [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") or [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") used as a [Reaction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reaction "Reaction") that nullifies an attack, and so forth.

**Q:** Isn't that unfair? In the case of a melee opponent, I could keep that opponent from ever attacking by simply using a Readied [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") over and over again.

**A:** On its surface, sure, it might seem unfair or even a little wonky, but careful consideration of what the rules represent can put this into perspective.

First, consider that time isn't broken up into neat little rounds in real life (or on screen). Really, the game uses a turn-based system just to keep the flow of time manageable and orderly during a chaotic fight. Your character's [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action"), then, isn't occurring instantaneously once every six seconds- it's evolving continuously over the round even though it is only resolved on your turn. Given this, when a character takes an [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") (for example, a [Melee Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attack "Melee Attack")), there has probably been some build-up to enable that action. The lead time might be as little as a fraction of a second (such as with some [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions"), for example), but others might take several seconds to unfold (such as with many [Standard Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Actions "Standard Actions") and [Full-Round Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full-Round_Actions "Full-Round Actions")).

Logically, if something stops you from completing your [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action"), you wouldn't have time to go back and start a completely different [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action"). In the case of a [Melee Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attack "Melee Attack"), the attacker has probably been thrusting, parrying, and slashing at his target for at least a second or two as he tries to find (or create) an opening. This rather long action is resolved as a single attack roll, and if it's "spoiled," you don't have time to set up something else.

Even a more discrete attack- such as that at the end of a [Charge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charge "Charge")- has its own problems if the target backs up or sidesteps out of harm's way. Once you begin that final swing, stab, or thrust, you're throwing all your weight into the blow. You can't "unswing" and conserve all the momentum you've been building to make the attack somewhere else. (If you've ever played baseball, consider how hard it is to "check" a swing when you realize a pitch is no good. Now, imagine trying to check a swing and then swing again a split second later!)

Second, it might seem unfair that a melee character can never make an attack, but remember that a defender who repeatedly uses Readied [Withdraws](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraws "Withdraws") *also* can't attack. Their [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") is already committed to the [Readied Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_Action "Readied Action"). Therefore, there is no net gain for the Readied [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw"), or for any number of [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") that can "spoil" another character's [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action").

Third, the end result is perfectly balanced in that it doesn't alter the outcome of the encounter in any way. After all, if you want to avoid a melee fighter, you usually can run away just as easily (using the [Run](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Run "Run") Action or two [Move](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move "Move") Actions to keep your distance), so nothing is actually changing other than your [Initiative Count](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative_Count "Initiative Count").

**Q:** Okay, but even with this explanation, it still seems wasteful and annoying to spend a round doing nothing because your opponent [Readied an Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_an_Action "Readied an Action") instead of running away. Is there any way around this?

**A:** Yes, there is. As described above, [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") aren't instantaneous- they evolve over the course of the round, and this applies to [Readied Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_Actions "Readied Actions") as well. For example, if you [Ready an Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready_an_Action "Ready an Action") to [Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack "Attack") a target with your [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol"), you'll be aiming at that target, finger on the trigger, from the moment you [Ready](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready "Ready") the [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action"). Likewise, a Readied [Melee Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attack "Melee Attack") would have your weapon primed for a quick strike (again, think of a baseball player prepared for a pitch), a [Readied Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_Action "Readied Action") to draw a blaster would have your fingers twitching right next to your holster (think of the classic showdown at the climax of a western), and a Readied [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") would have you bouncing on the balls of your feet, prepared to leap out of harm's way.

However, the description of the [Ready](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready "Ready") Action doesn't say anything about your [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") being hidden or unknown to others around you. Thus, other characters can almost always tell that you've [Readied an Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_an_Action "Readied an Action") and even what [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") you've [Readied](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied "Readied") (though they won't know the condition that will trigger it, of course). There might be some exceptions for [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") that have no obvious visible component, such as certain [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") or uses of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"), but these should be adjudicated by the Gamemaster on a case-by-case basis.

Given this, it's not very easy to "spoil" an [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") in the way you describe. If you [Ready](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready "Ready") a [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") to avoid a melee fighter, the fighter can tell that you've [Readied](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied "Readied") a [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw"), and they'll probably conclude that you're trying to lure them in only to scamper out of range. Therefore, rather than approach you, they'll probably choose a different [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action"). In fact, they might [Ready](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ready "Ready") a [Charge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charge "Charge") that triggers the next time you take any [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") at all, turning the tables on you!

Welcome to the 109th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Limits on Free Actions

**Q:** I have a question about the Force Technique [Improved Sense Surroundings](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Sense_Surroundings "Improved Sense Surroundings"). Its description says that you can "use the [Sense Surroundings](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sense_Surroundings "Sense Surroundings") application of the [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") Skill as a [Free Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Action "Free Action") rather than a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action")." Since there is no specific limit on how many [Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions") you can take (or when you can take them), it seems that you could make this [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check over and over again, as many times as you wanted. Because you could theoretically reroll until you rolled a Natural 20, this would let you automatically maximize your roll every time and recover all your spent [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") on top of that!

Would this trick actually work, and if so, shouldn't this be errata?

**A:** No, it wouldn't work.

First, there's the "unlimited [Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions")" part. While the rules don't explicitly spell out a specific number you can take in a round, they do make it clear that [Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions") aren't without limits: "The GM puts reasonable limits on what counts as a [Free Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Action "Free Action")." It's not that [Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions") don't require any time or effort; rather, they require so little that it's generally not worth counting. If you do enough of them, they can add up to a significant amount of time such that it is no longer reasonable to continue calling them [Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions").

For example, saying a single word or a short phrase (such as calling out to your friends for help) is a [Free Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Action "Free Action"), but saying a long sentence or more (such as reciting the epic history of the [Rodian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rodian "Rodian") hunter clans) is not. Could someone then claim to perform 10,000 one-word-long [Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions") that, in succession, tell the epic history of the [Rodian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rodian "Rodian") hunter clans? Of course not- this sequence of "[Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions")" just simulates a bunch of [Full-Round Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full-Round_Actions "Full-Round Actions"), so the GM is allowed (and expected) to disallow it.

Second, all of the above is actually moot because [Sense Surroundings](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sense_Surroundings "Sense Surroundings") only comes into play "when making [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") checks to detect or observe targets." In other words, these two [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") are directly linked such that you make the [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check immediately before making the [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check. You don't get to make multiple [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") checks, just as you wouldn't get to make multiple [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") checks to detect a hidden sniper.

Clearly, [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") checks aren't unlimited. Unless the GM calls for a [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check as a [Reaction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reaction "Reaction") (such as when a new enemy comes into your [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") or to oppose a [Stealth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stealth "Stealth") check), you have to spend a higher-value action to initiate a [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check (such as a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") to actively search for enemies not in your [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") or a [Full-Round Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full-Round_Action "Full-Round Action") to search a square).

### Variant Rule: Free Actions Aren't Free

Each round, a character can perform a number of [Free Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Actions "Free Actions") equal to 1 plus their [Charisma](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charisma "Charisma") modifier or [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") modifier (whichever is more), with a minimum of one [Free Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Action "Free Action") allowed each round. This encourages characters with a higher [Charisma](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charisma "Charisma") or [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence")- the leaders and planners- to coordinate the group even when they don't have any [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") that can come into play. Conversely, it encourages the heavy-hitters to "shut up and shoot" instead of trying to overplan their [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions"), thereby keeping the tempo of the fight moving along.

## Quick Draw and Lightsabers

**Q:** If a Jedi had the [Quick Draw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Quick_Draw "Quick Draw") Feat and wanted to draw their [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"), would they still have to use another [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") to activate the [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")?

**A:** No. The "[Draw a Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Draw_a_Weapon "Draw a Weapon")" Action normally includes everything necessary to render that weapon immediately usable, including deactivating the safety on a [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol") and activating a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") or [Vibroblade](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibroblade "Vibroblade"). Weapons are designed to be drawn and armed very quickly, so the GM generally shouldn't require a separate [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") unless there's some unusual difficulty in play (such as when the wielder isn't proficient with the weapon). Similarly, you don't have to spend a separate [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action") to deactivate or disarm a weapon when you holster it.

However, it is important to note that this applies only to the actual "[Draw or Holster a Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Draw_or_Holster_a_Weapon "Draw or Holster a Weapon")" Action (that is, moving the weapon either to or from a holster, hanger, sling, and so on). It does not apply when picking up or dropping a weapon, nor does it apply when using the [Stealth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stealth "Stealth") Skill to use [Sleight of Hand](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sleight_of_Hand "Sleight of Hand") to get the weapon in your hand without attracting attention. In these cases, you're not getting the weapon in your hand as efficiently as you would when drawing or holstering it.

## Serenity and Vehicle Weapons

**Q:** Can you use [Serenity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Serenity "Serenity") to get an automatic Natural 20 when using a [Vehicle Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapon "Vehicle Weapon")? If so, doesn't this make it devastatingly good?

**A:** This isn't clear in the text, but the answer is no.

[Serenity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Serenity "Serenity") says that "your first attack roll... made in the following round is considered to be a Natural 20." This use of "your" was intentional, an artifact left over from earlier phrasing that made this point more clear: any reference to "*your* attack roll" means that it applies only to a personal, hand-held weapon, but a reference to "*an* attack" (or attack roll) is not limited this way.

As described in the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")*, "Your attack roll with a ranged weapon is 1d20 + [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") + [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") modifier + [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") penalty (if any)." However, later, it says that "An attack roll with a [Vehicle Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapon "Vehicle Weapon") is... 1d20 + [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") + Vehicle's [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") modifier + [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") modifier." This difference in phrasing was intentional and meant to show that two different processes were occurring. Since an [Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack "Attack") with a [Vehicle Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapon "Vehicle Weapon") is never made as "1d20 + [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") + [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") modifier + [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") penalty," it is never technically "your attack roll" (first or otherwise), and thus it can't benefit from [Serenity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Serenity "Serenity"). (The Force Power *[Battle Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Battle_Strike "Battle Strike")* is another example of this.)

Originally, this was part of the justification for why Luke had to turn off his targeting computer to use [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") and blow up [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"). With the targeting computer off, the [Proton Torpedoes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Proton_Torpedoes "Proton Torpedoes") basically became improvised ranged weapons, and he was limited to a very short-range shot (within the same [Starship Scale](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Scale "Starship Scale") square) due to the lack of computer assistance. However, Luke could use his personal [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack") bonus and thus qualify for using a [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") (or even *[Battle Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Battle_Strike "Battle Strike")*, which we originally considered making his first [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power")).

Obviously, this explicit distinction was lost while condensing everything to fit in the available page count, but the remnants of it ("your attack roll") are still there.

***(See Jedi Consoling 110 "Your Attack" for further clarification.)***

Welcome to the 110th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## "Your Attack"

**Q:** I have a question about your ruling about using [Serenity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Serenity "Serenity") in [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") in Jedi Counseling 109. Is the "your attack" wording supposed to apply in every case? If so, wouldn't that make some [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") no longer work with [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles")?

**A:** First, let me apologize for the way I wrote that answer because I made the issue far more confusing than it needed to be. I started by answering the question- "The answer isn't clear in the text, but the answer is no"- and I should have stopped at that point.

Instead, I went into the background of how things were in an earlier draft and what this "your attack" wording was originally supposed to mean. In other words, everything after that first sentence is supposed to explain how things got off track, but discussing the original wording isn't particularly helpful since we'd moved so far from it elsewhere. I further compounded the error by forgetting other cases where this wording would have applied but which had already been clarified to work (such as [Careful Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Careful_Shot "Careful Shot") and [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") in *[Starships of the Galaxy](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships_of_the_Galaxy "Starships of the Galaxy")*).

Originally, I didn't want to call it "errata" because I knew that the actual wording of such errata hadn't been determined for any future printings of the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")*. I didn't want to confuse the issue by having errata that might be the subject of later errata. In retrospect, that would have been far less confusing. Given this, let's completely retract that clarification. Instead, we'll just call this errata:

*When using a [Vehicle Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapon "Vehicle Weapon"), you cannot apply any effect that causes an automatic Natural 20 or automatic [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit") (such as spending a [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") or using [Serenity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Serenity "Serenity")), unless a rule specifies that it can be used with [Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons").*

We still might rephrase this a bit, but the meaning shouldn't change substantially.

## Dark Side Temptations

**Q:** Is there any real reason for a character to be tempted to go to [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side")? It doesn't seem as if it would happen other than by deliberate choice on the part of the player. Even if they get close to falling, they could always stop when their [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") is 1 less than their [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom").

**A:** There are several ways that you might gain a benefit by doing something that increases your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score"):

- You might gain a situational benefit from performing an evil act. For example, you might be tempted to kill an unconscious or captive enemy to avoid the complications of having a prisoner in tow or the dangers of leaving that enemy unattended.
- You might learn and use [*[Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side "Dark Side")*] Force Powers.
- You might have the [Corruption](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Corruption "Corruption") Destiny, gaining the listed [Destiny](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny "Destiny") bonus every time your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") increases. (This gives you more of a reason to do both of the above.)
- You might face a [Sith Lord](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Lord "Sith Lord") who uses the [Temptation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Temptation "Temptation") class feature. In this case, you might choose to accept an increase to your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") instead of moving -1 step on the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track").

### Variant Rule: Calling on the Dark Side

All of these are tangible benefits, but I agree that many of these require prior planning, a deliberate effort to make the "wrong" choice, or the intervention of an enemy. If you want a game mechanic that provides a more spontaneous tangible benefit, I would suggest the following variant rule.

If your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") is less than your [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom"), you can call on [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") to gain a temporary [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point"). If the [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") is not used before the end of the encounter, it is lost. Calling on [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") is a [Major Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Major_Transgression "Major Transgression"). Only heroic characters can call on [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side").

### Variant Rule: The Measure of Evil

If you want to add the element of chance so that turning to [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") might come as a surprise, you could also use the following variant rule.

When you commit a [Dark Side Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Transgression "Dark Side Transgression"), your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") increases by a random amount: 1d6-3 for [Major Transgressions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Major_Transgressions "Major Transgressions"), 1d6-4 for [Moderate Transgressions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Moderate_Transgressions "Moderate Transgressions"), and 1d6-5 for [Minor Transgressions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Minor_Transgressions "Minor Transgressions"). (This never reduces your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score"), even if the result is less than zero.)

### Variant Rule: The Threshold of Evil

If you want to add the element of chance so that turning to [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") might come as a surprise, you could also use the following variant rule.

When your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") increases, roll 2d4 and add your new [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score"). If the result exceeds your [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom") by 5 or more points, your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") increases to your [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom") score, and you turn to [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side").

## Recovering Temporary Force Points

**Q:** If you gain a temporary [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") (such as with the **[Fringe Savant](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fringe_Savant "Fringe Savant")** Talent) and you also have the [Force Point Recovery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point_Recovery "Force Point Recovery") Force Technique, can you gain a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") by "recovering" the temporary [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") that you spent?

**A:** No. Temporary [Force Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Points "Force Points") are just that- temporary- and they cannot be recovered by any means after the end of the encounter.

Welcome to the 111th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Atonement and Force Points

**Q:** [Atonement](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atonement "Atonement") seems too easy. It's common to have [Force Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Points "Force Points") left over upon gaining a level. Since they would be lost anyway, couldn't you spend them on atonement, effectively reducing your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") for no real cost?

**A:** [Atonement](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atonement "Atonement") isn't quite that simple. It requires "a period of meditation, reflection, and absolution" on the part of the character, so it isn't a quick or instantaneous process. First, the player declares that his character is attempting to atone, spending 1 [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") as a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"); this represents the character pausing as they realize that they are walking down the wrong path.

Next, the Gamemaster decides how to adjudicate the attempt to atone, such as how long it will take, whether any special acts of contrition will be required, and so forth. If performed "on stage," it should span at least the length of an adventure (during which the hero is essentially a total saint). If performed between adventures, it will normally require at least a week and possibly as long as months (even years!) of sincere reflection. The precise time and effort required should usually reflect the character's transgressions, so someone with a very high [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") may have to meet a higher standard to prove that the [Atonement](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atonement "Atonement") is sincere.

The GM also adjudicates when the attempt is successful. If the hero's actions don't reflect a sincere desire to atone, their [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") won't be reduced. Still, the details are up to the GM, and [Atonement](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atonement "Atonement") should never interfere with the needs of the campaign.

One last detail here: You can atone only 1 point at a time. This means that you can't spend all your leftover [Force Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Points "Force Points") just before you gain a level. You could start a period of [Atonement](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atonement "Atonement"), certainly, but it wouldn't end for quite some time (as described above).

## Atonement and Force Point Recovery

**Q:** If you have [Force Point Recovery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point_Recovery "Force Point Recovery") and you atone to reduce your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score"), do you get the [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") back?

**A:** No. [Force Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Points "Force Points") that you sacrifice for [Atonement](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atonement "Atonement") are permanently lost and cannot be recovered by any means.

## Transgression Severity

**Q:** I'm having some problems deciding what type of [Dark Side Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Transgression "Dark Side Transgression") some actions should be. How can you tell when something is blatantly evil (a [Major Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Major_Transgression "Major Transgression")), questionably evil (a [Moderate Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Moderate_Transgression "Moderate Transgression")), or dubiously evil (a [Minor Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Minor_Transgression "Minor Transgression"))?

**A:** While the precise divisions are ultimately up to the GM, enforcing them as loosely or tightly as appropriate for the campaign, here are some guidelines to help you decide on corner cases. To decide if an action is "blatantly evil," ask yourself the following questions:

- Did the action harm a sentient, living character?
- Did the action harm a character that was at your mercy ([Helpless](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Helpless "Helpless"), unable to meaningfully defend itself, or something similar)?
- Did the action cause serious harm to a character (physically, mentally, or emotionally)?
- Was the action unnecessary to protect yourself or another character from an immediate, obvious threat?
- Was the action deliberate and the result intended?

If the answer to all of these questions is "Yes," then the action is almost certainly "blatantly evil" (a [Major Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Major_Transgression "Major Transgression")) and deserves increasing the character's [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score"). Examples include murder, torture (including acts such as intentional mutilation or maiming), slavery, rape, and any similar act considered reprehensible by most civilizations.

If exactly one question can be answered "No," then the action is probably "questionably evil" (a [Moderate Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Moderate_Transgression "Moderate Transgression")), which might be worth increasing the character's [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score"). Examples include assassinating an Imperial warlord who is about to give orders to exterminate the population of a planet (it's in defense of others, but he's still at your mercy), tormenting a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") to extract information, or starting an unnecessary fight that results in serious injury.

If exactly two of the questions can be answered "No," then the action is probably "dubiously evil" (a [Minor Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Minor_Transgression "Minor Transgression")) and likely doesn't deserve increasing the character's [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score"). Examples include killing in self-defense or recklessly (but accidentally) causing injury to others.

If three or more questions can be answered "No," then the action probably is not a [Dark Side Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Transgression "Dark Side Transgression") at all. For example, destroying an object is not a transgression unless doing so also causes some sort of substantial harm to a character. Similarly, hunting an animal (not sentient) in the wilderness (not [Helpless](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Helpless "Helpless")) for sustenance (protecting yourself from starvation) is perfectly acceptable in all but the most unusual circumstances.

Obviously, you'll still need to interpret the situation accordingly. How strictly do you want to define "harm"? For example, is a rich character harmed in a significant way by having a minor treasure stolen? Is a threat imminent and real, or is the character merely trying to justify unnecessary aggression? The purpose of these questions isn't to give you a never-fail method but to help you evaluate an action. When in doubt, go with your gut instinct. The more you have to parse and explain an action to make it acceptable, the more likely it is to be a [Dark Side Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Transgression "Dark Side Transgression").

## Severing Strike and the Dark Side

**Q:** Would **[Severing Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Severing_Strike "Severing Strike")** be a [Major Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Major_Transgression "Major Transgression")? It seems that you'd never learn this [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") unless you intended to maim others.

**A:** No, **[Severing Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Severing_Strike "Severing Strike")**, in itself, is not a [Dark Side Transgression](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Transgression "Dark Side Transgression") because it is designed to protect your target from an otherwise lethal blow. The same would be true for a doctor who has to perform a medical amputation. Although the act is "maiming" in the strictest sense, it is only to protect the target from a more dangerous medical problem.

This doesn't mean that **[Severing Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Severing_Strike "Severing Strike")** can't be used in a blatantly evil way, of course. Cutting off the limbs of an enemy who is at your mercy would still be tantamount to torture no matter how you do it (by [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"), scalpel, or angry [Wookiee](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiee "Wookiee")).

## Moving a Door

**Q:** What happens if you use *[Move Object](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Object "Move Object")* against a door? How about *[Force Thrust](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Thrust "Force Thrust")*?

**A:** If you use *[Move Object](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Object "Move Object")* against a door, you deal normal damage to it as the door strains against its frame. (Don't forget to apply the effects of the door's [Damage Reduction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Reduction "Damage Reduction").) If the damage is sufficient to reduce the door to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), it becomes [Disabled](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Disabled "Disabled") and the door is pulled free, making the doorway passable.

If you use *[Force Thrust](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Thrust "Force Thrust")* against a door, compare your [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check result to the door's [Break DC](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Break_DC "Break DC"). If successful, you have forced the door open. If not, the door remains intact and functional.

For those who are interested, an object's [Break DC](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Break_DC "Break DC") is calculated as if it were a character resisting a [Bantha Rush](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bantha_Rush "Bantha Rush") or *[Force Thrust](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Thrust "Force Thrust")*. Take its [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") bonus, add 10 (the average result on a roll of 1d20, rounded down), and- in most cases- add 5 more for being exceptionally stable. (Some particularly fragile objects may deviate on this last detail, but that is fairly rare.)

Welcome to the 112th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Unarmed Weapon Group

**Q:** What [Weapon Group](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Group "Weapon Group") includes [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks")? How about Natural Weapons? In most places, it seems like they're [Simple Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Simple_Weapons "Simple Weapons"), but [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus") refers to [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") as if they are separate.

**A:** [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") and Natural Weapons are considered [Simple Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Simple_Weapons "Simple Weapons") in which all characters are proficient. The reference to [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") in the [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus") Feat is an error.

## Unarmed Attacks and Weapon Finesse

**Q:** Do [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") and Natural Weapons benefit from the [Weapon Finesse](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Finesse "Weapon Finesse") Feat?

**A:** Yes. [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") and Natural Weapons are [Light Melee Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Light_Melee_Weapons "Light Melee Weapons").

## Combat Gloves Weapon Group

**Q:** Do [Combat Gloves](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Combat_Gloves "Combat Gloves") count as [Simple Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Simple_Weapons "Simple Weapons") or [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks")?

**A:** Both. They count as [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks"), and [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") are [Simple Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Simple_Weapons "Simple Weapons").

## Weapon Tool Mounts

**Q:** A Droid's [Tool Mounts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tool_Mounts "Tool Mounts")- including weapon mounts- are one type of [Appendage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Appendage "Appendage"), and [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") can use their [Appendages](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Appendages "Appendages") to make [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks"). Since characters are automatically proficient with [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks"), would that mean that a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is automatically proficient with any weapon on a [Tool Mount](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tool_Mount "Tool Mount")?

**A:** No. While the weapon mount can be used for [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") to bludgeon nearby foes, using the weapon itself for its intended purpose still requires proficiency to avoid the usual penalties.

For example, if an R2 unit had a [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol") installed on one of its [Tool Mounts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tool_Mounts "Tool Mounts"), it could make [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks") at no penalty, but it would take a -5 penalty on [Ranged Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attacks "Ranged Attacks") with the [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol"). (A [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") without a [Heuristic Processor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heuristic_Processor "Heuristic Processor") wouldn't be able to use the weapon without proficiency at all.)

## Weapon Familiarity with Feats and Talents

**Q:** How does the Weapon Familiarity Species Trait interact with weapon-specific [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), such as [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus")? Does a [Wookiee](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiee "Wookiee") with [Weapon Focus (Rifles)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus_(Rifles) "Weapon Focus (Rifles)") apply the bonus to [Bowcasters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bowcasters "Bowcasters"), or would that be covered by [Weapon Focus (Bowcaster)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus_(Bowcaster) "Weapon Focus (Bowcaster)")?

**A:** This isn't clear in the original text, but Weapon Familiarity allows a character to treat that weapon as a member of the indicated [Weapon Group](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Group "Weapon Group") for all purposes, not just proficiency. For example, if a [Wookiee](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiee "Wookiee") Soldier had [Weapon Focus (Rifles)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus_(Rifles) "Weapon Focus (Rifles)") and **[Weapon Specialization (Rifles)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Specialization_(Rifles) "Weapon Specialization (Rifles)")**, they would get +1 on attack rolls and +2 on damage rolls with [Rifles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rifles "Rifles") and [Bowcasters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bowcasters "Bowcasters").

## Weapon Focus "Proficiency"

**Q:** [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus") (and many other weapon-specific abilities) lists "proficient with weapon" among its prerequisites. Can you take that [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") if you're proficient with at least one weapon in that group, or do you have to be proficient with all weapons in that group?

**A:** As long as you are proficient with at least one weapon in a group, you are qualified to take [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus") (or any other weapon-specific ability) for that group. The benefits apply only for weapons within that group with which you are proficient.

For example, a [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") with the **[Spacehound](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Spacehound "Spacehound")** Talent is proficient with all [Starship Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Weapons "Starship Weapons") (all of which are [Heavy Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Weapons "Heavy Weapons")), so they would qualify to take [Weapon Focus (Heavy Weapons)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus_(Heavy_Weapons) "Weapon Focus (Heavy Weapons)"). They would gain a +1 bonus on attacks with [Starship Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Weapons "Starship Weapons"), but they wouldn't get that benefit with an [E-Web Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/E-Web_Repeating_Blaster "E-Web Repeating Blaster") or any other [Heavy Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Weapon "Heavy Weapon") until becoming proficient with them.

**Q:** Does that reasoning apply when a prerequisite says "Weapon Proficiency ([Weapon Group](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Group "Weapon Group"))"?

**A:** No. When the [Weapon Proficiency](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Proficiency "Weapon Proficiency") Feat is specified, you must actually have that [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat"). That said, the [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire") Feat needs a bit of errata to work as intended with this ruling:

*Replace prerequisites with the following: "Proficient with [Heavy Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Weapons "Heavy Weapons"), proficient with weapon."*

*Add the following sentence to the Special section of the [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat"): "If you do not have a [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") of 13 or higher, increase the penalty on attacks to -10 when using this Feat with non-[Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons")."*

This change allows pilots and other [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle")-oriented characters to use [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire") more easily. For example, an [X-Wing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/X-Wing "X-Wing") pilot ([Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") 1) with [Vehicular Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicular_Combat "Vehicular Combat") and [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") 10 could take [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire") to get the most out of the Starfighter's [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") capabilities, but this ability wouldn't transfer to a [Heavy Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Repeating_Blaster "Heavy Repeating Blaster") (they're not proficient with it). They could use [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire") with a [Blaster Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Rifle "Blaster Rifle"), of course, but they would take a -10 penalty on such attacks due to not meeting the [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") requirements listed above.

**Q:** Wouldn't that make [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire") work very differently than [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot")?

**A:** Both [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") and [Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike") are receiving some matching errata to make these [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") more accessible to a wider variety of characters while still preserving the concept and game balance intended by the original prerequisites:

*[Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot"): Remove the [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") prerequisite from the [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat").*

*Add the following sentence to the Special section of the [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat"): "If you do not have a [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") of 13 or higher, increase the penalty on attacks to -5 when using this Feat with non-[Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons")."*

*[Rapid Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Strike "Rapid Strike"): Remove the [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") prerequisite from the [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat").*

*Add the following sentence to the Special section of the [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat"): "If you do not have a [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") of 13 or higher, increase the penalty on attacks to -5 when using this Feat with non-[Light Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Light_Weapons "Light Weapons")."*

Welcome to the 113th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Mind Trick Limits

**Q:** This question is about the "make an otherwise unpalatable suggestion seem completely reasonable" aspect of *[Mind Trick](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mind_Trick "Mind Trick")*. If the heroes are facing multiple enemies, can a Force-user use *[Mind Trick](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mind_Trick "Mind Trick")* to cause one of the enemies to attack another enemy?

**A:** One of the limitations of using *[Mind Trick](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mind_Trick "Mind Trick")* to make a suggestion is that it can't obviously endanger the target's life. For this reason, telling an enemy to attack another enemy usually won't work. For example, a [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") who turns around and attacks other [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") would expect them to shoot back. Even if he survives, he would still expect to be court-martialed and executed for treason.

Still, this doesn't mean it's impossible to get someone to attack someone else with a carefully worded *[Mind Trick](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mind_Trick "Mind Trick")*, but it would be an indirect effect. For example, a *[Mind Trick](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mind_Trick "Mind Trick")* could plant a suggestion that would make attacking another character a logical (and perhaps safe) choice. For example, you might use *[Mind Trick](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mind_Trick "Mind Trick")* to tell a [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") the following: "That bounty hunter is actually a Rebel terrorist, and he's carrying a bomb!" This would almost certainly prompt a [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") to attempt to capture or kill the target right away.

That same suggestion wouldn't prompt a [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") to attack another [Stormtrooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtrooper "Stormtrooper") because he'd still expect to be court-martialed, but it could lead him to start investigating the "Rebel" and perhaps report his suspicions to a superior. None of that would do any good in the middle of a combat encounter, though.

## Teräs Käsi Mastery and Double Attack

**Q:** Can a character who has the **[Teräs Käsi Mastery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ter%C3%A4s_K%C3%A4si_Mastery "Teräs Käsi Mastery")** Talent and the [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") Feat combine these with the [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") Feat? Specifically, could I use two [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") to get the benefit of [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") and then use my [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") to make a [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack")? If so, would the extra die of damage from [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") apply to both attacks from the [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") or only to the first attack?

**A:** Yes, you can use **[Teräs Käsi Mastery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ter%C3%A4s_K%C3%A4si_Mastery "Teräs Käsi Mastery")**, [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack"), and [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") at the same time, using two [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") for [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") and then your [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") for an unarmed [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack").

However, [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") specifies that its extra damage applies only to your *next* attack in the same round, so the second attack from [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") (or any other source of extra attacks) would not gain this benefit.

## Trace and Hot Wire

**Q:** If my [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") takes both **[Trace](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trace "Trace")** and **[Hot Wire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hot_Wire "Hot Wire")**, can they make a [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") check in place of [Gather Information](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gather_Information "Gather Information") as long as they have access to a computer terminal that they can hot-wire?

**A:** No. **[Hot Wire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hot_Wire "Hot Wire")** works only when [Improving Access](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improving_Access "Improving Access") to a computer (that is, when you're trying to improve the computer's [Attitude](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") toward you). Making [Use Computer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") checks in place of [Gather Information](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gather_Information "Gather Information") checks is a completely separate use of the Skill, so [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") can't be substituted in that case.

## Force Disciples and Friendly Mind-Affecting Effects

**Q:** Does a Force Disciple's immunity to Mind-Affecting effects also apply to friendly effects, such as many of the [Officer Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Officer_Talents "Officer Talents")?

**A:** A [Force Disciple](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Disciple "Force Disciple") is immune to such effects, even if the effects would be helpful. Conceptually, think of [Force Disciples](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Disciples "Force Disciples") as having minds that exist on such a transcendental plane of thought that mundane influences simply don't move them enough to make a difference. That said, I don't think it would be unbalancing to allow a [Force Disciple](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Disciple "Force Disciple") to raise or lower this immunity (probably a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"), if you decide to use such a variant as a house rule).

## Exotic Lightsabers

**Q:** There are a lot of custom [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") in *Star Wars*, but my group can't decide if they all fall under the [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") Weapon Group. For example, would Lumiya's [Lightwhip](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightwhip "Lightwhip") or the [Lightsaber Tonfa](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Tonfa "Lightsaber Tonfa") used in *The Force Unleashed* video game be considered [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") or [Exotic Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Exotic_Weapons "Exotic Weapons")?

**A:** Both the [Lightwhip](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightwhip "Lightwhip") and the [Guard Shoto](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Guard_Shoto "Guard Shoto"), also known as the [Lightsaber Tonfa](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Tonfa "Lightsaber Tonfa"), are part of the [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") Weapon Group.

[Weapon Groups](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Groups "Weapon Groups") are pretty broad; for example, "[Simple Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Simple_Weapons "Simple Weapons")" includes [Nets](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nets "Nets"), [Slings](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Slings "Slings"), [Clubs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Clubs "Clubs"), [Knives](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knives "Knives"), and many others. We usually don't separate a weapon and call it exotic unless it's particularly bizarre or unusual in design. A [Flamethrower](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Flamethrower "Flamethrower"), for example, operates by shooting flaming liquid. As a result, you have to arc your shots and anticipate the liquid's splash to get the most out of the weapon, and this is very different from the simple point-and-shoot of a blaster or even a [Missile Launcher](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Missile_Launcher "Missile Launcher").

Similarly, a weapon whose design is sufficiently atypical may earn the exotic designation, and this combined with the Weapon Familiarity trait can provide a little local flavor. [Wookiees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiees "Wookiees") can use [Bowcasters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bowcasters "Bowcasters") and [Gungans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gungans "Gungans") can use [Atlatls](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atlatls "Atlatls") because they were raised around them, but offworld characters need to put in a little extra training to effectively operate these unfamiliar weapons.

## E-Web Weight
