**Q:** Is the weight on the [E-Web Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/E-Web_Repeating_Blaster "E-Web Repeating Blaster") correct? It seems awfully heavy compared to a [Heavy Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Repeating_Blaster "Heavy Repeating Blaster"), which is only one size smaller and in the same [Weapon Group](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Group "Weapon Group").

**A:** Yes, the weights are correct. Don't forget that the E-Web's weight includes a dedicated power generator, which is roughly 15 kg by itself. In addition, the [E-Web Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/E-Web_Repeating_Blaster "E-Web Repeating Blaster") uses a very large and heavy tripod to assist its crew in wielding such a massive weapon, whereas the [Heavy Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Repeating_Blaster "Heavy Repeating Blaster") uses a smaller tripod that is designed to be portable (allowing you to fire on the run, albeit inaccurately).

Welcome to the 114th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our regular column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Contextual Healing

**Q:** My gaming group plays D&D 4th Edition in addition to *Saga Edition*, and one thing we've noticed is that D&D heroes are able to "get back in the fight" a little more easily than *Star Wars* heroes, thanks to having so many healing surges. Although I like the realism of heroes being unable to instantly bounce back from terrible injuries, it can interfere with the flow of the adventure if the heroes have to stop and rest.

On the flip side, if a group has two Force-using heroes and both have *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")*, they can spring back to full strength in a matter of minutes. Because of this difference in between-encounter healing, I find that an adventure with perfect pacing for one group might be far too fast (or too slow) for another. Any suggestions?

**A:** Let's look at the current situation to see how it matches the needs of your game. First, any group of heroes can get back into the fight at least once during the day. With each getting the benefits of [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") and almost everyone having access to a [Medpac](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medpac "Medpac"), they can get back into decent shape pretty quickly even in the middle of a fight. The problem comes up, then, if the heroes get into a series of fights (or a few big fights) that deal damage far beyond the amount that can be healed easily.

In that case, there are two other options in the existing rules: [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery") and [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"). [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery") is pretty slow, requiring 1 hour normally (and still requiring 10 minutes with [Surgical Expertise](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgical_Expertise "Surgical Expertise")). On top of that, it can be risky if you don't have a very skilled surgeon, and you have to keep a [Surgery Kit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery_Kit "Surgery Kit") handy. Still, there's no limit to how often you can attempt it, so there's a good chance you'll get the heroes back into shape as long as you have a few hours between encounters.

If you don't have that much time (or if you don't have someone with a high [Treat Injury](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Treat_Injury "Treat Injury") modifier), the other existing option is to use a [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power"), most often *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")*. Not only can this be used in the middle of a fight, but you can recharge the power repeatedly simply by resting for 1 minute. The downside is that a single Force-user will run out of juice pretty fast, since he or she takes damage equal to half that healed (or use [Force Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Points "Force Points") to avoid the damage, but those are limited). Don't forget that you can't use *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")* on yourself, per the latest errata and clarifications.

If you have two such Force-users, though, you run into the other problem you mentioned. Since they heal damage faster than they take it, they can heal the entire group and each other if they have a few minutes to rest.

If any of this doesn't work for your preferred gaming style, I can offer you a few suggestions. However- and I can't emphasize this enough- **don't make any changes unless you, as the GM, have run into trouble with the existing rules**. Be careful and conservative in introducing any or all of the following ideas, adding them on a trial basis (at most one or two at a time) to see how they feel for your game and your group's shared image of *Star Wars*. If anything doesn't fit right, don't hesitate to change it back!

Now, on to the suggestions: First, you might consider using the following optional rule, which also appears in *[The Gem of Alderaan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Gem_of_Alderaan "The Gem of Alderaan")*, the eighth *[Dawn of Defiance](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dawn_of_Defiance "Dawn of Defiance")* adventure. It bridges the gap between [First Aid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/First_Aid "First Aid") (quick, but only usable once per day) and [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery") (slow but usable repeatedly), providing a way to benefit from [Medpacs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medpacs "Medpacs") more than once per day, albeit with some serious risks. Your heroes might want to acquire some [FastFlesh Medpacs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/FastFlesh_Medpacs "FastFlesh Medpacs") if they plan to use this option regularly:

### Optional Rule: Old Wounds

The following is a new use of the [Treat Injury](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Treat_Injury "Treat Injury") Skill:

**Critical Care (Trained Only; requires a [Medpac](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medpac "Medpac") and [Surgery Kit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery_Kit "Surgery Kit")):** You can attempt to use multiple [Medpacs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medpacs "Medpacs") on a creature in a 24-hour period. This requires one minute, and it expends the contents of one [Medpac](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medpac "Medpac"). If you succeed on a DC 20 Treat Injury check, the creature regains a number of [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") equal to its Character Level, plus 1 [Hit Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Point "Hit Point") for every point by which your check result exceeds the DC. You take a -5 penalty to your Treat Injury check for every previous attempt at Critical Care in the last 24 hours, even if that attempt was unsuccessful.

If you fail the Treat Injury check, the creature overdoses on the medicines in the [Medpac](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medpac "Medpac"), causing it to take damage equal to its [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"). If this would reduce the creature to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), it dies unless it can spend a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") to save itself.

You can attempt to perform Critical Care on yourself, but you take a -5 penalty on your Treat Injury check.

Second, you could make [Second Winds](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Winds "Second Winds") available more often. One possibility is to import something like the healing surges used in D&D 4th edition, giving characters access to more than one [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind"). The following variant is scaled back quite a bit from its D&D counterpart to better match the flavor of *Star Wars*:

### Variant Rule: Multiple Second Winds

Instead of having one [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") per day, every heroic character can catch a [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") a number of times per day equal to 1 + (class bonus to [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense")) + ([Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") modifier), with a minimum of 1. Any ability that would add an extra [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") (such as the [Extra Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Extra_Second_Wind "Extra Second Wind") Feat or the **[Tough as Nails](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tough_as_Nails "Tough as Nails")** Talent) instead adds a number of [Second Winds](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Winds "Second Winds") equal to this base number. As always, you can catch a [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") no more than once in a single encounter.

The above variant rule can be used together with or separately from the following optional rule, which makes [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") available more often, but at a significant cost:

### Optional Rule: The Edge of Exhaustion

If you have already used your [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind")(s) for the day, you can catch another [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") by voluntarily moving -1 [Persistent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent "Persistent") step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"). The [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") remains until you've had [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery") performed on you or until you get eight consecutive, uninterrupted hours of rest.

Finally, you might scale back the benefits of *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")* so that groups with multiple Force-users aren't quite so far ahead of the rest. (This is particularly useful if you implement either of the [Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Second_Wind "Second Wind") rules above.) You might want to use either or both of the following variant rules:

### Variant Rule: Vital Sacrifice

A character that uses the *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")* Force Power to heal another character cannot benefit from the use of *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")* in the same 24-hour period.

### Variant Rule: Vital Limits

A character can benefit from *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")* only once in a 24-hour period, but you can use a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") to overcome this restriction when you activate *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")*. (Since you can spend only one [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") per round, you cannot also spend a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") to avoid taking damage from the use of *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")*.)

If you use one or more of these rules, the heroes will be a lot more resilient, bouncing back quickly between Jedi fights. Don't be afraid to increase the tempo of the adventure to keep up, but ease into it over a few sessions as you get a feel for the right pacing.

## Equilibrium and Persistent Conditions

**Q:** Does the **[Equilibrium](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equilibrium "Equilibrium")** Talent move you to the top of the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"), or does it remove [Persistent Conditions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Conditions "Persistent Conditions") (such as from [Poison](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Poison "Poison"), [Disease](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Disease "Disease"), or serious injury)?

**A:** **[Equilibrium](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equilibrium "Equilibrium")** does both of these, removing all [Persistent Conditions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Conditions "Persistent Conditions") *and* moving you to the top of the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track").

## Conditions and Damage Threshold

**Q:** [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") penalties reduce all your [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores"). Do they also reduce your [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), since it is derived from your [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense")?

**A:** Yes. Although the effect is indirect, any modifiers that change your [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") also change your [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") by an equal amount because its underlying "starting value" has changed.

Welcome to the 115th installment of "Jedi Counseling," our column in which we answer your rules questions about the *Star Wars Miniatures Game* and the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*.

## Starship Designer Limits

**Q:** I've run into some issues with the [Starship Designer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Designer "Starship Designer") rules in *[Starships of the Galaxy](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships_of_the_Galaxy "Starships of the Galaxy")*. It seems that you can't make any existing [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") using these rules and starting with a [Stock Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starship "Stock Starship"). Am I doing something wrong?

**A:** Probably not. A vast number of individual [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") have been created over the last few decades since *A New Hope* came out, and at no point was there a cohesive [Starship Designer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Designer "Starship Designer") system to guide authors of fiction or gaming products. As such, it is effectively impossible to reverse-engineer a design system that covers all these varied examples- or, at least, any such system would be so complex that it wouldn't be easy to use in a game.

Given that, we designed the system from the point of the typical hero who might be interested in modifying an existing [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") or scratch-building a new one. This hero doesn't have the benefit of vast numbers of the best engineers and test pilots in the galaxy (like at the Corellian Engineering Corporation), nor does this hero have an entire star system dedicated to the building of city-sized [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") with mass-production facilities to match (like at Kuat Drive Yards).

This hero, instead, is relegated to building "a typical new [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") from a typical production facility," as noted at the bottom of [Stock Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starships "Stock Starships"). The [Stock Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starships "Stock Starships") are designed to reflect that, making it possible to make something decent and functional, but not something that would easily outshine the big name [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships").

For example, a hero could build a decent light freighter, something comparable to a [YT-1300](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/YT-1300 "YT-1300") in stats and price.

The biggest difference? The [YT-1300](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/YT-1300 "YT-1300") would have a lot of extra [Emplacement Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Emplacement_Points "Emplacement Points") left over- and that's precisely what the Corellian Engineering Corporation is known for. Think about it: if any yahoo on a backwater planet could build a freighter that's just as good, why would the YT-series freighters sell so well throughout the galaxy?

Since most heroes won't ever control a galaxy-spanning corporate giant and be able to use these resources, it seems a little silly to spend page space exploring the ins and outs of galactic-scale mass production. Similarly, we might give broad outlines of supply and demand by listing a planet's major exports and imports, but we won't be creating rules that account for macroeconomic cycles, unemployment, inflation, and so forth. (Let's face it: most of us are just here to shoot [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers").)

Now, if you as the GM want to build [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") beyond what the [Stock Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starships "Stock Starships") can manage, there's really not a reason for you to hamstring yourself with those rules: just write up stats that seem like a good challenge for your players, using similar [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") as benchmarks for things like price, appropriate game statistics for that type of ship, and so forth.

And if you and your players really want more comprehensive rules that go beyond those in the book, by all means do so! You might look at [Gear Templates](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gear_Templates "Gear Templates") to get some inspiration for coming up with appropriate special benefits and restrictions for different manufacturers.

Alternatively, you might allow manufacturers to come up with "proprietary" [Stock Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starships "Stock Starships") that are better than the typical ones listed in the book. For example, Kuat Drive Yards might have a really good "stock [Star Destroyer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Star_Destroyer "Star Destroyer")" and Corellian Engineering Corporation might have an outstanding "stock YT-series," and they use these as the baseline for producing several similar [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") designs.

**Q:** When building a new design from a [Stock Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starship "Stock Starship"), can you remove or replace existing systems that come with the [Stock Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starship "Stock Starship")? For example, could I take those high-performance engines off a stock interceptor and use the cash to buy other systems?

**A:** You can, but it's not very efficient. You get only 1/4 the listed value of any system you remove from a [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship"), even a [Stock Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starship "Stock Starship").

[Stock Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stock_Starships "Stock Starships") usually get their starting systems at a substantial discount because they are so well integrated into the baseline design. Removing them requires a lot of inefficient workarounds, resulting in a lower price comparable to selling a used [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") component.

## Passenger Conversion

**Q:** [Passenger Conversion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Passenger_Conversion "Passenger Conversion") says that it creates room for a number of passengers equal to the ship's size modifier.

Wouldn't the size modifier be -10 for ships that big? Or does it refer to some other size modifier (such as the one used for [Grappling](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grappling "Grappling"))?

**A:** It is an error, so consider this to be errata: [Passenger Conversion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Passenger_Conversion "Passenger Conversion") has sufficient space for a number of passengers equal to the ship's [Cost Modifier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cost_Modifier "Cost Modifier"), not size modifier.

## Cargo Pods and Emplacements Points

**Q:** Can you gain [Emplacement Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Emplacement_Points "Emplacement Points") by converting the cargo space provided by a [Cargo Pod](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cargo_Pod "Cargo Pod")?

**A:** No. A [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") can only convert its own internal cargo space into [Emplacement Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Emplacement_Points "Emplacement Points").

## Hagar Space and Vehicle Storage

**Q:** Do nonflying carried [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") require [Hangar Space](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Space "Hangar Space")? If so, it seems like the
[*Imperial*-Class Star Destroyer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Imperial-Class_Star_Destroyer "Imperial-Class Star Destroyer") uses more [Hangar Space](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Space "Hangar Space") for [AT-ATs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/AT-ATs "AT-ATs") and [AT-STs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/AT-STs "AT-STs") than it does for [TIE Fighters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/TIE_Fighters "TIE Fighters") and shuttles.

**A:** [Hangar Space](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Space "Hangar Space") is required only for [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") that are meant to launch directly from the [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship"), such as [Airspeeders](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Airspeeders "Airspeeders"), [Starfighters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighters "Starfighters"), and [Space Transports](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Space_Transports "Space Transports"). If the [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") is meant to be kept in storage until deployed planetside- as in the case of [AT-ATs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/AT-ATs "AT-ATs") and the like- then the [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") can be carried as cargo. The real difference is that a hangar-deployed [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") is ready to fight when launched, but a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") carried as cargo needs a little help to become combat ready (usually about 1 minute of continuous work, assuming the Vehicle's entire crew assists).

[Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") stored in this way are still quite bulky (meaning they take up more cargo space than their mass would otherwise suggest). Nevertheless, this is considerably more efficient than allocating [Hangar Space](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Space "Hangar Space") for the [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle"), which includes enough room for maintenance, repair, takeoff, landing, and maneuvering. The precise amount of cargo space required is ultimately up to the GM, but a good baseline is that a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") requires about 100 tons of cargo space- roughly 50 cubic meters- for every unit of [Hangar Space](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Space "Hangar Space") it would normally require. (As a comparison, this is about 1/4 as much room as the same [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") would require if you converted that cargo space into a [Hangar Bay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Bay "Hangar Bay").)

## Freighters and Nonstandard Modifications

**Q:** My players have discovered that they can make very good warships by modifying freighters, turning all that extra cargo space into [Emplacement Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Emplacement_Points "Emplacement Points"). I can't find anything wrong with it in the rules, but it looks like a converted freighter could overpower a [Star Destroyer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Star_Destroyer "Star Destroyer")! Am I missing something, or is this intentional?

**A:** You are missing something. Under [Nonstandard Modifications](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonstandard_Modifications "Nonstandard Modifications"), you'll find this little catch-all rule: "The GM is the final arbiter of what systems are sufficiently dissimilar to qualify as nonstandard, and particularly unusual combinations may be forbidden altogether."

In other words, no modification is legal unless you specifically say it is.

What you're describing- turning a freighter into a big pile of [Emplacement Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Emplacement_Points "Emplacement Points") to hold high-end [Capital Ship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Capital_Ship "Capital Ship") weaponry- skids right past nonstandard ("sufficiently dissimilar" systems) and smacks right into the middle of forbidden ("particularly unusual combinations"). The rules as written not only empower you to veto such an absurd design, they expect you to do so.

If your players object, tell them that they have to prove that the proposed modification is not "particularly unusual" by providing a few examples of similar cases. If they can't find any such example... well, "unique" is pretty unusual, isn't it? We've certainly seen freighters with extensive modifications ([The *Millennium Falcon*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Millennium_Falcon "The Millennium Falcon"), [The *Outrider*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Outrider?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Outrider (page does not exist)"), [The *Wild Karrde*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Wild_Karrde?action=edit&redlink=1 "The Wild Karrde (page does not exist)"), [The *Ebon Hawk*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Ebon_Hawk "The Ebon Hawk")), but none have had anything remotely approaching what you describe. In fact, the Gallofree [GR-75 Medium Transport](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/GR-75_Medium_Transport "GR-75 Medium Transport") is specifically described as being frequently modified for many different functions, but the idea of turning it into a "pocket battleship" never seems to have crossed the Rebels' minds.

You can use the following guidelines to help you adjudicate these sorts of unusual cases, but keep in mind that guidelines are just that: guidelines. They aren't hard-and-fast limits, and they aren't substitutes for good GM judgment and common sense. Very generally speaking, if any of the following are true, the modification is probably nonstandard. If two or more are true, the modification is probably not possible at all (unless there are counterexamples that show otherwise, of course).

- The [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") does not have any similar system in its stock version, or if the system is specifically described as nonstandard for that type of [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") (such as a [Hyperdrive](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hyperdrive "Hyperdrive") on a [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter")).
- The modification would increase the Starship's [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed"), [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), or [Shield Rating](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Shield_Rating "Shield Rating") by more than 50% or the number of [Weapon Systems](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Systems "Weapon Systems"), passengers, or carried [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") (measured in [Hangar Space](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Space "Hangar Space")) by more than 100% compared to the Starship's stock version.
- If the [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") has gained [Emplacement Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Emplacement_Points "Emplacement Points") equal to or greater than its [Cost Modifier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cost_Modifier "Cost Modifier") by converting cargo space, all further modifications are nonstandard except for volume-heavy modifications such as [Cryogenic Chambers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cryogenic_Chambers "Cryogenic Chambers"), [Escape Pods](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Escape_Pods "Escape Pods"), [Extended Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Extended_Range "Extended Range"), [Hangar Bays](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hangar_Bays "Hangar Bays"), [Medical Suites](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Medical_Suites "Medical Suites"), [Passenger Conversion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Passenger_Conversion "Passenger Conversion"), and [Smuggler's Compartments](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Smuggler%27s_Compartments "Smuggler's Compartments").

## Saga Edition FAQ

This is a compilation of official and semi-official clarifications from game developers taken from Wizards of the Coast's *Star Wars* forums and homepage. The anwers provided are comments made by game designers such as Rodney Thompson and Gary Sarli. The *Star Wars* forums have been since wiped out, and the following were preserved by RavingDork. (Some of the original threads can still be viewed thanks to archive.org.)

**Q1:** If a game designer posts on these forums, should his word be taken as official errata?

**A:** For the record, a clarification from a designer/developer/editor doesn't have to be "official"- i.e. published- to tell you the correct interpretation of an ambiguous statement.

The point of a publication is to communicate specific information; thus, if someone is officially tasked (i.e. contracted) with creating a publication, they're technically still performing that task if they have to clarify an ambiguous bit of that communication. If this were not the case and it was not part of the same contracted job, then that would imply that Rodney would have to *pay me* for billable hours just to answer his personal questions about what a rule is supposed to mean! (Sure, I could use the money, but still...)

To be clear, it's not the same as *errata*, where the actual text is changing and thus must be done in an official context (mostly because it's a licensed product, and Lucas Licensing has contractual approval over anything published under that license).

Yes, published clarifications are nice because they're visible to more readers and codified for future reference, and if you have a conflict between two clarifications the published one would be "official" and take precedence, but a clarification's publication or lack thereof doesn't change its relevance or veracity. (And, yes, a clarification can be incorrect, but until you have a particular reason to believe this to be true- such as conflicting clarifications from different authors- you have no reason not to take it at face value.)

But, then, I guess you'd have to believe that I can clarify without publishing to accept my clarification about clarifying without publishing. 😃

AbilitiesSpeciesClassesTalentsSkillsFeatsThe ForceHeroic TraitsEquipmentCombatVehiclesDroidsPrestige Classes

**Q2:** If I increase my [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") score high enough, do I get extra [Trained Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained_Skills "Trained Skills") and [Languages](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Languages "Languages")? What about [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom") and [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers")? [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") and bonus [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")? What about class starting [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and their prerequisites, would I get those if I later met the prerequisites?

**A:** Yes, yes, yes, and yes. Saga is very retroactive friendly

**Q3:** If a [Trandoshan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trandoshan "Trandoshan") (or similar creature with Limb Regeneration) lost multiple limbs, would they all grow back in 1d10 days? Or would it take longer? Would I roll 1d10 for each limb or 1d10 for all limbs?

**A:** All limbs grow back in the normal time frame for that creature assuming the creature in question did not perish from his grievous injuries (for [Trandoshans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trandoshans "Trandoshans"), all limbs would grow back in just 1d10 days). You can roll a single 1d10 for all the severed limbs if they were all lost during the same day.

**Q5:** If a [Wookiee](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiee "Wookiee") has [Weapon Focus (Rifles)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus_(Rifles) "Weapon Focus (Rifles)"), do they also apply the +1 bonus to [Bowcasters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bowcasters "Bowcasters") due to their Weapon Familiarity racial trait? What about [Gungans](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gungans "Gungans") with their [Atlatls](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atlatls "Atlatls") and [Cestas](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cestas "Cestas")?

**A:** Weapon Familiarity effectively changes the weapon type in all ways (as far as that particular character is concerned), not just for determining proficiency. For example, a [Wookiee](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiee "Wookiee") with [Weapon Focus (Rifles)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus_(Rifles) "Weapon Focus (Rifles)") and **[Weapon Specialization (Rifles)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Specialization_(Rifles) "Weapon Specialization (Rifles)")** would get a +1 to hit and +2 to damage with [Bowcasters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bowcasters "Bowcasters") as well as [Rifles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rifles "Rifles"). A [Gungan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gungan "Gungan") with the same [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats")/[Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") invested in [Simple Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Simple_Weapons "Simple Weapons") would receive the same bonuses with a [Cesta](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cesta "Cesta") or [Atlatl](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atlatl "Atlatl").

**Q6:** If a racial ability does the same thing as a [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent"), does it count as a "virtual [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent")" to qualify
for other [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and such?

**A:** No it does not become a virtual [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") and does not qualify for other abilities unless specifically named.

**Q7:** What is the damage dealt by a [Yuzzem](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yuzzem "Yuzzem") with the [Martial Arts III](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Martial_Arts_III "Martial Arts III") Feat? What comes after d12s?

**A:** The damage should probably cap at d12. (The benefit being that the [Yuzzem](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yuzzem "Yuzzem") get there a [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") earlier than everyone else.)

**Q8:** What size are the 3 meter tall [Thakwaash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thakwaash "Thakwaash") supposed to be?

**A:** They are supposed to be Medium (they were originally meant to be Large, but LucasArts overruled that, which is why the current write-up is a bit wonky), though you can play a Large sized version provided your GM allows as a house rule (with all the bonuses and penalties inherent to being Large).

**Q9:** Can a non-Force sensitive character [Multiclass](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Multiclass "Multiclass") to take levels in [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi")?

**A:** Yes.

**Q10:** Can you play a non-Force sensitive [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi")?

**A:** Yes. You will have to start as a non-[Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi"), however, and then [Multiclass](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Multiclass "Multiclass") into it. Once you take your first level of [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi"), don't pick up the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat. This is an ideal choice for such concepts as Darth Vader's training [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids"), an alternate [General Grievous](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/General_Grievous "General Grievous"), or for Antarian Rangers.

**Q11:** What are the starting credits for a character above 1st level?

**A:** As a very loose guideline, your wealth at any given level should be something like this: (level x [level -1]) x 2,000 credits\*

*\*Double this for a [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") with the **[Wealth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wealth "Wealth")** Talent*

Suggested Starting Credits by Level

| LEVEL | STARTING CREDITS | WITH **[WEALTH](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/WEALTH "WEALTH")** |  | LEVEL | STARTING CREDITS | WITH **[WEALTH](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/WEALTH "WEALTH")** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2 | 4,000 | 8,000 | 12 | 264,000 | 528,000 |
| 3 | 12,000 | 24,000 | 13 | 312,000 | 624,000 |
| 4 | 24,000 | 48,000 | 14 | 364,000 | 728,000 |
| 5 | 40,000 | 80,000 | 15 | 420,000 | 840,000 |
| 6 | 60,000 | 120,000 | 16 | 480,000 | 960,000 |
| 7 | 84,000 | 168,000 | 17 | 544,000 | 1,088,000 |
| 8 | 112,000 | 224,000 | 18 | 612,000 | 1,224,000 |
| 9 | 144,000 | 288,000 | 19 | 684,000 | 1,368,000 |
| 10 | 180,000 | 360,000 | 20 | 760,000 | 1,520,000 |
| 11 | 220,000 | 440,000 |  | | |

This basically assumes that roughly 60% of your total income is spent on upkeep, paying rent, buying food, paying taxes, repairing equipment, getting medical care, etc. (This is a much higher rate than in D&D, where you'd only be expected to "burn" about 10%- but D&D doesn't have an Imperial Revenue Service.)

For example, at 16th level, it should be somewhere around half a million credits.

The reason I say this is a very loose guideline, though, is that at least half of this wealth would tend to be in a non-liquid form (e.g. property, intangibles such as favors and allies, etc.). As a very rough guideline, having someone owe you a favor (i.e. one-time intervention on your behalf, not to exceed one encounter) is worth about 2,000 x CL; having a long-term ally (i.e. someone who will help you out any time they are available) is worth about 40,000 x CL x (% chance that they're available).

**Q13:** If I have all three of the **[Lightsaber Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Defense "Lightsaber Defense")** Talents along with the **[Makashi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Makashi "Makashi")** Talent you get a +5 bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), correct. If I then take the **[Jar'Kai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jar%27Kai "Jar'Kai")** Talent does it double to a +10 bonus?

**A:** No. **[Jar'Kai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jar%27Kai "Jar'Kai")** requires you to wield two [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers"), and **[Makashi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Makashi "Makashi")** says you must be wielding a single [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") in one hand. The best you could do would be a +6 from **[Lightsaber Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Defense "Lightsaber Defense")** x3 and **[Jar'Kai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jar%27Kai "Jar'Kai")**.

**Q:** If you are a [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier")/[Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") with the **[Harm's Way](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Harm%27s_Way "Harm's Way")** and **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** Talents, can you jump in the way of a blaster shot meant for an ally and then deflect it harmlessly away?

**A:** Yes.

**Q14:** Does the **[Equilibrium](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equilibrium "Equilibrium")** Talent reset your [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") to the top? Or does it just remove [Poisons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Poisons "Poisons"), [Diseases](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Diseases "Diseases"), and other effects so that you can begin recovering/healing normally?

**A:** It does both.

**Q15:** Does using [Dark Side Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Talents "Dark Side Talents") such as **[Dark Presence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Presence "Dark Presence")** or **[Swift Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Power "Swift Power")** automatically increase your [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") by one? These [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") don't mention it, while it is specifically called out by their prerequisite [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent"), **[Power of the Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Power_of_the_Dark_Side "Power of the Dark Side")**.

**A:** Unless it explicitly says so, no.

**Q16:** The **[Lightsaber Throw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Throw "Lightsaber Throw")** Talent allows you to throw a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") as a [Thrown Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thrown_Weapon "Thrown Weapon") with which you are proficient, which deals normal damage. However, Table 8-3: Melee Weapons has a footnote indicating that [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") can be [Thrown](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thrown "Thrown") (the same footnote applies to [Knives](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knives "Knives"), [Spears](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Spears "Spears"), and a few others). On table 8-5: Weapon Ranges, [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") are again mentioned as [Thrown Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thrown_Weapons "Thrown Weapons") (although the numbering of the footnotes does not match up with the numbering on the table). Does this mean that anyone can use a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") as a [Thrown Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thrown_Weapon "Thrown Weapon") (the only advantage of **[Lightsaber Throw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Throw "Lightsaber Throw")** being the ability to retrieve it as a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action")), and if so, how much damage does it do as a ranged weapon? (For that matter, how much damage does any [Thrown](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thrown "Thrown") melee weapon do?)

**A:** It's [Improvised](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improvised "Improvised") unless you have **[Lightsaber Throw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Throw "Lightsaber Throw")**, and a [Thrown Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thrown_Weapon "Thrown Weapon") if you do have the Talent. [Thrown](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thrown "Thrown") melee weapons do their normal damage.

**Q17:** Do reroll abilities stack? So a [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") that gives a reroll, and the racial ability that gives a
reroll to the same [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"), do I get two rerolls?

**A:** Yes, they do stack. For example, a [Zabrak](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Zabrak "Zabrak") with the **[Acute Senses](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acute_Senses "Acute Senses")** Talent could reroll their [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check twice taking the last one. Effectively, they would get three [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") checks.

*Q18:'* From the [Jedi Consular Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Consular_Talent_Tree "Jedi Consular Talent Tree")- **[Adept Negotiator](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Adept_Negotiator "Adept Negotiator")**. Are there any restrictions to the number of times this can be used on a target? We've noted under the [Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persuasion "Persuasion") Skill that if you fail at a [Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persuasion "Persuasion") check you cannot test again for 24 hours. Would this also apply to **[Adept Negotiator](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Adept_Negotiator "Adept Negotiator")**? Do they need [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") to attempt it? What happens if they or their allies "attack" you? Does your condition move up? Can you attack someone and then still continue to **Negotiate**?

**A:** There is no limit to the number of times you can use this on a single target. The target must be able to see, hear, and understand you, so yes you must have [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight"). If one of his buddies attacks you, and then you attack them back, then you can continue to use the Talent, but that would break the "end of the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track")" effect.

**Q19:** If you use the **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** Talent to send a blaster shot back at the enemy do you apply your level bonus to the damage? Or do you use the bad guy's damage roll?

**A:** You roll the normal damage for the weapon and apply your level bonus to it.

**Q20:** What happens to an opponent's weapon when you use the **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")** ability against it? Does it get damaged or destroyed? What if they made an [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") strike?

**A:** Unless you specifically attack an opponent's weapon, it does not take damage (such as from **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")**). Someone using an [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") strike does not take damage from being blocked.

**Q21:** Sith **[Dark Healing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Healing "Dark Healing")** says it deals/heals 1d6 per Class Level. It's available to both the [Sith Apprentice](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Apprentice "Sith Apprentice") AND the [Sith Lord](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Lord "Sith Lord") PrC's. So... what? It applies to the class you originally took the Talent for? The higher of the two? Or the two added together?

**A:** You use the sum of your [Sith Apprentice](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Apprentice "Sith Apprentice") and [Sith Lord](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Lord "Sith Lord") levels (as well as any other levels that have access to the Talent).

**Q22:** The Sith Talent **[Dark Healing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Healing "Dark Healing")** requires the Sith to make a [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack") roll... so can it get a [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit")? Be deflected? Redirected?

**A:** Yes, it can critical and be deflected (it is a [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack")). No, it cannot be redirected (since it is not a blaster bolt).

**Q23:** The description for the **[Stunning Strike](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stunning_Strike "Stunning Strike")** Talent says that the target slides an additional step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") if your attack roll beats their [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"). Is this intentional, or was it supposed to say "if your damage roll...?"

**A:** It should be damage, not attack.

**Q24:** How can you tell if a [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") is a [Force Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talent "Force Talent")? Certainly, they are not limited to chapter 6. What about **[Force Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Persuasion "Force Persuasion")** from the [Jedi Consular Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Consular_Talent_Tree "Jedi Consular Talent Tree") and others like it? I ask because I have a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") who wants to go into [Force Adept](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Adept "Force Adept"). Will the Jedi Consular/Knight/Sentinel [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") allow me to meet the prerequisites?

**A:** If it is not found in Chapter 6: [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"), than it is not a [Force Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talent "Force Talent"). [Force Tradition Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Tradition_Talents "Force Tradition Talents"), like the [Jensaarai Defender Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jensaarai_Defender_Talent_Tree "Jensaarai Defender Talent Tree") and [Dathomiri Witch Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dathomiri_Witch_Talent_Tree "Dathomiri Witch Talent Tree"), are also [Force Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talents "Force Talents"), like Control, Sense, Alter, and Dark Side.

**Q25:** Are you considered your own ally in Saga for purposes of **[Born Leader](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Born_Leader "Born Leader")**, **[Cover Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover_Fire "Cover Fire")**, and similar [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and abilities?

**A:** No. Such abilities do not have any effect on you unless the ability specifically says so.

**Q26:** Is it possible for me to take the same [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") twice? What if it is from two separate [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees") (such as **[Notorious](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Notorious "Notorious")** under the [Bounty Hunter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bounty_Hunter "Bounty Hunter") and [Crime Lord](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crime_Lord "Crime Lord"))?

**A:** Not unless the [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") specifically says you can. Two [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") with the same name are the same [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") even if they are from different trees. Unless the [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") in question specifically says you can take it twice, you can't.

**Q28:** Can one use **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** and **[Evasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Evasion "Evasion")** vs. the same [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack")? If so, does a failed **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** + a failed **[Evasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Evasion "Evasion")** against an [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack") that hits you result in 1/4 of the original damage?

**A:** Yes you may use both **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** and **[Evasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Evasion "Evasion")** against an attack. It does not deal 1/4 damage, it negates the remainder altogether.

**Q29:** When a [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") uses their **[Harm's Way](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Harm%27s_Way "Harm's Way")** ability, do they automatically suffer damage from the attack? Or is the attack compared to their [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") first?

**A:** It is compared to their [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") first.

**Q30:** Is **[Dark Healing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Healing "Dark Healing")** affected by **[Damage Reduction 10](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Reduction_10 "Damage Reduction 10")**?

**A:** Yes. Pretty much everything except [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") is effected by [Damage Reduction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Reduction "Damage Reduction").

**Q31:** Does the **[Force Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Pilot "Force Pilot")** Talent allow you to pick up the [Vehicular Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicular_Combat "Vehicular Combat") Feat even if you don't have training in the [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") Skill? Can you use it to get into the [Ace Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ace_Pilot "Ace Pilot") Prestige Class without the [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") Skill?

**A:** Yes and yes. For all intents and purposes, you are treated as being [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in that particular [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"). The RAW disagrees with the above statement, but the Devs disagree with the RAW on this particular matter (they clarified that it simply got lost in translation). The only difference is that whenever you are called to make a [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") check, you make a [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check instead. If you have abilities that allow you to reroll or [Take 10](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Take_10 "Take 10") on [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") checks, then you may use those abilities when making [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") checks as well (but only when substituting for [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") checks). This also applies to other [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") that allow [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") replacement as well.

**Q32:** How many times can you take **[Coordinate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Coordinate "Coordinate")**? 3 times or 5 times? The wording is ambiguous.

**A:** 5 times.

**Q33:** Does [Darkvision](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Darkvision "Darkvision") trump the **[Improved Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Concealment "Improved Concealment")** Talent?

**A:** If the [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") is in effect due to low-light or darkness (as opposed to fog, smoke, etc.), then Yes.

**Q34:** How do you handle things like **[Elusive Target](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Elusive_Target "Elusive Target")** and firing into melee when you are using [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") and [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks")? Do you take the appropriate penalties anyways?

**A:** **[Elusive Target](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Elusive_Target "Elusive Target")** specifically says, "opponents you are attempting to target." [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") target the area, not the opponent, and compare the attack roll to their [Reflex Defenses](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defenses "Reflex Defenses"). **[Elusive Target](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Elusive_Target "Elusive Target")** has no effect against [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") as written.

**Q35:** Can I use the [Lightsaber Form Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talents "Lightsaber Form Talents") with other weapons? Unlike other [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), a great many of the forms don't specifically say you need a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") to use them. For example, could I use **[Djem So](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Djem_So "Djem So")** to attack someone with my [Vibroblade](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibroblade "Vibroblade") or [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol")? Could I reroll all of my [Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pistol "Pistol") attacks with **[Juyo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Juyo "Juyo")**? Can I use **[Sokan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sokan "Sokan")** even if I am not wielding a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") at all (such as if I had been disarmed)?

**A:** [Lightsaber Form Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talents "Lightsaber Form Talents") specifically require a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") (a [Sith Sword](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sith_Sword "Sith Sword") can also be used in some cases, such as with any [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") requiring **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** or **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")** as a prerequisite, though that particular weapon is an exception to the rule). The Talent Tree specifically says, "Anyone using a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")..." (to clarify, using means "wielding"). Feel free to use **[Sokan](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sokan "Sokan")** while unarmed (or some other variation of [Lightsaber Form Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Form_Talents "Lightsaber Form Talents")) in your games, but that would be a house rule.

**Q36:** Can I **[Block](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Block "Block")** or **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** an attack that hit me automatically as a result of [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") expenditure?

**A:** No. An attack that hit automatically due to a [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") can generally only be negated by another [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") (to make the attack miss). However, you could use the **[Force Fortification](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Fortification "Force Fortification")** Talent to take normal damage instead of critical damage.

**Q37:** Do [Noble Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble_Talents "Noble Talents") that activate on [Ranged Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attacks "Ranged Attacks") also activate on [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") when they miss (dealing half damage)?

**A:** Unless an ability that activates on [Ranged Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attacks "Ranged Attacks") further clarifies that it activates on [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks"), assume that it doesn't.

**Q38:** When precisely do you get the bonus form the **[Bullseye](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bullseye "Bullseye")** Talent?

**A:** The target loses its [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") modifier for a single attack. The Talent effectively modifies the benefits gained by the [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") Action.

**Q39:** The rules are clear in that you can **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** attacks made from [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") of up to frigate size. However, can you redirect those shots? My reading of the **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** Talent seems to say that you CAN do it IF the ship in question is firing [Blaster Cannons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannons "Blaster Cannons"). Things such as [Laser Cannons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Laser_Cannons "Laser Cannons") and [Turbolasers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Turbolasers "Turbolasers") are simply not mentioned in the Talent's text. Is this interpretation correct?

**A:** The intent is for you to be able to redirect blasters, lasers, and even turbolasers so long as the weapon does not suffer the -20 penalty vs. targets less than Colossal size. This includes almost all weapons on [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") up to Colossal size as well as [Point-Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Point-Defense "Point-Defense") weapons on larger [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles"). For example, in Dark Empire, Luke redirects a shot from an AT-AT's [Laser Cannons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Laser_Cannons "Laser Cannons").

Now, to the issue of [Splash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Splash "Splash"): this is a bit of nuance that gets lost in the rules, but in the case of energy weapons (such as blaster/laser cannons), the [Splash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Splash "Splash") is a completely secondary effect that occurs as a result of surface effects on the target or the ground nearby. In other words, the weapon pumps so much energy into such a small space that a small chunk of the target or ground is vaporized, exploding outward as it expands and producing shrapnel in the process. If the original attack is "nullified" by using **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")**, the [Splash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Splash "Splash") doesn't occur at all around the original target; if the shot is redirected, the new target is subject to [Splash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Splash "Splash"). By the way, the reason **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** was written where it won't work against (most) [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") is specifically to prevent goofy outcomes, like "deflecting" a [Grenade](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grenade "Grenade") (which should detonate due to the heat of the blade, sufficient to melt or vaporize almost anything and more than enough to set off an explosive), missile (ditto), or [Flamethrower](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Flamethrower "Flamethrower") (which has no discrete "shots" to deflect, being a spray of flaming liquid or gas that is too wide to be blocked by a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") blade). The same logic is also why it doesn't work against "oversized" ([Capital Ship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Capital_Ship "Capital Ship")) weapons. A shot from a Star Destroyer's [Turbolaser](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Turbolaser "Turbolaser") is so wide that the blade can't possibly deflect enough of the shot to be meaningful. So, to sum up the intent:

**[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** can be used against [Ranged Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attacks "Ranged Attacks") that originate from weapons that are not oversized (i.e. that take a -20 penalty against targets smaller than Colossal size), *if*

- you are targeted by a non-[Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack") weapon or a weapon eligible for **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** (blasters, lasers, and turbolasers, when not set to [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun")), in which case the attack is completely nullified (including secondary effects such as [Splash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Splash "Splash") or [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") triggered when an attack hits, such as a Gunslinger's **[Debilitating Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Debilitating_Shot "Debilitating Shot")**) *or*
- you are in the area of an [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") attack (including a [Strafing Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strafing_Attack "Strafing Attack")), in which case damage is reduced by one-half the original (i.e. half damage on a hit, no damage on a miss).

**[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** can be used immediately after successfully using **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** against a [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack"), *if*

- the attack was made by a blaster, laser, or turbolaser that was not set to [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun"), *and*
- you have not attempted to use **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** since the beginning of your last turn.

If successful, make a [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack") against a target within Point-Blank [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range"), resolving the effects as if you made a single attack with the weapon that you deflected.

- Apply modifiers based on your [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), and so forth, not those of the original attacker. (e.g. you add 1/2 your Heroic Level to damage instead of 1/2 the attacker's level).
- The new target is subject to any secondary effects (such as [Splash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Splash "Splash")), just as if you had originated the attack.
- You only redirect a single shot from the weapon regardless of how many shots were directed at you by the original attack, so you don't get the benefits of [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire"), [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot"), or [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") under any circumstances.
- A [Fire-Linked](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fire-Linked "Fire-Linked") weapon is treated as a single weapon for game purposes (i.e. all shots are designed to focus on small area, unlike a [Weapon Battery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Battery "Weapon Battery"), which is designed to bracket a target). As such, your damage roll includes any extra damage from fire-linking, already included in the damage listed for the [Fire-Linked](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fire-Linked "Fire-Linked") weapon.

[Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") shots can't be redirected, but they can certainly be deflected.

**Q40:** The [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative") Skill says you can [Take 10](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Take_10 "Take 10") on it. I thought the rules said you can't [Take 10](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Take_10 "Take 10") when stress and distractions (which combat most certainly is) would prevent it. What does this text entail exactly?

**A:** If you're about to launch an attack on someone (such as an ambush against unaware targets), have prepared yourself, and are the one initiating the encounter, you can [Take 10](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Take_10 "Take 10").

**Q41:** If I have a [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") or similar ability that states "*you are considered trained in the **X** Skill for purposes of this Talent*" does that mean I can qualify for [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), and [Prestige Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prestige_Classes "Prestige Classes") as if I had been [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in that [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill")?

**A:** Yes.

**Q42:** How does [Sense Surroundings](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sense_Surroundings "Sense Surroundings") work exactly?

**A:** [Sense Surroundings](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sense_Surroundings "Sense Surroundings") allows you to ignore [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") and [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment") for the purposes of making [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") checks (including any penalties resulting from [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") or [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment")). Thus, a Jedi could "sense" other characters, creatures, or [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") in another room even though the door is shut. You must still make a [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check to detect the targets and you suffer all the normal penalties to your [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check with the exception of those from [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") or [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment") (thus, you still cannot detect targets that are extraordinarily far away due to the accumulative range penalty). You must still roll a [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check to detect targets who aren't actively hiding (DC 5 for a Medium creature; +5 for every size difference smaller than Medium, -5 for every size difference bigger than Medium). Sensing targets in this manner does not allow you to see them or determine what they are. You are simply aware of their presence and location. In any event, you do not have [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") to your target (and thus can't target them with a great deal of [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers")).

**Q43:** How do characters survive such high falls in the movies? The existing rules for [Falling Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Falling_Damage "Falling Damage") don't seem to allow for survival at those kinds of falling distances.

**A:** First, note that the errata allows you to use both [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump") and [Acrobatics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatics "Acrobatics") to reduce [Falling Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Falling_Damage "Falling Damage") in the same way (that is, knock off 3 meters if you beat DC 15, and an additional 3 meters for every 10 points you beat that DC by). Also, the [Falling Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Falling_Damage "Falling Damage") rules doesn't make this clear, but any distance above 60 meters is treated as 60 meters because that's when you hit terminal velocity (which is why the damage caps at 20d6).

Thus, you start reducing the damage from the fall with an [Acrobatics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatics "Acrobatics") check and a [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump") check (possibly with *[Surge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surge "Surge")*), you then subtract from 60 meters, not 100 (or whatever the "real" distance of your fall may be).

For example, a Jedi with a +25 modifier in their [Acrobatics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatics "Acrobatics")/[Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump") Skills falls out a high window on [Coruscant](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Coruscant "Coruscant"). Being thousands of feet high, the fall would surely kill them. However, they activate their *[Surge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surge "Surge")* power (with a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point")) to increase their [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump") Skill to +65. They then makes both an [Acrobatics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatics "Acrobatics") check and a [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump") check and gets +35 and +75, respectively. Therefore they knock off 21 meters from the fall. Even though they are higher than 60 meters, we subtract 21 from 60 leaving us with what would be effectively treated as a 39 meter fall. At 39 meters they will only take 13d6 points of damage (an average of 45 damage, 22 if the attack roll misses), low enough for a mid to high level character to survive easily (and even a few lucky/tough low-level characters!)

**Q45:** When treating a wounded character who has the [Total-Replacement Cyborg](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Total-Replacement_Cyborg "Total-Replacement Cyborg") option, do you use the [Treat Injury](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Treat_Injury "Treat Injury") or the [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") Skill to heal/repair them?

**A:** You still use [Treat Injury](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Treat_Injury "Treat Injury").

**Q46:** Does the benefit of [Point-Blank Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Point-Blank_Shot "Point-Blank Shot") (and similar ranged-based benefits) extend to Short [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") when used with an [Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate") weapon or a [Targeting Scope](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Targeting_Scope "Targeting Scope")?

**A:** No.

**Q47:** If a Jedi had the [Quick Draw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Quick_Draw "Quick Draw") Feat and wanted to draw their [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"), would they still have to
use another [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") to activate the [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")?

**A:** No. The "[Draw a Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Draw_a_Weapon "Draw a Weapon")" Action normally includes everything necessary to render that weapon immediately usable, including deactivating the safety on a [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol") and activating a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") or [Vibroblade](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibroblade "Vibroblade"). Weapons are designed to be drawn and armed very quickly, so the GM generally shouldn't require a separate action unless there's some unusual difficulty in play (such as when the wielder isn't proficient with the weapon). Similarly, you don't have to spend a separate action to deactivate or disarm a weapon when you holster it. However, it is important to note that this applies only to the actual "[Draw or Holster a Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Draw_or_Holster_a_Weapon "Draw or Holster a Weapon")" Action (that is, moving the weapon either to or from a holster, hanger, sling, and so on). It does not apply when picking up or dropping a weapon, nor does it apply when using the [Stealth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stealth "Stealth") action to use [Sleight of Hand](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sleight_of_Hand "Sleight of Hand") to get the weapon in your hand without attracting attention. In these cases, you're not getting the weapon in your hand as efficiently as you would when drawing or holstering it.

**Q48:** Can you use [Fighting Defensively](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fighting_Defensively "Fighting Defensively") and the [Melee Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Defense "Melee Defense") Feat together?

**A:** Yes. They work just fine together provided you find a way to gain an extra [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") in the round.

**Q49:** Can a Jedi use their [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") Feat in conjunction with the **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** Talent to deflect attacks farther than they normally could?

**A:** No. [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") requires that you use a ranged weapon. A Jedi using **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** is not using a ranged weapon, but a Talent.

**Q50:** Shouldn't [Force Training](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Training "Force Training") be on the [Bonus Feat List](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Bonus_Feats "Jedi Bonus Feats") for the [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class?

**A:** No. You cannot learn the [Force Training](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Training "Force Training") Feat as a bonus feat for any class. You can only use your level-dependent [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") (or more specifically, your level 1, 3, 6, 9, 12, 15 and 18 [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats")) or the bonus [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") for being a [Human](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Human "Human").

**Q51:** When a person with the Martial Arts [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and the [Crush](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crush "Crush") Feat gets someone in a [Grapple](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grapple "Grapple"), do they deal their improved [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") damage? What about the **[Sneak Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sneak_Attack "Sneak Attack")** Talent? Does the "crusher" get their **[Sneak Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sneak_Attack "Sneak Attack")** damage to their [Unarmed Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attack "Unarmed Attack") since the "crushee" is considered [Flat-Footed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Flat-Footed "Flat-Footed")?

**A:** Martial Arts increases your [Unarmed Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attack "Unarmed Attack") damage. If you make an [Unarmed Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attack "Unarmed Attack"), it does that damage. [Crush](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crush "Crush") deals bludgeoning damage equal to your [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") damage. Thus, Martial Arts increases [Crush](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crush "Crush") damage. However, **[Sneak Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sneak_Attack "Sneak Attack")** applies to [Melee Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attacks "Melee Attacks") or [Ranged Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attacks "Ranged Attacks"), not to the [Crush](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crush "Crush") Feat. It specifically says [Melee Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attacks "Melee Attacks") or [Ranged Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attacks "Ranged Attacks"). [Crush](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Crush "Crush") is merely a Feat that does [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") damage as a result of a [Pin](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pin "Pin").

**Q52:** Do Natural Weapons (claws, bite, etc.) stack with the Martial Arts [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") in some way?

**A:** Martial Arts increases the damage die of a creature's natural attacks, just as it does for a creature's [Unarmed Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed_Attacks "Unarmed Attacks"). For example, A [Cathar](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cathar "Cathar") (claw attack, 1d6 damage) with [Martial Arts I](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Martial_Arts_I "Martial Arts I") would deal 1d8 damage with a claw attack whereas a [Cathar](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cathar "Cathar") with [Martial Arts III](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Martial_Arts_III "Martial Arts III") would deal 1d12 damage with a claw attack.

**Q53:** Does [Point-Blank Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Point-Blank_Shot "Point-Blank Shot") apply to area effect weapons such as [Flamethrowers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Flamethrowers "Flamethrowers") and [Grenades](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grenades "Grenades")?

**A:** Yes. It even applies to weapons that technically don't have [Range](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Range "Range") increments (such as a [Flamethrower](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Flamethrower "Flamethrower")).
