| Knowledge (History) | [Knowledge (Galactic Lore)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Galactic_Lore) "Knowledge (Galactic Lore)") | Friendship | **[Charm Beast](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charm_Beast "Charm Beast")** or **[Force Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Persuasion "Force Persuasion")** |
| Knowledge (Jedi Lore) | [Knowledge (Galactic Lore)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Galactic_Lore) "Knowledge (Galactic Lore)") | Heal Another | *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")* |
| Knowledge (Medicine) | [Knowledge (Life Sciences)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Life_Sciences) "Knowledge (Life Sciences)") | Heal Self | *[Vital Transfer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vital_Transfer "Vital Transfer")* |
| Knowledge (Physics) | [Knowledge (Physical Sciences)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Physical_Sciences) "Knowledge (Physical Sciences)") | Illusion | N/A |
| Knowledge (Politics) | [Knowledge (Bureaucracy)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Bureaucracy) "Knowledge (Bureaucracy)") or [Knowledge (Social Sciences)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Social_Sciences) "Knowledge (Social Sciences)") | Move Object | *[Move Object](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Object "Move Object")* |
| Knowledge (Streetwise) | [Knowledge (Bureaucracy)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Bureaucracy) "Knowledge (Bureaucracy)") or [Knowledge (Social Sciences)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Knowledge_(Social_Sciences) "Knowledge (Social Sciences)") | See Force | [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") |

In most cases, [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") haven't changed too much, but Feats that provide a bonus to [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") are an exception. Most of these Feats are best realized with the [Skill Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus "Skill Focus") Feat. Use the table below to determine the equivalent mechanic for all of your character's [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") in the old rules.

| OLD FEAT | NEW MECHANIC |  | OLD FEAT | NEW MECHANIC |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Acrobatic | [Skill Focus (Acrobatics)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Acrobatics) "Skill Focus (Acrobatics)") | Shot on the Run | [Running Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Running_Attack "Running Attack") |
| Advanced Martial Arts | [Martial Arts III](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Martial_Arts_III "Martial Arts III") | Skill Emphasis | [Skill Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus "Skill Focus") |
| Alertness | [Skill Focus (Perception)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Perception) "Skill Focus (Perception)") | Spacer | [Skill Focus (Pilot)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Pilot) "Skill Focus (Pilot)") |
| Ambidexterity | [Dual Weapon Mastery I](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dual_Weapon_Mastery_I "Dual Weapon Mastery I") | Spring Attack | [Running Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Running_Attack "Running Attack") |
| Armor Proficiency (Heavy) | [Armor Proficiency (Heavy)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor_Proficiency_(Heavy) "Armor Proficiency (Heavy)") | Stamina | [Extra Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Extra_Second_Wind "Extra Second Wind") |
| Armor Proficiency (Light) | [Armor Proficiency (Light)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor_Proficiency_(Light) "Armor Proficiency (Light)") | Starship Dodge | [Vehicle Dodge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Dodge "Vehicle Dodge") |
| Armor Proficiency (Medium) | [Armor Proficiency (Medium)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor_Proficiency_(Medium) "Armor Proficiency (Medium)") | Starship Operation | [Vehicular Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicular_Combat "Vehicular Combat") |
| Armor Proficiency (Powered) | N/A | Steady | [Skill Focus (Acrobatics)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Acrobatics) "Skill Focus (Acrobatics)") |
| Athletic | [Skill Focus (Climb)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Climb) "Skill Focus (Climb)") or [Skill Focus (Jump)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Jump) "Skill Focus (Jump)") | Stealthy | [Skill Focus (Stealth)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Stealth) "Skill Focus (Stealth)") |
| Blind-Fight | **[Keen Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Keen_Shot "Keen Shot")** | Sunder | N/A |
| Cautious | [Skill Focus (Mechanics)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Mechanics) "Skill Focus (Mechanics)") | Surgery | [Skill Focus (Treat Injury)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Treat_Injury) "Skill Focus (Treat Injury)") |
| Cleave | [Cleave](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cleave "Cleave") | Toughness | [Improved Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Damage_Threshold "Improved Damage Threshold") |
| Combat Expertise | [Melee Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Defense "Melee Defense") | Track | **[Expert Tracker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Expert_Tracker "Expert Tracker")** |
| Combat Reflexes | [Combat Reflexes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Combat_Reflexes "Combat Reflexes") | Trick | [Skill Focus (Deception)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Deception) "Skill Focus (Deception)") |
| Defensive Martial Arts | N/A | Trustworthy | [Skill Focus (Gather Information)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Gather_Information) "Skill Focus (Gather Information)") or [Skill Focus (Persuasion)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Persuasion) "Skill Focus (Persuasion)") |
| Dodge | [Dodge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dodge "Dodge") | Two-Weapon Fighting | [Dual Weapon Mastery II](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dual_Weapon_Mastery_II "Dual Weapon Mastery II") |
| Endurance | [Skill Focus (Endurance)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus_(Endurance) "Skill Focus (Endurance)") | Weapon Finesse | [Weapon Finesse](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Finesse "Weapon Finesse") |
| Exotic Weapon Proficiency | [Exotic Weapon Proficiency](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Exotic_Weapon_Proficiency "Exotic Weapon Proficiency") or [Weapon Proficiency (Lightsabers)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Proficiency_(Lightsabers) "Weapon Proficiency (Lightsabers)") | Weapon Focus | [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus") |
| Fame | N/A | Weapon Group Proficiency | [Weapon Proficiency (Advanced Melee Weapons)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Proficiency_(Advanced_Melee_Weapons) "Weapon Proficiency (Advanced Melee Weapons)") |
| Far Shot | [Far Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Far_Shot "Far Shot") | Weapon Group Proficiency | [Weapon Proficiency (Heavy Weapons)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Proficiency_(Heavy_Weapons) "Weapon Proficiency (Heavy Weapons)") |
| Force-Sensitive | [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") | Weapon Group Proficiency | [Weapon Proficiency (Pistols)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Proficiency_(Pistols) "Weapon Proficiency (Pistols)") |

| Great Cleave | [Great Cleave](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Great_Cleave "Great Cleave") | Whirlwind Attack | [Whirlwind Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Whirlwind_Attack "Whirlwind Attack") |

| Headstrong | N/A | Alter | [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") ([Alter Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Alter_Talent_Tree "Alter Talent Tree")) |

| Improved Bantha Rush | [Bantha Rush](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bantha_Rush "Bantha Rush") | Aware | [Improved Sense Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Sense_Force "Improved Sense Force") |
| Improved Critical | [Triple Crit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Triple_Crit "Triple Crit") | Burst of Speed | *[Surge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surge "Surge")* |

| Lightning Reflexes | [Improved Defenses](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Defenses "Improved Defenses") | Force Whirlwind | [Improved Move Light Object](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Move_Light_Object "Improved Move Light Object") |

Like everything else, [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") usually works similarly to how it did before, but there are a few significant exceptions.

## Weapons

The primary things you'll need to adjust with weapons are range, translating multifire/autofire into the new rules, and (occasionally) changing damage dice.

### Range

Ranged weapons have their range determined by their weapon group. In addition, they may have the [Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate")/[Inaccurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Inaccurate "Inaccurate") quality, depending on their range increments (see the table below).

**Exotic Weapons:** To determine the range for an Exotic Weapon, compare it to the most similar weapon to determine what range group it should use. For example, a Medium-size weapon with a stock is probably most similar to a [Rifle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rifle "Rifle"), while a Large shoulder-fired weapon is probably most similar to a [Heavy Weapon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Weapon "Heavy Weapon"). Determine the [Accurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Accurate "Accurate") or [Inaccurate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Inaccurate "Inaccurate") quality as described below.

| WEAPON GROUP | INACCURATE | ACCURATE |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Heavy Weapons | 60 m or less | 120 m or more |
| Pistols | 8 m or less | 12 m or more |
| Rifles | 20 m or less | 40 m or more |
| Simple Weapons | 4 m or less | 12 m or more |
| Thrown Weapons | 2 m | 6 m or more |

### Autofire Capability

Most weapons are single-shot only. Rifles with a multifire setting will have single-shot and [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") capability in the new rules. Any weapon with an [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") setting in the old rules will be an [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire")-only weapon in the new rules.

### Threat Range

Note that all weapons inflict [Critical Hits](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hits "Critical Hits") at the same rate in the new rules, so the critical threat range in the previous rules is not used.

### Damage

Damage dice are the same, with a few exceptions:

#### Explosives

[Grenades](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grenades "Grenades") and the like no longer have a bonus after their damage dice (for example, 4d6+1 is now 4d6). Other explosive weapons, such as Missile Launchers and Flechette Launchers, have d6 damage dice. Figure the weapon's maximum damage and divide by 6, rounding down, to determine the number of damage dice.

*Example:* A DF-D1 Flechette Launcher deals 5d8 points of damage. Its maximum damage is 40; dividing by 6 and rounding down, we get 6. Thus, in Saga Edition, the DF-D1 deals 6d6 points of damage.

#### Blasters

All blasters deal 3 dice of damage. Add the weapon's maximum damage to its minimum damage, and look up the result on the following chart:

| MAX DAMAGE | NEW DICE |
| --- | --- |
| 12 or less | 3d3 |
| 13-18 | 3d4 |
| 19-24 | 3d6 |
| 25-30 | 3d8 |
| 31-36 | 3d10 |
| 37 or more | 3d12 |

*Example 1:* An A-280 Blaster Rifle deals 3d8+3 points of damage. Its maximum damage is 27, and its minimum damage is 6, yielding a total of 33. According to the chart, the A-280 Blaster Rifle deals 3d10 points of damage in the new rules.

*Example 2:* A DY-225 Heavy Blaster deals 3d8-2 points of damage. Its maximum damage is 22, and its minimum damage is 1, yielding a total of 23. According to the chart, the DY-225 Heavy Blaster deals 3d6 points of damage in the new rules.

#### Slugthrowers and Powered Melee Weapons

All slugthrowers and powered melee weapons deal 2 dice of damage. Add the weapon's maximum damage to its minimum damage, and look up the result on the following chart:

| MAX DAMAGE | NEW DICE |
| --- | --- |
| 7 or less | 2d3 |
| 8-11 | 2d4 |
| 12-15 | 2d6 |
| 16-19 | 2d8 |
| 20-23 | 2d10 |
| 24 or more | 2d12 |

*Example 1:* A Great Force Pike deals 3d8 points of damage. Its maximum damage is 24, and its minimum damage is 3, yielding a total of 27. According to the chart, a Great Force Pike deals 2d12 points of damage in the new rules.

*Example 2:* A Vibrorapier deals 2d6+2 points of damage. Its maximum damage is 14, and its minimum damage is 4, yielding a total of 18. According to the chart, a Vibrorapier deals 2d8 points of damage in the new rules.

#### Nonpowered Weapons

All other nonpowered weapons should only deal a single die of damage. Add the weapon's maximum damage to its minimum damage, and look up the result on the following chart:

| MAX DAMAGE | NEW DICE |
| --- | --- |
| 4 or less | 1d3 |
| 5-6 | 1d4 |
| 7-8 | 1d6 |
| 9-10 | 1d8 |
| 11-12 | 1d10 |
| 13 or more | 1d12 |

## Armor

Armor works differently in *Saga Edition*, providing a bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") (and sometimes [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense")) instead of Damage Reduction.

### Armor Bonus to Reflex Defense

The Armor bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") is equal to the armor's previous Damage Reduction. Add +1 to the Armor bonus if the suit had a DR of 3 or more, add +2 to the Armor bonus if the suit had a DR of 5 or more, and add +3 to the Armor bonus if the suit had a DR of 7 or more.

### Equipment Bonus to Fortitude Defense

Most full-body armor (that is, covering the whole body, including the head and face) provides an [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") bonus to [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"). This bonus is +1 for an Armor bonus of +4 or less, +2 for an Armor bonus of +5 to +8, +3 for an Armor bonus of +9, and +4 for an Armor bonus of +10.

### Maximum Dexterity Bonus

Most armor has a Maximum [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") Bonus equal to (12 - Armor bonus)/2, rounded down.

## Other Equipment

With few exceptions, [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") will work as written in the new rules, but devices that provide an [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") bonus of greater than +2 should be very rare. In these cases, consider alternatives that reduce penalties or add capabilities instead of adding bonuses.

For example, a Camouflage Poncho might grant the ability to have [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment") as long as you are in the poncho's designated terrain type and you are either [Prone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prone "Prone") or adjacent to an object that would grant [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment") or [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover"). Camo Scout Armor might do the same thing in any terrain. A Shadowsuit might take this a step further and allow the wearer to reroll [Stealth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stealth "Stealth") checks (keeping the second result, even if it is worse).

### Electronic Devices

Most electronic devices that provide a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus have an [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") of 10 + (2 × bonus), but very simple electronic devices- that is, those that don't provide a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus- usually have an [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") of 1.

Converting Vehicles and Starships can be a little tricky because you need to determine statistics in a particular order. Follow the guidelines below in the order listed.

**Size:** A vehicle's new size is based on its old size (in character, starship, or station scale), as shown in the table below. You'll need this when recalculating [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense").

**Speed:** A Vehicle's [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed") is determined by its maximum velocity (in km/h). Generally, [Capital Ships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Capital_Ships "Capital Ships") (that is, anything of Colossal (Frigate) or greater size) do not have a [Character Scale](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Scale "Character Scale") speed rating. At this 1.5- meter-square scale, they don't move around on battlefields- they are battlefields.

Maximum velocity itself does not change.

| MAX VELOCITY | SPEED\* |
| --- | --- |
| Less than 10 km/h | 2 Squares |
| 10-29 km/h | 4 Squares |
| 30-99 km/h | 6 Squares |
| 100-299 km/h | 8 Squares |
| 300-999 km/h | 12 Squares |
| 1,000 km/h or more | 16 Squares |

*\*Reduce [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed") by one step for [Walkers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Walkers "Walkers") of Gargantuan or greater size.*

**Speed (Starship Scale):** For Airspeeders and Starships with a maximum velocity rating (In km/h), determine the Vehicle's [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed") in space using the following chart:

If a Starship does not have a maximum velocity rating, its new speed in space is equal to 1/2 its space speed in the previous rules.

| MAX VELOCITY | SPEED (IN SPACE) |
| --- | --- |
| Less than 800 km/h | 1 Square |
| 800-949 km/h | 2 Squares |
| 950-1,049 km/h | 3 Squares |
| 1,050-1,199 km/h | 4 Squares |
| 1,200-1,299 km/h | 5 Squares |
| 1,300 km/h or more | 6 Squares |

**Hit Points:** [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") are determined by the Vehicle's previous Hull Points. The exact formula is determined by the Vehicle's class (Starship vs Vehicle) and its previous Hull Points.

- Starship with 200 or more Hull Points: [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") = Hull Points × 3.
- Starship with less than 200 Hull Points: [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") = Hull Points.
- Other Vehicle (including Airspeeders): [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") = Hull Points × 2.

**Damage Reduction:** A Vehicle's [Damage Reduction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Reduction "Damage Reduction") is determined by its size:

- Up to Large, DR 5
- Huge or Gargantuan, DR 10
- Colossal or Colossal (Frigate), DR 15
- Colossal (Cruiser) or greater, DR 20
- Ground Vehicles with 150 or more [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") add an additional +5 to their DR (maximum DR 20)

**Shield Rating (SR):** A Vehicle's [Shield Rating](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Shield_Rating "Shield Rating") is equal to its Shield Points/2, rounding down to the nearest multiple of 5.

**Strength:** A Starship's [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") is determined by its [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") and size (both determined above). A Vehicle's [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") is determined by its [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") only.

- Starship, Colossal (Station): [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") = 98 + ([Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/75).
- Starship, Colossal (Cruiser): [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") = 74 + ([Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/75).
- Starship, Colossal (Frigate): [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") = 50 + ([Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/75).
- Starship, Colossal or less: [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") = 26 + ([Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/7.5).
- Other Vehicle, 100 or more [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"): 18 + ([Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/10).
- Other Vehicle, less than 100 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"): 10 + ([Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/5).

**Constitution:** Any Living Vehicle (such as [Yuuzhan Vong](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yuuzhan_Vong "Yuuzhan Vong") technology) will have a [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") equal to its [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") score. This doesn't affect [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") or [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"), but it can be useful for determining if a coralskipper or another Living Vehicle dies after being reduced to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"). Living Vehicles automatically have all appropriate environmental immunities. For example, a coralskipper is not subject to vacuum, atmospheric, or radiation hazards because it is a Starship designed to function in such hostile environments.

All non-living Vehicles (that is, the vast majority of *Star Wars* technology) have no [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") score at all.

**Dexterity:** There is no direct analog to a Vehicle's [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") in the previous rules. Use the guidelines below to estimate a vehicle's [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity"), picking a specific value in the listed range based on how that Vehicle compares with others. When in doubt, give the Vehicle the minimum [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") in a given range.

A Vehicle shouldn't have a [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") score below 10 unless it is an immobile space station.

| DEXTERITY | VEHICLE |
| --- | --- |
| 10-13 | Most freighters, dreadnoughts, heavy walkers |
| 14-16 | Most cruisers, most frigates, armed transports, heavy bombers, light walkers, heavy repulsorlift vehicles |
| 17-19 | Corvettes, most starfighters, light repulsorlift vehicles |
| 20-23 | Superiority fighters, combat airspeeders, older speeder bikes |
| 24-26 | Most speeder bikes, swoops, interceptors |

**Intelligence:** A Vehicle's [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") score is generally 10 + the best fire control bonus of all its weapons. For relatively modern Vehicles with sophisticated on-board computers (that is, almost everything in the *Star Wars* galaxy, except Vehicles from underdeveloped planets), the minimum [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") should be 12 for Ground Vehicles and 14 for Airspeeders and Starships.

| CHARACTER SIZE | STARSHIP SIZE | STATION SIZE | NEW SIZE | SIZE MODIFIER |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fine |  |  | Fine | +10 |
| Diminutive |  |  | Diminutive | +5 |
| Tiny |  |  | Tiny | +2 |
| Small |  |  | Small | +1 |
| Medium |  |  | Medium | +0 |
| Large | Fine |  | Large | -1 |
| Huge | Diminutive |  | Huge | -2 |
| Gargantuan | Tiny |  | Gargantuan | -5 |
| Colossal | Small | Fine | Colossal | -10 |
|  | Medium | Diminutive | Colossal (Frigate) | -10 |
|  | Large | Tiny | Colossal (Frigate) | -10 |
|  | Huge | Small | Colossal (Frigate) | -10 |
|  | Gargantuan | Medium | Colossal (Cruiser) | -10 |
|  | Colossal | Large | Colossal (Cruiser) | -10 |
|  |  | Huge | Colossal (Station) | -10 |
|  |  | Gargantuan | Colossal (Station) | -10 |
|  |  | Colossal | Colossal (Station) | -10 |

**Weapons:** Damage for Starship Weapons stays the same as in the previous rules. For other Vehicles, weapon damage will change depending on the die type used:

- *Damage #d10:* new damage is #d10×2.
- *Damage #d8:* new damage is #d10.
- Any other damage dice: no change except for dropping any modifiers (for example, 4d6+1 becomes 4d6).
- *Autofire:* Turbolasers, heavy cannons, single laser cannons (that is, those that aren't fire-linked, double cannons, or quad cannons), ion cannons, tractor beams, and missile/torpedo/grenade launchers are single-shot only. All other Vehicle Weapons have [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") capability.
- *Capital Ships:* [Capital Ships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Capital_Ships "Capital Ships") have half as many [Weapon Batteries](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Batteries "Weapon Batteries") (rounded down) as in the previous rules. This accounts for the lack of fire arcs and the somewhat messy issue of batteries, which had a different meaning in the older d6 version of the game than in the later d20 versions.

**Grapple:** A Vehicle's [Grapple](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grapple "Grapple") modifier is equal to its pilot's [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") + the Vehicle's [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") modifier + the Vehicle's special size modifier;

- Large +5
- Huge +10
- Gargantuan +15
- Colossal +20
- Colossal (Frigate) +25
- Colossal (Cruiser) +30
- Colossal (Station) +35

**Armor Bonus:** A Vehicle's Armor bonus is determined by its [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), class, and size.

- *Starship with 200 or more [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"):* Armor bonus is equal to [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/500 - size modifier, rounded down.
  - For example, a Colossal (Frigate)-sized Starship with 1,000 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") would have an armor bonus of +12 (1,000/500 is equal to 2, and 2 minus -10 is equal to 12).
- *All other Vehicles:* Armor bonus is equal to [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points")/50 - size modifier, rounded down.

**Reflex Defense:** The Vehicle's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") is equal to 10 + size modifier + armor bonus (or pilot's Heroic Level) + [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") bonus.

**Fortitude Defense:** The Vehicle's [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") is equal to 10 + its [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") bonus (or [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") bonus, for Living Vehicles).

**Damage Threshold:** [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") is equal to [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") + special size modifier;

- Large +5
- Huge +10
- Gargantuan +20
- Colossal +50
- Colossal (Frigate) +100
- Colossal (Cruiser) +200
- Colossal (Station) +500

While most of the guidelines under "Species," "Skills," and "Feats" apply to creatures, the following tips will make conversion a little easier.

**Class and Level:** All creatures convert their existing class and level into levels of the [Beast](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Beast "Beast") class.

**Hit Points:** Creatures' [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") are equal to their Vitality Points, and their [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") and [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") may be adjusted upward or downward depending on their class in the old rules.

| CREATURE CLASS | HIT POINTS |
| --- | --- |
| Herd Animal | +(2xLevel) |
| Parasite | +(1xLevel) |
| Predator | No Change |
| Scavenger | +(1xLevel) |
| Vermin | No Change |

**Base Attack Bonus:** All creatures now have a [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") equal to their level × 3/4. This will decrease the [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") for Predators but increase it for Herd Animals and Parasites.

**Size:** Creatures' sizes stay the same, but the size bonuses have changed, and size bonuses are no longer added to attack bonuses. Use the following chart to adjust the creature's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") and attack bonuses:

| SIZE | REFLEX DEFENSE | ATTACK BONUS |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Colossal | -2 | +8 |
| Gargantuan | -1 | +4 |
| Huge | +0 | +2 |
| Large | +0 | +1 |
| Medium | +0 | +0 |
| Small | +0 | -1 |
| Tiny | +0 | -2 |
| Diminutive | +1 | -4 |
| Fine | +2 | -8 |

**Natural Attacks:** A creature's natural attacks should be changed to match the description in the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* (see [Beast](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Beast "Beast")). Note that a creature adds double its [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") modifier to its damage if it has only one type of natural attack, and [Beasts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Beasts "Beasts") no longer take a penalty for using secondary natural attacks.

**Natural Armor:** Creatures have Natural Armor equal to that listed in the old rules or their [Beast](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Beast "Beast") class level, whichever is less.

**Defenses:** Creature's [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores") are equal to 10 + the relevant [Ability](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability "Ability") modifier ([Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") for [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") for [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"), and [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom") for [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense")). If the creature has Natural Armor, add its value to its [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense").

**Damage Threshold:** Creatures' [Damage Thresholds](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Thresholds "Damage Thresholds") are equal to their [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"), plus a bonus for creatures of greater than Medium size: Large, +5; Huge, +10; Gargantuan, +20; Colossal, +50.

**Speed:** Adjust a creature's [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed") as described in "Species."

**Skills:** Adjust a creature's species bonus to different [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") as described in "Species." In addition, you may convert a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus into a bonus [Trained Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained_Skill "Trained Skill"). Normally, most [Beasts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Beasts "Beasts") will have only one [Trained Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained_Skill "Trained Skill").

**Other Abilities:** Most special abilities can be used as written, but follow the guidelines in "Species" for converting anything unusual.

## Saga Edition Preview

Classes and Character Creation“I Know a Few Maneuvers...”Use the Force, Luke!Prepare for Ground AssaultAdvanced Combat TrainingDroids, Droids, DroidsSkirmish at the Sarlacc PitStarship and Vehicle Combat

*By Rodney Thompson*

The *Star Wars Roleplaying Game: Saga Edition* rulebook comes out in May. Are you ready for the new core rules? Maybe you've already planned your first character. (I know I have- [Chalkazza](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chalkazza "Chalkazza"), a [Wookiee](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiee "Wookiee") bounty hunter). Or maybe you're outlining your first campaign, cooking up villains and nemeses to throw at an unsuspecting band of heroes. Of course, before you can start blasting [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") and locking [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers"), you've got to be able to create those wonderful characters.

Fortunately, *Saga Edition* has you covered. One of the primary goals of the new edition was to make [Character Creation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Creation "Character Creation") more flexible. At the same time, we wanted to streamline the process so that new players could jump right in, and we tried to give experienced players a wealth of options when crafting their characters.

As some ravenous readers might know, we've reduced the number of [Heroic Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Classes "Heroic Classes") to five: [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi"), [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble"), [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel"), [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout"), and [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier"). We bid the Fringer farewell and send the Tech Specialist back to the assembly line, granting their heroic abilities to other classes. We also distilled the Jedi Guardian and Jedi Consular classes into a single [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class, which is more in line with what we see in the movies. The [Force Adept](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Adept "Force Adept") has become a Prestige Class, but we reinvented the process of creating a non-Jedi Force-user. It now involves a new set of mechanics that finally lets players create Force-users of their own [Force Traditions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Traditions "Force Traditions") without being locked into class features that didn't necessarily fit the character's backstory.

At the same time, we worked to infuse the remaining classes with excitement and a lot of abilities. If you want to play a "Fringer," you're likely to use the [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") or [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") class, and a "Tech Specialist" will use the [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") class as well. The merging of these classes allows you to create heroic characters that might fit a certain theme while still being able to do exciting things. *Star Wars* is all about excitement, intrigue, and depth, and your character's abilities should convey that.

## Talent Trees

When it came time to redesign the classes, we took a hard look at what works for the d20 system and what could be improved. We asked questions such as, "Why do all Force Adepts learn to use [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") to understand other languages?" and "Why do all Nobles get resource access, regardless of their history?" Sure, certain iconic archetypes stand out in the *Star Wars* universe, but do all iconic roles demand the same class features?

*Saga Edition* features a system of [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") similar to that which was used in the d20 Modern game system. At odd-numbered levels, you select a [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent"), which lets you do something unique in much the same way that class features did under the old rules. These [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") are broken down into subgroups called trees that arrange the [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") by function or theme. Some [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") can't be selected unless you've already taken another [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") from the same tree (much like a [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") prerequisite), and thus you build a set of class features that play off of one another.

The changes to the system allow you to build a character to your liking, selecting very specific features rather than being saddled with the same features as every other member of your class. Moreover, the flexible new rules let you choose the order in which you gain abilities, so you can prioritize which talents are most important to you and which ones you'll pick up at a later level. So if you envision your [Rodian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rodian "Rodian") smuggler as a "run and gun" type, you can build their class features to suit those needs. By the same token, if you want them to be a fast-talking swindler and con artist, they're just as easy to create. If each class represents a classic *Star Wars* archetype, your selection of [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") lets you tailor that archetype to your personality and game play style.

You can even create two members of the same class that share almost no [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), and with the addition of [Prestige Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prestige_Classes "Prestige Classes"), you might have absolutely no overlap with other similar characters. This not only allows multiple heroes of the same class to fulfill unique roles, but with larger groups, it ensures that each PC contributes something unique to the game.

In addition, [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees") expand the amount of information we can include in future *Saga Edition* releases. Rather than worrying about alternative or replacement class features, upcoming products will include new [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees") for existing classes, as well as new [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") for existing trees.

[Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees") will also keep [Prestige Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prestige_Classes "Prestige Classes") from becoming overly specialized. No longer will the Naval Officer and the Officer need to be two different [Prestige Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prestige_Classes "Prestige Classes"), and the [Jedi Knight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Knight "Jedi Knight") Prestige Class can encompass Jedi healers, investigators, battlemasters, and so on. The individual requirements for specialization will be carried in the [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") themselves, not in totally separate classes.

## More Character Options

In addition to providing heroes with access to [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees"), we tried to make individual characters more capable overall. *Saga Edition* uses a [Hit Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Point "Hit Point") system to track damage, with a "[Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track")" that slides up and down over the course of the battle, imposing and removing penalties.

Also, heroes get triple their Hit Die at first level, so they're better able to survive a few blaster shots without going down for the count. We wanted a group of 1st-level heroes to be able to fight [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") in the first game session, if the GM so wished. *Star Wars* characters fight bad guys, and lots of them, and the *Saga Edition* rules let the heroes jump right into light combat. [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") are still pretty threatening at 1st level, but now heroes have enough resources and abilities that deciding whether to fight or flee is actually a tough decision.

## The Force Is With You

We've revamped Jedi and other Force-users to make them more flexible. The single [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") class contains [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees") for the [Jedi Consular](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Consular_Talent_Tree "Jedi Consular Talent Tree"), the [Jedi Guardian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Guardian_Talent_Tree "Jedi Guardian Talent Tree"), and the [Jedi Sentinel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Sentinel_Talent_Tree "Jedi Sentinel Talent Tree"), as well as a Talent Tree for [Lightsaber Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber_Combat_Talent_Tree "Lightsaber Combat Talent Tree"). In the *Star Wars* movies, we see that [Yoda](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yoda "Yoda") is part Jedi Consular and part Jedi Guardian, and now players can create characters that are similar hybrids. Similarly, you can create a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") who behaves more like [Yoda](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yoda "Yoda") from the classic trilogy- focusing more on [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") than the [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber")- and you can do it with a great diversity of character options.

Moreover, in *Saga Edition*, you can build a [Force Adept](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Adept "Force Adept") character using a combination of Force abilities and the [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), and [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") of other classes. For example, let's say you want to create an urban Force-user who grew up on the streets of [Chandrila](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chandrila "Chandrila") and who focuses more on city survival than on "primitive" [Force Traditions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Traditions "Force Traditions"). You could start with a [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") as your Heroic Class and add [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") that let them wield [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") while dodging speeders and firing blasters. The new edition of the rules greatly expands the possibilities for Force-users, allowing for a wider array of character options.

### Sample Talent Tree: Jedi Sentinel

As an example of what a [Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Tree "Talent Tree") might look like, let's walk through the tree for the Jedi Sentinel. The [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") that follow this path are the true enemies of [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side"), hunting down evil wherever they go. Masters of subtlety, Jedi Sentinels manage to withstand the lure of [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side"), even when confronted almost constantly with its power.

- **Clear Mind:** You may reroll any opposed [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check made to avoid being detected by other Force-users. You must take the result of the reroll, even if it is worse.
- '*Dark Side Sense:* Jedi Sentinels are exceptionally talented at rooting out evil. You may reroll any [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check made to sense the presence and relative location of creatures with a [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") of 1 or higher. You must take the result of the reroll, even if it is worse.
- **Dark Side Scourge:** Against creatures with a [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") of 1 or higher, you deal extra damage on [Melee Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attacks "Melee Attacks"). The number of points of extra damage is equal to your [Charisma](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charisma "Charisma") bonus (minimum +1).
  - *Prerequisite:* **Dark Side Sense**
- **Force Haze:** You can spend a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") as a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") to create a "haze" that hides you and your allies from the perceptions of others. You can hide a number of creatures in [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") equal to your Class Level. Make a [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check and compare the result to the [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense") of any opponent that moves into [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") of any creature hidden by your **Force Haze**. If your check result beats the opponent's [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense"), all hidden creatures are treated as if they had [Total Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Total_Concealment "Total Concealment") against that opponent. The **Force Haze** lasts up to 1 minute but is dismissed instantly if anyone hidden by the **Force Haze** makes an attack.
  - *Prerequisite:* **Clear Mind**
- **Resist the Dark Side:** You gain a [Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force "Force") bonus of +5 to all [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores") against [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") that have the [*[Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side "Dark Side")*] descriptor. You gain the same bonus against [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") that originate from any dark Force-user (that is, any Force-user whose [Dark Side Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Score "Dark Side Score") equals their [Wisdom](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wisdom "Wisdom") score).
  - *Prerequisite:* **Dark Side Sense**

### Sample Stat Block

Here's a preview of a statistics block that was constructed using the *Saga Edition* rules: [Chalkazza](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chalkazza "Chalkazza"), Wookiee Bounty Hunter.

## Improvements All Around

High-level play is much more streamlined and efficient in *Saga Edition*, and combat runs just as smoothly at 20th level as it does at 1st level. Furthermore, the new [Character Creation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Creation "Character Creation") rules let you build a high-level NPC in 5 to 10 minutes instead of an hour. Because generating enemies and allies on the fly is fast and simple, it's easier for Gamemasters to improvise when players diverge from the planned session.

All in all, the changes to [Heroic Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Classes "Heroic Classes"), [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), and other systems make the process of generating characters quick and flexible.

In future previews of *Saga Edition*, we'll take a closer look at individual aspects of [Character Creation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Creation "Character Creation"), such as the [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") system and building Force adepts. We'll also examine other aspects of the new rules, such as the revamped combat system, the way [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") are handled, and the unified [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle")/[Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") combat system.

*By Rodney Thompson*

One of the most significant changes to the rules in the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game: Saga Edition* is a revamp of the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system, which was overhauled to make it better fit the style of what a *Star Wars* game should be. The changes to the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system are part of the overall game design philosophy that went into the creation of the new edition.

Conceptually, the new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system is similar to that of the d20 System. You still roll a d20, add your [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus, and try to overcome a DC based on the challenge presented. The method of arriving at that [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus, however, has changed. One goal of *Saga Edition* is to make the game faster and easier to run, and the revamped skill system does just that. Gamemasters will enjoy the benefits of the new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system as well, since it speeds up the creation of NPC statistics.

## Consolidation

One of the first changes made to the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system was the consolidation of [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). We took a hard look at the [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") in the game and refined the list to make it sleeker and more concise. First, we grouped [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") by concept and relevant ability modifier, and then we decided which [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") to combine. Along the way, we removed some [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") that were rarely used in most games. For example, we removed Craft, because traditionally the Repair skill was used to build [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids"), [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships"), and so on.

The new, slimmer [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") list deals more in concepts and themes than in specifics. However, we didn't want to consolidate too much- making the [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") too generic might have made their purpose less clear, too. Thus, some [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") were left unconsolidated because their function was distinct from that of other [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills").

For example, we realized that the distinction between the Search and Spot skills was only a matter of intent. Does a character notice something because they're trying to notice it, or did they notice it incidentally? Either way, what matters is the end result. [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") should be differentiated when they differ in utility. But since Search and Spot differed only in intent, they were easy to consolidate.

Once we decided to combine those two [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), we naturally examined the Listen skill next. The game had separate [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") for seeing and hearing, but what about the other senses- smelling, feeling, and tasting? It didn't make sense to represent only some of the senses but not others. So we folded Listen in with the combined Search/Spot to form the core of the new [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") skill, which is used by the GM to determine whether characters notice something by any of their sensory methods.

Lastly, we took a look at Sense Motive. The [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") allowed a character to perceive changes in tone (listening), body language (sight), and mannerisms (both) to determine someone's true motives. That sounded a lot like what we were already doing with the [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") skill, and so we folded Sense Motive into the new skill as well.

As you can see from the example above, consolidation allows individual [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") to be more consistent with their utility. In *Saga Edition*, a GM need only think in broad terms to determine which skill checks to use in the course of an adventure.

- Will the heroes notice the smell of blaster burns when they walk into a room? [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception")!
- Will the heroes convince a diplomat to see their side of things? [Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persuasion "Persuasion")!
- Will the heroes trick the local police into letting them go free? [Deception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deception "Deception")!

Another benefit of consolidation is that it allows heroes to be good at more than just a few specialized [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). In the old system, classes that didn't have a lot of Skill Points often found themselves unable to contribute to certain situations. Now, all characters are more likely to be able to participate in any given scene because they no longer have to spread their [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") so thin. Additionally, skill resources are no longer split among similar [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") such as Spot and Listen, so a hero can pick up a greater variety of [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") rather than focusing on one conceptual track.

## Trained/Untrained

Though it may be considered a radical change, *Saga Edition* eliminates the concept of Skill Ranks. Instead of investing resources and micromanaging [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), a character is either [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") or [Untrained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Untrained "Untrained") in a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"), period. Our research into how players created their characters showed that they typically maxed out their ranks in a couple of [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") and put only a few points into other [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). That led to disparities between their favored [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") and their "leftover" [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). Moreover, players who created "jack of all trades" characters typically chose classes that received a lot of Skill Points and spread them evenly over many [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills").

Skill Points were one of the most complicated parts of [Character Creation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Creation "Character Creation"). Not only was it prone to error, but it also meant that creating high-level NPCs could take up to an hour. Eliminating Skill Points allowed us to streamline [Character Creation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Creation "Character Creation") and keep [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonuses consistent with what most players were accustomed to.

So, what does it mean to be [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained")? When you're [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"), you can perform any of the "Trained Only" uses found in many [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). While some entire [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") were "Trained Only" in the previous version of the game, now only certain uses of the [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") require training. Moreover, being [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") provides you with a flat bonus that, when combined with a bonus based on your Class Level and your [Ability Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Score "Ability Score") modifier, determines your final [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") modifier. Essentially, being [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") means that you can do things that other characters of the same level might not be able to do, bumping you up to the next tier of [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") DCs.

The formula for determining a character's [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus is as follows:

**1/2 Character Level + relevant [Ability](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability "Ability") modifier + 5 (if [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained")) + 5 (if [Skill Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus "Skill Focus"))**

One advantage of this system is that characters who aren't [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") can still use it in some cases. Since part of your [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus is based on your level and [Ability](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability "Ability") modifier, as you advance in levels, you essentially gain "Free" bonuses to [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") you aren't [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in. Thus, you become able to do simple things that previously were available only to those [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill").

For example, a 1st-level character [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in the [Use Computer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") Skill can slice computers, open doors, and reprogram Droids right out of the gate. But imagine that later in the campaign, when the hero is at 10th level, they become disabled during a battle just as their fellow heroes need to hot-wire a door. Another character who is not [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in [Use Computer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") can try to open the door. Their success depends on how well they roll, but at least the group isn't completely stymied. Essentially, lower-level challenges can be attempted by any higher-level hero, even if the character is [Untrained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Untrained "Untrained") in the needed [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill").

At this point, you might be wondering why these changes were made. The simple answer is this: anyone can do anything in *Star Wars* if the scene calls for it. For example, over the course of the saga, we see [Obi-Wan Kenobi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Obi-Wan_Kenobi "Obi-Wan Kenobi") fly ships, deceive people, perform amazing acrobatics, use diplomacy, ride animals, command troops, get information from his contacts on the street, and more. Similarly, [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") can fast talk, run, shoot, fly [The *Millennium Falcon*](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Millennium_Falcon "The Millennium Falcon") in circles around [Star Destroyers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Star_Destroyers "Star Destroyers"), hot-wire doors, lead troops, formulate plans, ride a [Tauntaun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tauntaun "Tauntaun"), and use other skills seemingly at will. The new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system simulates this extremely well. Obi-Wan and Han might not be [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in all the relevant [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), but their [Untrained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Untrained "Untrained") bonus allows them to attempt those actions with some chance of success.

In game terms, that chance is important for capturing the *Star Wars* feel. In the past, some players have been unwilling or unable to participate in certain scenes because they didn't have ranks in the relevant [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"). For example, let's say the Gamemaster wants to have a chase sequence where the heroes pursue an enemy while riding [Tauntauns](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tauntauns "Tauntauns") through an icy canyon on [Hoth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hoth?action=edit&redlink=1 "Hoth (page does not exist)"). Very few people put ranks in the [Ride](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ride "Ride") Skill- it just doesn't come up that often. As a result, some of the players might be tempted to say, "Well, I don't have ranks in [Ride](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ride "Ride"), so I can't participate in this scene." Thus, the exciting, fast-paced chase sequence the Gamemaster hoped for is reduced to one or two players participating while the others sit and watch.

The new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system encourages players to try things as the encounter demands. Sure, you might not be as good at riding a [Tauntaun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tauntaun "Tauntaun") as your friend, but at least you can hop on and join the fun rather than waiting back at Echo Base.

## What Kind of Bonus Was That Again?

To further streamline the process of determining [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonuses, *Saga Edition* cuts down on elements that provide static bonuses to [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). We kept the [Skill Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus "Skill Focus") Feat, which provides a flat bonus to your [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") modifier, and you still get bonuses from [Training](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Training "Training"), Class Levels, and [Ability Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Scores "Ability Scores"). But there are no other ways to modify your [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus. The [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") that provided a +2 bonus to two [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") are gone, as are Species Traits or class features that provided a bonus to [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). Synergy bonuses have been eliminated as well. In the old version of the rules, all of these factors contributed to a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system that was needlessly complex and vastly prone to errors and abuse.

In *Saga Edition*, min-maxing your character is less a matter of pumping bonuses into [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") and more a matter of finding abilities that work well with your [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"). This also means that Gamemasters can create NPCs more easily, without keeping track of dozens of small bonuses; more on this point later.

## Specialization Through Other Means

Some of you might be thinking, "Without all the little bonuses to [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), how can a character be the best at something? How do you represent someone being better at the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"), or more specialized in a group of [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills")?"

The answer lies in the way other abilities synergize with skills in *Saga Edition*. If you want to build a hero who is the best of the best in a particular [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"), first become [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill"), and then take the [Skill Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Focus "Skill Focus") Feat. That bumps your [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus up quite a bit, and if your relevant [Ability Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Score "Ability Score") modifier is high, you should be a cut above the rest (at least, compared to others of your level). However, in place of all the small +1 and +2 bonuses to [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), you'll select [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and other special abilities that make you even better.

For example, [Wookiees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiees "Wookiees") have a natural ability to reroll [Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persuasion "Persuasion") checks to [Intimidate](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intimidate "Intimidate"). The creatures are naturally fearsome, so rather than providing them with a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") bonus, we gave them a greater chance to succeed on their [Skill Check](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Check "Skill Check") roll. Likewise, the [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") class has a Talent (**[Improved Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Initiative "Improved Initiative")**) that allows [Scouts](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scouts "Scouts") to reroll their [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative") check (yes, [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative") is now a skill) and take the result of the second roll. Other abilities might allow you to [Take 10](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Take_10 "Take 10") under pressure, or use one skill's modifier in the check for another [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill").

Specializing in a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") is now a matter of choosing [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") that allow you to do new things with your [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") rather than just increasing their modifiers. Not only does this lead to more exciting and dynamic game play, but it's also a lot more fun than just adding up numbers on a [Character Sheet](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Sheet "Character Sheet").

## 20th-Level Characters in 1/20th the Time

By consolidating [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") and eliminating Skill Points, we've made it much easier to create high-level characters in *Saga Edition*. Just choose which [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills") are [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") and select [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") and abilities to augment them. Gamemasters no longer need to stat out an entire NPC just to get a [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") modifier. Instead, they simply look at their level, the relevant [Ability](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability "Ability") modifier, and whether they're [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill")- a process that can take mere moments. On-the-fly creation of NPC statistics is much faster under the new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system, which facilitates high-level play.

Overall, the new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system not only leads to quick character generation but also helps the game capture the exciting cinematic feel of the *Star Wars* movies without getting bogged down in bonuses and Skill Ranks.

*By Rodney Thompson*

One of the most important aspects of the *Star Wars* setting is the omnipresence of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"). Without [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"), *Star Wars* would be little more than another example of space opera. [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") distinguishes the setting and is a key part of the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*. For *Saga Edition*, [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") revamp represented one of the most daunting challenges of the entire design process. Getting it right certainly took a lot of work, a lot of tweaking, and a lot of time during the design process.

In *Saga Edition*, [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") has been distilled into a more straightforward mechanic, relying on a single [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") and the activation of powers and special abilities rather than on multiple skills and feats. The old system overtaxed the resources of Force-users too often. Rather than consuming permanent resources, such as Skill Points and Feat Slots, *Saga Edition* uses the mechanics of the individual powers with limited uses to balance out Force use. [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") has become a system unto itself, rather than a part of other subsystems.

Under the old system, it was difficult for a Force-user to be skilled at anything other than [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"). One of the first changes made for *Saga Edition* was the creation of a new [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") called [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force"). This Skill allows a character to do basic things with [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") without consuming many resources. These actions won't have a huge impact on any given encounter, but they still allow the use of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") with a simple [Skill Check](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Check "Skill Check"). A character must have the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat to use the [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") Skill [Untrained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Untrained "Untrained"), and only someone [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") can activate [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") (Explained below).

## Use the Force

*Requires the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat*

You draw upon the Force to help you recover from injuries, gain special insights, or perform other remarkable acts. You must have the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat to be trained in this Skill.

**Special:** You can't make Use the Force checks unless you have the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat. Use the Force is a [Class Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Class_Skill "Class Skill") for any character with the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat.

You can [Take 10](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Take_10 "Take 10") on a Use the Force check, but you can't [Take 20](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Take_20 "Take 20").

**Activate Force Power (Trained Only):** You make a Use the Force check to use a [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power"). This use of the Skill requires no [Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Action "Action").

**Force Trance (Trained Only):** As a [Full-Round Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full-Round_Action "Full-Round Action"), you can enter a Force Trance with a DC 10 Use the Force check. In this state, you remain fully aware of your surroundings. Each hour you remain in the trance, you regain a number of [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") equal to your Character Level. You can emerge from the trance as a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"). If you remain in a Force Trance for 4 consecutive hours, you emerge from the trance fully rested (as though you'd rested for 8 hours). While you're in a Force Trance, you can go ten times as long as normal without food or water (see the [Endurance](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Endurance "Endurance") Skill).

**Move Light Object (Trained Only):** As a [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action"), you can use [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") to telekinetically lift and move a relatively light object within your [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight"). A successful DC 10 Use the Force check allows you to move an object weighing up to 5 kg a distance of 6 squares in any direction. As a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action"), you can use the object as a projectile weapon, but the DC increases to 15. If your Use the Force check beats the target's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), the object hits and deals 1d6 points of bludgeoning damage.

**Search Your Feelings:** As a [Full-Round Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full-Round_Action "Full-Round Action"), you can make a DC 15 Use the Force check to determine whether a particular action will yield favorable or unfavorable results to you in the immediate future (10 minutes or less). For example, you can make the check to determine whether destroying a Dark Side artifact will have immediate unforeseen repercussions. The answer does not take into account the long-term consequences of a contemplated action. Using the above example, a successful check would not portend a future encounter with vengeful darksiders angered by the destruction of the dark side artifact. (The GM must assess the immediate consequences of the action, based on what they know about the circumstances.)

**Sense Force (Trained Only):** You automatically sense disturbances in [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"). A location that is strong in [The Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Dark_Side "The Dark Side") of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") can be sensed out to a range of 1 kilometer. A relative, companion, or close friend in mortal danger or great pain can be sensed out to a range of 10,000 light years. A great disturbance, such as the destruction of an entire populated planet or the distress of a whole order of allies, can be sensed anywhere in the same galaxy. As a [Full-Round Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full-Round_Action "Full-Round Action"), you can make a DC 15 Use the Force check to determine the distance and general direction to the location of the disturbance.

As a [Full-Round Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Full-Round_Action "Full-Round Action"), you can use this ability to actively sense other Force-users out to a range of 100 kilometers. If you succeed on a DC 15 Use the Force check, you know how many Force-users are within range, their approximate distance and direction from you, and whether you've met them before or not. Another Force-user within range can try to conceal their presence from you by making an opposed Use the Force check. If they equal or exceed your Use the Force check, you don't sense their presence at all.

**Sense Surroundings:** As a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"), you can make a DC 15 Use the Force check to ignore the effects of [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") and [Concealment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Concealment "Concealment") when making [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") checks to detect or observe targets. Increase the DC by 5 if this ability is used against targets with [Total Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Total_Cover "Total Cover").

**Telepathy:** As a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action"), you can establish a telepathic link with a distant creature. Through the link, you can exchange emotions or a single thought, such as "Go!", "Help!", or "Danger!" The target must have an [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") of 2 or higher, and the distance between you and the target determines the DC (see below). Against an unwilling target, you must make a Use the Force check against the target's [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense"); if the check fails, you cannot establish a telepathic link or attempt to telepathically contact the target for 24 hours unless the target becomes a willing one.

| TELEPATHY DISTANCE | DC |
| --- | --- |
| Same planet | 15 |
| Same system | 20 |
| Same region/quadrant of the galaxy | 25 |
| Different region/quadrant of the galaxy | 30 |

## Unlimited Power(s)!

Now that you've seen the [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") Skill, you should have a good idea of how it works at its most basic level. None of its uses can radically alter the flow of combat. To do that, a character must be [Trained](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Trained "Trained") in the Skill and have access to [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers"). Force-users gain access to a [Feat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feat "Feat") called [Force Training](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Training "Force Training") that gives them a pool of powers to draw upon. Each power represents a single use of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") that helps make up a character's "Suite."

When a character uses a [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power"), they remove it from their suite of powers, make a [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check, and follows the explanation of the effect. That power is now considered spent and can't be used again. Force-users regain their [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") by resting for one minute outside of combat, activating special [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), spending [Force Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Points "Force Points"), and a variety of other means. Additionally, if you roll a Natural 20 on your [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check to activate a [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power"), you regain all of your spent powers- [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") is with you!

The new system allows Force-users to do incredible things without completely overshadowing the other heroes. [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") take up your actions and have a finite amount of uses; to regain them, you must spend time, resources, or effort. Thus, well-trained Force-users will be able to use [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") well and often, pausing occasionally to regain their powers.

The system works very well for Jedi in particular, who now face a tactical choice between using their [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") and wading into combat with a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"). For example, a Jedi must decide whether to:

- open combat with their [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers"), then enter battle to continue the fight;
- hold their powers in reserve, engaging the enemy with a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") and activating their [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") only when needed;
- disengage from combat to recover a spent power; or
- charge back in with [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") at the ready.
