The combination of [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") and [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") combat in *Saga Edition* is not only more engaging for players, but it also encourages more diversity in combat and simulates what is seen in the movies.

The sample Force Power *[Rebuke](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rebuke "Rebuke")*, below, illustrates how the new Force system works.

### Rebuke

You harmlessly absorb or deflect one [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power") used against you, perhaps even turning it against its creator.

- **Time:** [Reaction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reaction "Reaction").
- **Target:** One [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power") directed at you.

**Make a [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check.** If your result equals or exceeds the check result of the [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power") directed at you, you harmlessly redirect it and suffer no ill effects. If you result exceeds the check result of the [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power") directed at you by 5 or more, you may choose to turn the [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power") against its creator, who suffers the effect.

**Special:** If you successfully reflect a [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power") back at its originator, the originator may attempt to *Rebuke* the power as well. If they reflect it back again, both you and the originator are affected by the [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power"). You can spend a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") as a [Reaction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reaction "Reaction") to suffer no ill effects from a [Force Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Power "Force Power") that has been rebuked twice- once by you and once by the power's originator.

*[Rebuke](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rebuke "Rebuke")* is one of the new powers added to *Saga Edition* that helps the game more closely model events seen in Episodes II and III. The power covers the duel between [Yoda](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yoda "Yoda") and [Count Dooku](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Count_Dooku "Count Dooku") duel at the end of *Attack of the Clones*. It also helps to illustrate how Anakin and Obi-Wan were able to lock themselves into a Force duel in *Revenge of the Sith*, sending them hurling to either side of the control room on [Mustafar](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mustafar "Mustafar").

## Force Talents

Force-users also have access to a selection of special [Force Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talents "Force Talents") that enhance their Force-using ability. Any character who takes the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat gains access to several Force Talent Trees ([Alter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Alter_Talent_Tree "Alter Talent Tree"), [Control](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Control_Talent_Tree "Control Talent Tree"), [Dark Side](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dark_Side_Talent_Tree "Dark Side Talent Tree"), and [Sense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sense_Talent_Tree "Sense Talent Tree")), which add special abilities to their powers or grant them special actions regarding [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force"). Some [Force Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talents "Force Talents") require the character to expend a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") to activate them, while other [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") alter the way a character uses [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") in a typical situation.

Any character with the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat can always select a [Force Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talent "Force Talent") whenever they would gain a new [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") from a standard [Heroic Class](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Class "Heroic Class") or a [Prestige Class](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prestige_Class "Prestige Class"). Thus, Force-sensitive characters can mix and match [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") from their classes with [Force Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talents "Force Talents"), giving them greater diversity in how they're built. [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi"), who gain [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") as a Bonus Feat, can also select from these [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees"). However, the class-specific [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") for [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") help cater toward their unique talents and make choosing a generic [Force Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talent "Force Talent") a tough decision.

## Alternate Force Traditions

All of the elements described above come together to create our new system for creating non-Jedi Force-users. In the old system, it was the domain of the Force Adept class. In *Saga Edition*, creating a Force-user from an alternate tradition is a simple matter of taking the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat and choosing [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers") and [Force Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Talents "Force Talents") that suit the tradition. For example, a player who wants to create an urban Force-user might select the [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") class, choose a few [Scoundrel Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel_Talents "Scoundrel Talents"), dip into the [Alter Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Alter_Talent_Tree "Alter Talent Tree") for a few more [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), take the [Force Training](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Training "Force Training") Feat a few times to learn [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers"), and mix and match the use of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") with the features of the [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") class.

Moreover, some specific [Force Traditions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Traditions "Force Traditions") have their own unique [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees") that represent abilities inherent to that particular tradition. They're considered Force Talent Trees, but only characters who belong to that tradition can choose from that tree. The *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* presents two alternate [Force Traditions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Traditions "Force Traditions"): [The Jensaarai](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Jensaarai "The Jensaarai") and [The Witches of Dathomir](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Witches_of_Dathomir "The Witches of Dathomir"). Each tradition has its own [Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Tree "Talent Tree"), which provides Force-users with unique abilities that complement their other [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats").

Let's close this Saga Edition Preview with a sample statistics block for a [Human Jensaarai Defender](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Human_Jensaarai_Defender "Human Jensaarai Defender"), which makes use of the new system.

*By Rodney Thompson*

Combat in the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game* is fast and furious, with heroes moving all over the battlefield and performing incredible stunts and feats of heroics. *Star Wars* also puts a great deal of emphasis on ranged combat, as blasters are the most common weapon in the galaxy, while still allowing Jedi to move into melee combat range, swinging a [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") at hapless foes. Moreover, mobility is highly valued in *Star Wars* combat, and creating a combat system that allowed for all of these dynamics at once presents something of a challenge.

The *Saga Edition* combat rules build on the groundwork laid down by previous iterations of the d20 System. For *Saga Edition*, we wanted to streamline the combat system so that it was faster on a round-by-round basis, yet still allowed Jedi to engage in lengthy duels like those in the movies. The solution was to create combat rules that sped up an individual character's round while still keeping combat going over longer periods. Simultaneously, the system lets heroes mow through [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") and Battle Droids, encouraging combat encounters that see "cannon-fodder" enemies dropping quickly and enemies with Heroic Class levels lasting longer. Additionally, we wanted to ensure that high-level combat moved as quickly as low-level combat.

## Enter the Swift Action

One of the biggest additions to the rules was the [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"). Introduced in the *Dungeons & Dragons* game, the [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") is a middle ground between the [Free Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Action "Free Action") and the [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action"). It gives the system a bit more versatility in how it assigns [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") without opening the door to a nearly infinite number of [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") (like the [Free Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Free_Action "Free Action") does). The [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") represents something that takes almost no time or effort, such as activating an item, switching between weapon modes, dropping an item, or falling [Prone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prone "Prone").

One difference between the [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") and other [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") is that you can take more than one [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") in a round if you sacrifice a [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action") or a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action"). Just as you can sacrifice a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") to take an additional [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action"), you can also sacrifice a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") or [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action") to take an additional [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"). As a result, a character can theoretically take up to three [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") in a single round.

Additionally, some Actions can't be completed unless you spend multiple [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions"). For example, [Aiming](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aiming "Aiming") requires you to use two [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions"), which are spent before making the attack that you enhance with the [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") Action.

Further, some Actions allow you to spend multiple [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") on successive rounds. For example, to activate the [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action, which moves you one step up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"), you must spend three [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions"). You can activate them all in the same round or spread them out over the course of multiple rounds. The [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action doesn't take effect until the last [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") is spent, but this system keeps you from being completely immobile and inactive for a round.

## Attacks and Damage

When it comes to the core of combat, not much has changed in *Saga Edition*. You still make an attack roll, which consists of your [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus"), your [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") or [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") bonus, and any other bonuses or penalties that apply. You now compare your attack roll result against the [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") of the target (Which replaces the traditional Defense Score) and, if successful, you deal your weapon's damage to the target. Little has changed fundamentally about how attacks are resolved.

In an effort to speed up the combat round, we made some changes that affect how any given round goes. First, characters no longer gain multiple attacks as they go up in levels, meaning that a character gets only one attack in a combat round, even at 20th level. There are a few exceptions to this restriction, such as fighting with two weapons, but they have their own balancing mechanisms.

Replacing iterative attacks are [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") such as [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") (which lets a character take a -2 penalty to their [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack") roll to gain +1 die of damage on their attack) and [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") (which lets them spend two [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") to gain +1 die of damage on a [Melee Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Melee_Attack "Melee Attack")). Since characters get more [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") in the *Saga Edition* rules, many characters will pick up [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") or [Mighty Swing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mighty_Swing "Mighty Swing") as Bonus Feats. So, the changes speed up combat by eliminating the resolution of multiple attacks, while still retaining the same effect.

Moreover, all heroic characters gain a bonus on their damage rolls equal to one-half their Heroic Level. This represents their general increase in proficiency and helps to ensure that higher-level heroes can consistently kill lower-level nonheroic enemies (such as light [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers")) in one shot. Additionally, this means a high-level enemy can challenge a lower-level party of heroes, emphasizing the difference in levels without adding two (or more) rolls per round of combat.

## Defend Yourself!

In *Saga Edition*, saving throws have been replaced by three static [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores").

[Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") represents your ability to avoid attacks, including [Area Attacks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attacks "Area Attacks") and [Hazards](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hazards "Hazards") that traditionally require Reflex Saves.

[Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") represents your general hardiness and ability to resist poison, disease, and environmental hazards. It's also integral to calculating your [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), which equals your [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") plus any bonuses provided by Armor or the [Improved Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Improved_Damage_Threshold "Improved Damage Threshold") Feat.

[Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense") represents your willpower and mental toughness, and is used to resist Mind-Affecting effects.

When another character or hazard attempts to affect you negatively in some way, it must make an attack roll or [Skill Check](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Check "Skill Check") against your relevant [Defense Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Score "Defense Score"). This puts much of the power in the attacker's hands and makes combat more proactive. An enemy can't steal the heroes' thunder by making a really great Reflex Save. Instead, it's up to the attacking heroes to overcome their foe's [Defense Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Score "Defense Score").

A character's [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores") are equal to 10 + their Heroic Level + the relevant [Ability](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability "Ability") modifier. Additionally, each class provides a small bonus to [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores"). For example, the [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") class provides a +1 class bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") and a +2 class bonus to [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"). A character wearing [Armor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor "Armor") replaces their level bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") with the armor's bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), and some suits of armor also provide an [Equipment](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Equipment "Equipment") bonus to [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"). However, a character's level bonus to [Defenses](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defenses "Defenses") will quickly outstrip their armor bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), which means that at higher levels, armor is not quite as integral.

Of course, some characters, such as [Jango Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jango_Fett "Jango Fett") and [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett"), might want to wear [Armor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor "Armor") throughout their career. To do so effectively, they'll need to spend character resources. The [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") has an [Armor Specialist Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor_Specialist_Talent_Tree "Armor Specialist Talent Tree") that allows them to wear armor, gaining benefits while sacrificing Talent Slots. For example, here are a pair of [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") from this [Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Tree "Talent Tree"):

- **Armored Defense:** When calculating your [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), you may add either your Heroic Level or your

Armor bonus, whichever is higher. You must be proficient with the [Armor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor "Armor") you are wearing to gain this
benefit.

- **Improved Armored Defense:** When calculating your [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), you may add your Heroic Level plus one-half your Armor bonus (rounded down) or your Armor bonus, whichever is higher. You must be proficient with the [Armor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Armor "Armor") you are wearing to gain this benefit.
  - *Prerequisite:* **Armored Defense**

## The Condition Track

When a creature, object, [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid"), or [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") takes damage that exceeds its [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), it moves one step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"). The switch from vitality/wound points to [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") allowed us to evaluate how damage affects a character as a whole, and dovetailed nicely with attempts to consolidate the various conditions (such as shaken, stunned, and so on) into a more usable form.

The end result is that being pushed down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") reduces your combat effectiveness when you take a significant hit. Though the first two steps on the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") are relatively minor, the third step (-5 to all [Defenses](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defenses "Defenses"), attack rolls, and skill checks) requires a hero to step back and recover a bit before rejoining the battle.

In addition, some characters have the ability to push someone up and down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") with other actions. For example, a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") who takes the right [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") from the [Jedi Consular Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Consular_Talent_Tree "Jedi Consular Talent Tree") can put the power of [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") behind their words and bump a character one step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") by making a [Persuasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persuasion "Persuasion") check that exceeds the target's [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense"). By the same token, a [Noble Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble_Talent "Noble Talent") allows the hero to bolster another ally, moving the target one step up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") by inspiring them to keep fighting.

Various effects can move a character up and down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"), and some even remove all negative conditions. Thus, a character's position on the track can change over the course of a round. Fortunately, they can move back up by taking the [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action. That moves them one step up the track, allowing them to get back in the fight on the next round. Similarly, a [Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") who takes the right [Talent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent "Talent") from the [Jedi Guardian Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Guardian_Talent_Tree "Jedi Guardian Talent Tree") can spend a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") to move *two* steps up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") as a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action").

In some cases, such as with exposure to poison, radiation, or debilitating damage, a character's position on the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") becomes [Persistent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent "Persistent"). This means that they can't take the [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action until they fulfill certain requirements. For example, a character who is knocked unconscious from damage might need 8 hours of bed rest or even [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery") before they can move back up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") and start to heal naturally.

## Healing, Unconsciousness, and Death

Any character who takes damage heals naturally with 8 hours of rest, regaining a number of [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") equal to their Character Level. Additionally, as a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") once per day, they can [Catch a Second Wind](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Catch_a_Second_Wind "Catch a Second Wind"), which allows them to regain a number of [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") equal to 1/4 of their total [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") or equal to their [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") score- whichever is greater. This rule allows characters to keep going between fights, even if they can't be healed anymore.

The [Treat Injury](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Treat_Injury "Treat Injury") Skill can still be used to heal wounded characters. A character can receive [First Aid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/First_Aid "First Aid") in this manner once per day; the number of [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") healed depends on the results of the [Skill Check](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Check "Skill Check"). If the heroes have an hour to spare, one character can perform [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery") on another, healing even more [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"). [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery") can be performed on a character more than once per day.

A character who is pushed to the bottom of the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") or reduced to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") falls unconscious. If an attack that reduces them to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") also exceeds their [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), they die. But even if they survive, being unconscious can be dangerous.

An unconscious character can take no [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") for 1 minute and then must make a DC 10 [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") check. If the check succeeds, they move one step up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") and regains a small number of [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), allowing them to rejoin the fight. However, if the check fails, their wounds are too grievous to allow them to keep acting, and their condition becomes [Persistent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent "Persistent"). Until they rest for 8 hours or has [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery"), the character can't gain [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") from [Natural Healing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Natural_Healing "Natural Healing") or use the [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action to move up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"). Furthermore, they must keep making [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") checks for each hour they are unconscious, and grievous failure results in death.

These new *Saga Edition* rules increase the value of long-term care, immersion in a [Bacta Tank](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bacta_Tank "Bacta Tank"), and characters who can perform [Surgery](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surgery "Surgery"). By the same token, the system allows heroes to get back up and fight if their wounds aren't too serious, and characters with the [Treat Injury](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Treat_Injury "Treat Injury") Skill can get their friends back in the action, even if their condition is [Persistent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent "Persistent").

*By Rodney Thompson*

The changes to the combat system in the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition* rulebook are extensive. "Prepare for Ground Assault" revealed some of the most basic changes, including the elimination of iterative attacks and the method for calculating defenses. This preview covers further changes to the combat system intended to make combat faster and more intuitive.

## Aiding Another

The basics of the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action remain the same in the new version of the rules. Characters still make checks against a DC of 10 to provide a +2 bonus on another character's action. However, two changes to the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") rules encourage cooperation in combat.

Characters can now use [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") to grant a +2 bonus to an ally's attack roll against a target as long as they both have [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") to the target. The target no longer needs to be engaged in melee combat with the ally.

Instead of using [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") to grant a bonus to an individual ally's [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores"), you can use it to lay down [Suppressing Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Suppressing_Fire "Suppressing Fire") against an opponent. As a result, the target takes a -2 penalty on attack rolls for 1 round.

These changes make the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action easier to use in combat, and they provide more legitimate options for characters that have difficulty damaging a target.

## Aiming

*Saga Edition* includes the ability to [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim"). When [Aiming](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aiming "Aiming") with a ranged weapon, you can take two consecutive [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") in a round to line up the shot carefully. On your next attack, you ignore all [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") bonuses to your target's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), provided that you don't lose [Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight") to your target or take any other [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions") before making your attack. Furthermore, you can enhance your attacks with the [Careful Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Careful_Shot "Careful Shot") Feat and the [Deadeye](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deadeye "Deadeye") Feat, which provide benefits such as increased attack bonuses and extra damage.

## Critical Hits

To speed up game play further, we simplified the way that [Critical Hits](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hits "Critical Hits") work. In *Saga Edition*, a Natural 20 is an automatic [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit") that deals double damage. All weapons default to this method of scoring a [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit"), though some [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") and [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") can augment Critical Hits in different ways. This change streamlines combat and encourages players to enjoy the rush of a Natural 20. Rather than being deflated by a poor roll to confirm a [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit"), players can enjoy their Natural 20 as an automatic success- and a big one at that.

Of course, the change also means that low-level enemies, such as [Stormtroopers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stormtroopers "Stormtroopers") and Battle Droids, can hit a character pretty hard, making a [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit") a true [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit") regardless of the attacker's level.

## Order the Retreat and Keep Moving!

Under the old rules, if a character in melee combat wanted to move away from an opponent, they have to spend their entire action disengaging and moving to safety. In *Saga Edition*, the disengage action has been replaced by the [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") Action, which uses slightly different mechanics.

[Withdrawing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdrawing "Withdrawing") is a [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action") that lets you move up to half your movement speed. Your first square of movement no longer provokes an [Attack of Opportunity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack_of_Opportunity "Attack of Opportunity"). However, you can [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") only if you are moving into a square that is not [Threatened](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Threatened "Threatened"); otherwise, you must take a normal [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action").

This change reflects the mobile and fluid nature of *Star Wars* combat. Characters should no longer feel as if they must choose between withdrawing and attacking, because now they can do both.

In addition, we changed some [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") slightly to make mobile combat more appealing. For example, consider the [Running Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Running_Attack "Running Attack") Feat:

### Running Attack

You can move as you attack.

*Prerequisite:* [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") 13

*Benefit:* When making an attack with a melee or ranged weapon, you can [Move](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move "Move") both before and after the attack, provided that your total distance moved is not greater than your [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed").

Multiple Feats (Shot on the Run and Spring Attack) have been condensed into a single Feat that has fewer prerequisites. This change encourages a style of combat often seen in the *Star Wars* films, in which mobile heroes dash in, attack, and keep moving.

Likewise, a character can now dash between two large objects that can be used for [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover"), fire their weapon between them, and retain their [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"). This should encourage heroes to be more mobile and take greater advantage of [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") than ever before.

## All-New Autofire

In previous editions of the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*, the [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") rules slowed down combat because they involved making extra rolls and referencing a massive table. In *Saga Edition*, we simplified the [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") rules, making them more like those seen in other d20 System games.

A character with an [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire")-capable weapon can switch to [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") mode by taking a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"). Then they target a 2-square-by-2-square area within the weapon's range and makes an attack roll with that weapon at a -5 penalty. Compare the result of the roll to the [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") of any targets within the area. Any creatures hit take full damage from the attack, while creatures missed take half damage. [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") requires a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") and consumes 10 shots, whether that represents charges from a [Power Pack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Power_Pack "Power Pack") or slugs from a slugthrower.

Some special abilities reduce the penalties on [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") attacks, while others provide new uses for the [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") rules. For example, the [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire") Feat allows you to use a weapon set on [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") against a single target at the normal -5 penalty. If you hit the target, you deal an extra 2 dice of damage. Since the attack isn't an [Area Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Area_Attack "Area Attack"), it doesn't deal half damage on a miss, and the damage can't be reduced with the Scout's **[Evasion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Evasion "Evasion")** Talent.

## Stun and Ion Damage

[Stun Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun_Damage "Stun Damage") and [Ion Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion_Damage "Ion Damage") both received overhauls so they'd work better with the new [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") and [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") rules.

If a living target is hit by a [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") attack, compare the [Stun Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun_Damage "Stun Damage") (which is now the same as the weapon's normal damage) to the target's [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"). If the [Stun Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun_Damage "Stun Damage") exceeds the [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), the target moves 2 steps down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"); otherwise, the attack has no additional effect. Half of the [Stun Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun_Damage "Stun Damage") is then subtracted from the target's Hit Points. To use a [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") weapon, a character must be within 6 squares of their target (except when they're using [Stun Grenades](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun_Grenades "Stun Grenades"), which create a radius effect).

[Ion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion "Ion") weapons work much the same way, except they affect only [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids"), [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles"), objects, and characters with cybernetic enhancements. In addition, [Ion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion "Ion") weapons can be fired at normal ranges and are not subject to the same 6-square limitation as [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") weapons.

## Reserve Shield Rules

We revised the shield rules to make them more consistent with what we see in the *Star Wars* movies (and to enhance the [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") combat rules, which will be covered in "Starship and Vehicle Combat"). Instead of having Shield Points, which work like reserve [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), all shielded objects now have a [Shield Rating](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Shield_Rating "Shield Rating") (SR) that acts like [Damage Reduction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Reduction "Damage Reduction") against incoming attacks. For example, a Droideka has SR 20, which means that its shields absorb 20 points of damage much in the same way that DR absorbs damage. However, unlike [Damage Reduction](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Reduction "Damage Reduction"), when an attack exceeds the target's [Shield Rating](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Shield_Rating "Shield Rating"), its SR is reduced by 5. [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") and [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") with shields can take actions to recharge their shields to their normal maximum.

The new rules mean that a single lucky shot will no longer eliminate a target's shields. Instead, knocking out shields will require several hits, or even a constant bombardment by multiple attackers.

## High-Level Combat

Taken together, the changes described above- plus other changes not covered in this preview- make combat faster and more like the action of the *Star Wars* movies. As an added benefit, combat runs much faster at higher level, too. Since most characters have only one attack per round, most rounds consist of no more than three [Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Actions "Actions"). A 20th-level character's turn lasts just about as long as a 1st-level character's turn (accounting for a greater number of options at higher levels, of course). Moreover, enemies using the [Nonheroic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonheroic "Nonheroic") class continue to act as cannon fodder even at high levels, letting heroes face threats that can be taken down in one or two hits even as they come face to face with 20th-level Sith Lords.

*By Rodney Thompson*

When we revamped the rules for [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") for the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition*, one of our primary goals was to make it easier to play heroic [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") characters. Just as [C-3PO](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/C-3PO "C-3PO") and [R2-D2](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-D2 "R2-D2") are present throughout the *Star Wars* saga, players should have the option of bringing their own interesting [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") into the campaign's story.

Of course, [Droid Heroes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Heroes "Droid Heroes") present several roleplaying challenges. The *Star Wars* universe treats [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") as property, sometimes for comedic value, and sometimes such that [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") become more like background elements than story motivators.

## Droid Traits

As in previous editions of the game, all [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") share common traits that define how they differ from living characters. [Droid Traits](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Traits "Droid Traits") are similar to Species Traits, an intentional design choice that makes it easier to envision a [Droid Hero](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Hero "Droid Hero"). In essence, "[Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid")" becomes the character's Species, just as "[Mon Calamari](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mon_Calamari "Mon Calamari")" or "[Zabrak](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Zabrak "Zabrak")" are Species choices.

All [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") share the following [Droid Traits](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Traits "Droid Traits"):

- **Ability Modifiers:** [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") are nonliving entities, so they do not have [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") scores. [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") can increase any two of their five remaining [Ability Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Scores "Ability Scores") by +1 each at 4th level and every four levels thereafter, just like any other character. These increases represent improved heuristics and algorithms that the [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") has developed from experience as well as upgrades to its components undertaken as part of routine maintenance. Droid [Ability](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability "Ability") modifiers are determined by their degree and size. A [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") can never have an [Ability Score](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Score "Ability Score") less than 1, regardless of modifications.
- **Automatic Languages:** All [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") can speak, read, and process [Binary](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Binary "Binary"), as well as understand one language chosen by the designer (usually Basic).
- **Behavioral Inhibitors:** [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") (except [4th-Degree Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/4th-Degree_Droids "4th-Degree Droids")) cannot intentionally harm a sentient living being or knowingly allow a sentient living being to be harmed. Furthermore, all [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") must follow orders given to them by their rightful owners, as long as those orders don't require the [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") to harm a sentient living being. [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") with [Heuristic Processors](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heuristic_Processors "Heuristic Processors") can sometimes violate these restrictions by creatively interpreting their behavioral inhibitors.
- **Ion Damage Vulnerability:** As electronic constructs, [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") are vulnerable to damage from [Ion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion "Ion") weapons. Generally, [Ion](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ion "Ion") weapons have the same effects on [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") that [Stun](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stun "Stun") weapons have on living beings.
- **Maintenance:** [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") do not sleep, eat, or breath. However, they do need to enter shutdown mode and recharge for 1 hour after 100 hours of operation. If a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") fails to do so, it must make an [Endurance](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Endurance "Endurance") check each hour (DC 10, +1 per additional hour after the first) or move -1 [Persistent](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent "Persistent") step along the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"). This [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") can only be removed by the [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") recharging for 1 hour.
- **Memory:** A Droid's Trained [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), and [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") can be reassigned with the [Use Computer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") skill (see [Reprogram Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reprogram_Droid?action=edit&redlink=1 "Reprogram Droid (page does not exist)")). A [Droid Hero](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Hero "Droid Hero") can use its own [Use Computer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") Skill to perform this reprogramming, but it takes a -5 penalty on its [Skill Check](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Check "Skill Check"). If a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is ever subjugated to a complete [Memory Wipe](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Memory_Wipe "Memory Wipe"), it becomes a basic model of its type, losing any levels and abilities gained.
- **Nonliving:** A [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is immune to [Poison](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Poison "Poison"), [Disease](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Disease "Disease"), [Radiation](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Radiation "Radiation"), noncorrosive [Atmosphere](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Atmosphere "Atmosphere") Hazards, [Vacuum](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vacuum "Vacuum"), Mind-Affecting effects, [Stunning](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Stunning "Stunning") effects, and any other effect that only works on living targets. [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") have no connection to [The Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Force "The Force") and can't gain the [Force Sensitivity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Sensitivity "Force Sensitivity") Feat, or learn [Force Powers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Powers "Force Powers"). [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") do not have a [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") score, so they don't get bonus [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") for having a high [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution"), and they apply their [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") modifier to their [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"). Unlike living beings, [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") don't "die," but they can be disabled or destroyed.
  - If a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is reduced to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), it is disabled and cannot be reactivated until it is repaired so that it has at least 1 [Hit Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Point "Hit Point"). If the attack that reduced the [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") also exceeds the Droid's [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), the [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is destroyed instead. A destroyed [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") cannot be repaired or salvaged. [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") do not gain an ability bonus to [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution")-based [Skill Checks](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Checks "Skill Checks") and may not take [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") or [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents") with a [Constitution](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Constitution "Constitution") prerequisite.
- **Repair:** [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") can regain lost [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") only through the use of the [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") Skill (see [Repair](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Repair "Repair")). A [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") can use this application to repair itself, but it takes a -5 penalty on its [Skill Check](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill_Check "Skill Check").
- **Shut Down:** A [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") that is Shut Down can take no actions and is effectively unconscious. Shutting down a willing [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action"). Shutting down an unwilling [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") is more difficult, requiring you to [Grab](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grab "Grab") the [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid"), and then make a [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") check (DC = Droid's [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense")) as a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") while its [Grabbed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grabbed "Grabbed"). You cannot shut down an unwilling [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") with [Locked Access](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Locked_Access "Locked Access") unless it is disabled or otherwise [Helpless](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Helpless "Helpless").
- **Skills:** [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") normally cannot use any [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") Untrained except for [Acrobatics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatics "Acrobatics"), [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb"), [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump"), and [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception"). A [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") with a [Heuristic Processor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heuristic_Processor "Heuristic Processor") ignores this limitation.
- **Systems:** [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") can have many of their characteristics changed by installing or replacing existing [Droid Systems](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Systems "Droid Systems").

## Playing an Existing Model

Players who want to run [Droid Heroes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Heroes "Droid Heroes") might wish to choose an iconic [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") type. The [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") chapter of the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* presents [R2-Series Astromech Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-Series_Astromech_Droids "R2-Series Astromech Droids"), [3PO-Series Protocol Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/3PO-Series_Protocol_Droids "3PO-Series Protocol Droids"), and a number of other standard [Droid Models](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Models "Droid Models"). Though not all [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") in the book are suitable for heroic play, a great many of them are.

In terms of game mechanics, this option is very similar to the rules in previous editions. The specific [Droid Model](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Model "Droid Model") you choose determines your [Ability Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Scores "Ability Scores"), size, class, and level. If you choose a [Droid Model](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Model "Droid Model") that has 1 or 2 levels in the [Nonheroic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonheroic "Nonheroic") class, you can add 1 level of a [Heroic Class](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Class "Heroic Class") of your choice. Otherwise, you can play a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") with 3 [Nonheroic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonheroic "Nonheroic") levels or 1 Heroic level, and character generation proceeds as normal.

## Playing a New Model

In *Saga Edition*, you can also create a new custom [Droid Model](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Model "Droid Model"), choosing its [Ability Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Scores "Ability Scores"), [Heroic Classes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heroic_Classes "Heroic Classes"), [Skills](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skills "Skills"), [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), and [Droid Systems](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Systems "Droid Systems"). This option allows you to set the Droid Model's [Ability Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Scores "Ability Scores"), select its "degree" (a designation based on its role), and give it weapons and equipment that define the equivalent of a new stock model of that [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid").

For example, let's say you want to play a combat-oriented [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid"). You could make use of the statistics for the [B1-Series Battle Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/B1-Series_Battle_Droid "B1-Series Battle Droid") or [B2-Series Super Battle Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/B2-Series_Super_Battle_Droid "B2-Series Super Battle Droid"), but that might not fit your vision. Instead, you can create a new [Droid Model](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Model "Droid Model"), assign [Ability Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ability_Scores "Ability Scores") that are geared more closely to your desired role (in this case, you'd probably give the [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") high [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") and [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") scores), pick a standard class (such as [Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier")), and continue with character generation as normal. The end result- the 1st-level character that you create- becomes a new stock model of [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid"), and your particular character becomes a heroic individual [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") that has a role to play in galactic events.

## Tinkering With Your Droid

The *Saga Edition* rules make it simpler and more logical to tinker with [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids"). To modify a Droid's hardware, characters (including [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") themselves) use the [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") Skill. To reprogram a Droid's software, they turn to the [Use Computer](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_Computer "Use Computer") Skill. In keeping with the goal of making [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") more suitable for heroic use, [Reprogramming](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reprogramming "Reprogramming") a [Droid](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid "Droid") requires a character to overcome the Droid's [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense")- a sign that you have penetrated the Droid's software defenses. This ensures that [Droid Heroes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droid_Heroes "Droid Heroes") are more resistant to [Reprogramming](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reprogramming "Reprogramming") than stock models.

*By Rodney Thompson*

Now that you've seen previews of basic and advanced combat, let's put it all together to see how a scene might unfold. This preview breaks down the skirmish at the Great Pit of Carkoon from *Return of the Jedi*, when [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") rescued his friends from the clutches of the vile gangster [Jabba the Hutt](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jabba_the_Hutt "Jabba the Hutt").

This combat example focuses on the events taking place outside during the battle. The interior struggles, including [Princess Leia](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Princess_Leia "Princess Leia") strangling the bloated [Hutt](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hutt "Hutt"), are taking place at the same time, but to keep this example simple, we left them out (though Leia does enter the scene in round 12). Additionally, all of Jabba the Hutt's thugs use the generic [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") statistics from the *[Core Rulebook](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Core_Rulebook "Core Rulebook")* and don't differentiate between the [Species](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Species "Species").

## Dramatis Personae

**[Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker"):** [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") 1/[Jedi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi "Jedi") 7/[Ace Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ace_Pilot "Ace Pilot") 2/[Jedi Knight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Knight "Jedi Knight") 1, 99 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") 26, [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") 25, [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") +14 (2d8+11)

**[Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo"):** [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") 5/[Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") 2/[Ace Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ace_Pilot "Ace Pilot") 3/[Gunslinger](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gunslinger "Gunslinger") 2, 77 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") 28, [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") 25, [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") +10 (1d4+7)

**[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca"):** [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") 6/[Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") 4, 110 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") 24, [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") 27, [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") +11 (1d6+9)

**[Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian"):** [Scoundrel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scoundrel "Scoundrel") 8, 50 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") 22, [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") 19, [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") +7 (1d4+5)

**[Princess Leia](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Princess_Leia "Princess Leia"):** [Noble](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Noble "Noble") 8/[Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") 2, 63 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") 23, [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") 23, [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") +9 (1d4+6)

**[Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett"):** [Scout](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Scout "Scout") 3/[Soldier](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Soldier "Soldier") 5/[Bounty Hunter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Bounty_Hunter "Bounty Hunter") 5/[Elite Trooper](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Elite_Trooper "Elite Trooper") 2, 122 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") 34, [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") 33, [Blaster Carbine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Carbine "Blaster Carbine") +18 (3d8+7), [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol") +18 (3d6+7), [Whipcord](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Whipcord?action=edit&redlink=1 "Whipcord (page does not exist)") +18 ([Grab](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grab "Grab"))

**Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs"):** [Nonheroic](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nonheroic "Nonheroic") 2, 9 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") 10, [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") 11, [Vibro-Axe](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibro-Axe "Vibro-Axe") +2 (2d10+2), [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol") +1 (3d6)

Luke begins the combat encounter when he jumps off of the plank above the [Sarlacc Pit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Sarlacc_Pit "Sarlacc Pit"). He makes an [Acrobatics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Acrobatics "Acrobatics") check, rolling a 12 for a result of 20 (well above the required DC 15 to move past a threatening enemy), and finishes his flip on the opposite side of the [Weequay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weequay "Weequay") Thug that had attempted to push him off.

## Surprise Round

The action begins with Luke, Lando, and [R2-D2](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-D2 "R2-D2") acting on the [Surprise Round](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surprise_Round "Surprise Round"), since they're the only ones aware of the surprise Luke has planned.

- [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") rolls a 16 on [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative"), for a total of 29.
- [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") rolls a 19 on [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative"), for a total of 25.
- [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") rolls an 8 on [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative"), for a total of 24.
- [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") rolls a 14 on [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative"), for a total of 20.
- [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") rolls a 5 on [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative"), but takes a -2 penalty from his [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition"), for a total of 19.
- Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") roll a 10 on [Initiative](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative "Initiative"), for a total of 16.

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 29 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 27 | [R2-D2](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-D2 "R2-D2") | Normal |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 24 | [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") | Normal |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 16 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |

Luke [Readies](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readies "Readies") an attack with his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") against the [Klatooinian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Klatooinian "Klatooinian") Thug adjacent to him, once he receives his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber").

[R2-D2](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-D2 "R2-D2") fires Luke's [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") to him on [Initiative Count](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative_Count "Initiative Count") 27 as a [Readied Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_Action "Readied Action") (rolling a 9 on his [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack"), which even with a -5 penalty for range results in a 13, well more than the [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") of 10 needed to hit Luke's square). This moves Luke's [Initiative Count](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Initiative_Count "Initiative Count") down to 27 as it triggers his [Readied Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_Action "Readied Action"). Next round, R2 moves belowdeck to help [C-3PO](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/C-3PO "C-3PO").

Luke ignites his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") with a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") and his [Readied](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied "Readied") attack begins. He rolls a 12 on his attack roll (for a total of 21). This hits the [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug"), and Luke rolls a total of 19 points of damage, depleting the Thug's [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") and exceeding his [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), killing him. The [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") tumbles over the side of the skiff and into the waiting mouth of the Sarlacc.

Lando initiates a [Grab](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grab "Grab") attack against the [Nikto](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nikto "Nikto") pilot of the skiff. He rolls a 12, which gives him a total of 14 after applying the -5 penalty for [Grabbing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grabbing "Grabbing"), and successfully [Grabs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grabs "Grabs") the pilot.

## Round 1

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 27 | [R2-D2](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-D2 "R2-D2") | Normal |
| 27 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 24 | [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") | Normal |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
