Luke chooses to take a full round to use the [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") Feat against a [Human](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Human "Human") Thug and a [Weequay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weequay "Weequay") Thug. Applying a -5 penalty to both attack rolls, he rolls an 11 and a 13, for totals of 20 and 22 respectively, more than enough to hit each [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug"). His damage rolls are 20 and 16, which is enough to kill both [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs"). The attacks send them tumbling off the side of the skiff, providing even more food for the Sarlacc below.

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca"), still bound, takes no action.

[Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") moves onto the deck of Jabba's sail barge.

Lando continues to [Grab](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grab "Grab") the [Nikto](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nikto "Nikto") pilot.

Han, still bound, takes no action.

The [Nikto](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nikto "Nikto") pilot attempts to make an attack against Lando, taking a -2 penalty to his attack roll for being [Grabbed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grabbed "Grabbed"). He rolls a 17, for a total attack bonus of 16. The blaster shot fires off harmlessly into the air.

## Round 2

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 27 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 25 | Sail Barge Gunner | Normal |
| 24 | [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") | Normal |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |

Luke moves to [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") and undoes his binders.

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") removes Han's binders.

A [Delaying](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delaying "Delaying") Thug on Jabba's sail barge fires a [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") at Lando, rolling a Natural 20 and dealing 50 points of damage. Since this would normally kill Lando outright, he spends a [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") to make the attack miss. The attack misses, but because the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") is a [Splash](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Splash "Splash") weapon, Lando still takes half damage. That reduces his [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") by half (to 25) and exceeds his [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), moving him -1 step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"). The attack roll also exceeds the [Nikto](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nikto "Nikto") pilot's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), dealing 25 points of damage to him. Since Lando spent a [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") to have the attack miss, the GM rules that the dying [Nikto](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Nikto "Nikto") drags Lando over the side of the skiff, falling into the Sarlacc's gullet and leaving Lando hanging onto the side of the skiff. Additionally, the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") damages the skiff itself, moving it -1 step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track").

[Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") uses his [Jet Pack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jet_Pack "Jet Pack") to fly from the sail barge to the skiff.

Lando uses two [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") to begin the [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action, then attempts a [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check to pull himself up onto the skiff. Lando rolls a 9, for a total of 14, just shy of the DC 15 needed to pull himself back up.

Han makes a [Perception](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Perception "Perception") check to search for some kind of weapon he can use. He rolls a 12, for a total of 24, locating a [Vibro-Axe](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibro-Axe "Vibro-Axe") that has been discarded on the deck of the skiff.

## Round 3

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 27 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 24 | [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") | Normal |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | -1 |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 16 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |

Luke swings his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") in an attempt to destroy Boba Fett's [Blaster Carbine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Carbine "Blaster Carbine"). He rolls a Natural 20, automatically striking the weapon and dealing double damage. The 44 points of damage he deals is more than enough to destroy the weapon even without the Lightsaber's ability to ignore DR, slicing it neatly in half.

A blaster shot from the Sail Barge strikes Chewie, dealing 12 points of damage to the [Wookiee](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Wookiee "Wookiee"). [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") attempts to protect his friend by knocking him [Prone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prone "Prone"). Han does not resist, and the two fall to the deck of the skiff.

[Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") uses his [Whipcord](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Whipcord?action=edit&redlink=1 "Whipcord (page does not exist)") to make a [Grab](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grab "Grab") attack against Luke. He rolls a 13, for a total of 31, which is well above Luke's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), so Luke is [Grabbed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grabbed "Grabbed").

Lando completes his [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action with a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action"), and calls out to Han or Chewie for help. Given the difficulty he faces in climbing, he doesn't want to risk falling if he fails a check by 5 or more, since the Sarlacc waits below to devour him.

Han allows Chewie to continue to cover him from blaster fire.

The [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") on the Sail Barge fires the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") at Luke, rolling a total of 12, which would not normally strike Luke. However, Luke uses the opportunity to **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** at [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett"). He rolls a 2 on his [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check, which still results in an 18 and allows him to deflect the Blaster Cannon's shot. Luke then makes a [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack") roll (at a -2 penalty from being [Grabbed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grabbed "Grabbed")) and rolls an 18. Though this is a good roll, Luke knows that [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") is likely to be a tough target to strike, so he spends a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point"), rolls 2d6 (getting a 2 and a 6), and adds 6 to the result, for a total attack of 34. This is exactly what he needs to strike [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") with the Blaster Cannon's shot, so damage is rolled. The [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") shot deals an incredible 87 points of damage to [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett"), moving him -1 step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") and knocking him [Prone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prone "Prone"). Again, the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") damages the skiff itself, moving it another -1 step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track").

A second skiff arrives, loaded with 5 [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") and a pilot. Each of the [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") fires at Luke, but none of them roll high enough to beat his [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), and he remains unharmed.

## Round 4

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 27 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 24 | [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") | -1 |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 16 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |

Luke tries to jump onto the approaching skiff. He makes a [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump") check and rolls 12, for a total of 19. Since the DC to make the 2-square jump was an 18, Luke makes it- but only barely. Rather than risk losing a chance to attack some of the thugs, Luke [Readies an Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readies_an_Action "Readies an Action") to use his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") to attack any [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") that comes within his [Threatened Area](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Threatened_Area "Threatened Area").

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") begins moving across the skiff toward Lando while still protecting Han and warning him about [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett").

[Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") drops [Prone](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Prone "Prone") to gain a +5 bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") against the attacks coming from the Sail Barge's [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon"), and uses two [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") to begin the [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action.

Lando continues to call for help, hoping that Han or Chewie will use the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to help him climb.

Han picks up a [Vibro-Axe](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibro-Axe "Vibro-Axe"). "[Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett")? [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett")? Where?"

More [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") fire at Luke, but his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") blade whirls to fend off attacks, so none comes close enough to harm him.

One [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") attempts to get close enough to [Grab](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Grab "Grab") Luke. This triggers Luke's [Readied Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_Action "Readied Action"), and he rolls a 19 on his attack roll (for a total of 33) and a 22 for damage, killing the [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") and sending him into the Great Pit of Carkoon.

## Round 5

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 24 | [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") | -1 |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 16 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |
| 15 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") chooses to [Fight Defensively](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fight_Defensively "Fight Defensively") and opts to take no attacks, granting himself a +5 dodge bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") as he tries to protect Han.

[Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") stands up, takes a [Swift Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Action "Swift Action") to finish [Recovering](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recovering "Recovering") and fires a shot at Luke with his wrist blaster. Unfortunately, he rolls only a 4, for a total of 22, not enough to exceed Luke's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense").

Lando decides to use his **[Fortune's Favor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortune%27s_Favor "Fortune's Favor")** Talent. He spends a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") and applies a +5 bonus on his skill checks for the duration of the encounter, hoping that it will prevent him from falling into the Sarlacc pit even if he rolls badly on a [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check.

Han decides to spend a [Destiny Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Destiny_Point "Destiny Point") to make an attack roll against [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett"), given their history and the dire nature of the situation. This automatically grants Han a [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit") against [Boba Fett](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Boba_Fett "Boba Fett") with the [Vibro-Axe](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibro-Axe "Vibro-Axe"), dealing 36 points of damage after adding his heroic damage bonus and doubling the damage from the [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit"). Thanks to Luke deflecting the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") shot at the bounty hunter earlier, the damage is sufficient to reduce him to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"). It also exceeds Boba Fett's [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), meaning that he is killed. The bounty hunter's [Jet Pack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jet_Pack "Jet Pack") misfires and he streaks across the battlefield, crashes into the side of the sail barge, and plummets into the waiting mouth of the Sarlacc. (However, Expanded Universe sources would claim that Fett spent a [Force Point](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Point "Force Point") to keep from dying, and that the Sarlacc found him "somewhat indigestible.")

The [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") remaining on the skiff fire at Luke, and all miss.

Luke makes an [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") attack against a [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug"), rolling a 6 for a total of 17, and dealing 11 points of damage. Luke's kick sends the [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") tumbling off the side of the skiff and into the Sarlacc's beak.

## Round 6

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 16 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |
| 15 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") moves to the edge of the skiff and successfully uses the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to grant Lando a +2 bonus on his next [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check.

Lando attempts a [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check but rolls a Natural 1, for a total of 14. Thanks to his use of the **[Fortune's Favor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortune%27s_Favor "Fortune's Favor")** Talent, Lando doesn't fall into the Sarlacc Pit.

Han moves to the edge of the skiff and successfully uses the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to grant Lando a +2 bonus on his next [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check.

The [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") remaining on the skiff fire at Luke, and all miss.

Luke rolls a 9 on an attack against a [Weequay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weequay "Weequay") Thug for a total of 23, dealing 19 points of damage. The [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") flies off the side of the skiff to be consumed by the Sarlacc below.

## Round 7

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 16 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |
| 15 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") successfully uses the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to grant Lando a +2 bonus on his next [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check.

Lando rolls a 13 on his [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check for a total of 27, which lets him climb most of the way up to the skiff with the help of his friends.

Han successfully uses the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to grant Lando a +2 bonus on his next [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check, which should get him up onto the deck of the sail barge.

The [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") on the skiff with Luke move in to attack him with [Vibro-Axes](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibro-Axes "Vibro-Axes"). They all miss.

With multiple [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") in range, Luke uses the [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") Feat to strike two adjacent [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs"). With attack rolls of 6 and 15 resulting in a 15 and a 24 respectively, each [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") is struck. Damage rolls of 18 and 22 are enough to kill the two [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs"), who fall into the sands of the Great Pit of Carkoon.

The [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") on the sail barge manning the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") sees Lando being pulled up onto the skiff and fires at the vehicle. The [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") shot severely damages the skiff, bumping it -1 step further down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"). Since this puts the skiff at the -5 step on the track, it tilts and wavers, spilling Han over the side and causing Lando to lose the progress he had made. Fortunately, Han, Chewie, and Lando all make the DC 15 [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check (accounting for favorable circumstances) to catch themselves and keep them from falling down into the Sarlacc Pit.

## Round 8

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 20 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 15 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 13 | Sail Barge Gunner | Normal |

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") uses the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to grant Han a +2 bonus on his next [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check.

Lando [Delays](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delays "Delays"), waiting for Han to be pulled up.

Han makes a [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check, rolling a 19 and pulling himself back up onto the skiff safely.

Lando makes a [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check, rolling a 3 for a result of 13, making no progress.

At this point, no more [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") remain on the skiff (and [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") inside the sail barge are [Delaying](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delaying "Delaying")), so Luke uses his *[Surge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Surge "Surge")* Force Power to leap over to the side of the sail barge. Luke rolls a 16 on his [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") check for a total of 28, granting himself a +30 bonus on his [Jump](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jump "Jump") check. Luke rolls a 10 for a total of 47, allowing him to jump 15 meters (or 10 squares), which just gets him to the side of the sail barge.

A [Weequay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weequay "Weequay") Thug with a [Readied Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Readied_Action "Readied Action") opens a hatch on the side of the sail barge and moves out to attack Luke, but misses.

Luke finishes his action by making an [Unarmed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Unarmed "Unarmed") attack against the [Weequay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weequay "Weequay") (which provokes an [Attack of Opportunity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack_of_Opportunity "Attack of Opportunity") from the [Weequay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weequay "Weequay"), who misses), rolling a 14 for a total of 25. Luke deals 11 points of damage, tossing him down into the sands of [Tatooine](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Tatooine "Tatooine").

The [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") manning the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") aboard the sail barge fires at the skiff again, doing minimal damage to the vehicle and none to Han, [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca"), or Lando.

## Round 9

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 25 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 18 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 15 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 14 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |
| 6 | Sarlacc | Normal |

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") [Delays](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delays "Delays") to give Han time to help Lando.

Han leans over the side of the skiff to extend a [Vibro-Axe](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibro-Axe "Vibro-Axe") to Lando, aiding him on his [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check.

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") takes his action to hold Han's feet as he hangs off the side of the skiff.

Now that Lando is further down into the Sarlacc Pit, the DC of his [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check has increased to 20. Lando attempts to [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") once more, rolling a 7. This results in a [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check total of 19, not enough to make any progress, but not causing Lando to slip further down, either.

Luke moves the rest of the way up the side of the sail barge and attacks the gunner manning the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon"). Luke rolls a 15 for a total of 29, dealing 20 points of damage to the [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") and killing him with a slash from his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber").

Two [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") attempt to shoot Luke, who chooses to **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")** them both. Their attack rolls are a 17 and a 3, for totals of 18 and 4. Luke rolls a 7 and a 14 on his [Use the Force](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Use_the_Force "Use the Force") checks to **[Deflect](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Deflect "Deflect")**, resulting in a 23 and a 25 respectively (the latter accounting for the -5 penalty for the second deflection). Additionally, Luke chooses to **[Redirect Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Redirect_Shot "Redirect Shot")** on one, rolling a 7 on his [Ranged Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ranged_Attack "Ranged Attack") roll for a total of 19. This strikes a [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") and deals 16 points of damage, killing the [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug"). The second [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") flees in fear.

A [Gamorrean](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gamorrean "Gamorrean") Thug attempts to move around behind Luke while the Jedi is distracted, provoking an [Attack of Opportunity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack_of_Opportunity "Attack of Opportunity") as he moves out of one of Luke's [Threatened](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Threatened "Threatened") squares. Luke rolls a 10 on his [Attack of Opportunity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack_of_Opportunity "Attack of Opportunity") and cuts the [Gamorrean](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gamorrean "Gamorrean") down with 26 points of damage from his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber").

Sensing that food is close, the Sarlacc attempts to grab Lando with one of its tentacles. Since Lando is hanging from the side of the skiff, he loses his [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") bonus to [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), and the Sarlacc's roll of 18 is more than enough to grab the gambler by the leg.

## Round 10

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 19 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |
| 19 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 18 | [Lando Calrissian](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lando_Calrissian "Lando Calrissian") | Normal |
| 15 | [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") | Normal |
| 14 | Jabba the Hutt's [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") | Normal |

Han [Delays](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delays "Delays") until Chewbacca can hand him a [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol") from the deck of the skiff.

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") holds onto Han's feet as he hands the [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol") over.

Han takes the gun from [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") as a [Move Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Action "Move Action") and uses two [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") to [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") at the tentacle, keeping Lando from providing it with [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover").

Lando [Delays](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delays "Delays"), waiting for Han to shoot the Sarlacc's tentacle. "No, wait, I thought you were blind!"

Luke [Delays](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delays "Delays") to prepare for more enemies to arrive.

Some [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") come up from the inside of the sail barge and move near Luke, firing their blasters and missing.

Luke stops [Delaying](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delaying "Delaying") and uses the [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack") Feat on a [Weequay](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weequay "Weequay") and a [Human](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Human "Human") that have moved into his [Threatened Area](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Threatened_Area "Threatened Area"). Luke rolls a 9 and an 18, resulting in attack totals of 18 and 27 respectively, both of which strike the [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs"). The [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") deals 22 and 18 points of damage, split between the two [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs"), dropping them both with a few quick strikes.

## Round 11

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 18 | [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") | Normal |
| 18 | [Han Solo](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Han_Solo "Han Solo") | -2, [Persistent Condition](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Persistent_Condition "Persistent Condition") |

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") continues to hold onto Han's feet.

Han fires his [Blaster Pistol](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Pistol "Blaster Pistol"), rolling a 10 for a total of 22, which is enough to hit the Sarlacc's tentacle. The shot deals 27 points of damage, causing the tentacle to release Lando's leg and retract back into the pit.

Lando makes a [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check, rolling a 17 for a result of 27. He makes it most of the way up to the skiff's deck.

Several more [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") move onto the deck of the sail barge.

Luke attacks one Thug's [Vibro-Axe](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vibro-Axe "Vibro-Axe"), rolling a 14 for a total of 28. This is well more than the number needed to attack the weapon (21), and the Jedi deals 23 points of damage to the weapon, slicing it in half.

[Princess Leia](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Princess_Leia "Princess Leia") moves up onto the deck of the sail barge, then follows Luke's instructions to "Get the gun!" and moves over to a large [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") mounted on the deck.

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") begins making repairs to the skiff. He moves over to one of the damaged repulsors and prepares to [Jury-Rig](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jury-Rig "Jury-Rig") some repairs.

Han successfully uses the [Aid Another](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aid_Another "Aid Another") Action to assist Lando on his [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check.

Lando rolls a 9 on his [Climb](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Climb "Climb") check for a total of 21, allowing him to climb the rest of the way up onto the deck of the skiff.

Two remaining [Thugs](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thugs "Thugs") take the [Withdraw](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Withdraw "Withdraw") Action to run away from Luke.

One [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") continues to fire at Luke, rolling a Natural 20 on his attack roll. The [Critical Hit](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Critical_Hit "Critical Hit") deals 16 points of damage to Luke as a blaster bolt strikes him on his artificial hand.

Luke moves to the [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") that just shot him and makes an attack with his [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber"). With an attack roll of 7 that results in a 21, Luke cuts down the [Thug](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Thug "Thug") with 25 points of damage.

[R2-D2](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/R2-D2 "R2-D2") and [C-3PO](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/C-3PO "C-3PO") move onto the deck of the sail barge and fall off the side, landing safely in the sand after taking 10 points of [Falling Damage](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Falling_Damage "Falling Damage").

[Princess Leia](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Princess_Leia "Princess Leia") manipulates the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon"), rotating it around to point at the sail barge's deck.

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") makes a [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") roll to [Jury-Rig](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jury-Rig "Jury-Rig") the skiff so they can escape. He rolls a 10, but even with his +10 [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") bonus, he knows he won't make the DC 25 needed for jury-rigged repairs. Fortunately, [Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") has the **[Jury-Rigger](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jury-Rigger "Jury-Rigger")** Talent, so he uses it to reroll his [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") check, this time rolling an 18, resulting in a total of 28. The skiff moves +2 steps up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track") to the -1 position, which should be good enough for the heroes to escape.

Han moves over to take [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") on the skiff.

Lando moves to the skiff pilot's position and prepares to take off.

Luke moves over to grab a rope hanging from the canopy of the sail barge, motioning for Leia to join him.

## Round 14

| INITIATIVE | CHARACTER | CONDITION |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 23 | [Princess Leia](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Princess_Leia "Princess Leia") | Normal |

[Princess Leia](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Princess_Leia "Princess Leia") moves over to the rope as well, grabbing hold of Luke.

[Chewbacca](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Chewbacca "Chewbacca") moves to take control of the skiff's magnetic grapplers.

Lando [Delays](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Delays "Delays"), waiting on his companions.

Luke fires the [Blaster Cannon](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Blaster_Cannon "Blaster Cannon") at the sail barge, sending powerful bolts of energy into the vehicle's core. Then he and Leia swing on the rope across the Great Pit of Carkoon, landing safely on the skiff where their friends await.

Lando makes a [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") check, rolling a 13 for a result of 29, which is enough to increase the skiff's [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed") by an additional 2 squares, allowing them to pick up the [Droids](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Droids "Droids") and flee to safety as Jabba's sail barge explodes in a ball of fire in the sands behind them.

*By Rodney Thompson*

From the opening scenes of *A New Hope*, [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") have played an integral role in the *Star Wars* saga. For players of the *Star Wars Roleplaying Game*, [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") represent a chance to get into the cockpit of an [X-Wing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/X-Wing "X-Wing") and perform the same kinds of amazing feats that [Luke Skywalker](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Luke_Skywalker "Luke Skywalker") did when he blew up [The Death Star](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/The_Death_Star "The Death Star"). For the *Saga Edition* rules, we revised and streamlined [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat") to integrate it better with the combat rules for ground [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") and for characters.

To encourage players to make the transition between [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat") and [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat"), the [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") rules have been simplified to use many of the same mechanics as [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat"). Movement and attacks work in much the same way as they do at [Character Scale](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Scale "Character Scale"), and pilots and gunners can use many of the same actions that they would in [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat"). Additionally, [Ground Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ground_Vehicles "Ground Vehicles") and [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") use the same basic combat system, though [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") and [Airspeeder](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Airspeeder "Airspeeder") combat can take place at a different scale to make movement easier to handle at such high speeds. These changes should ease the transition when a hero dashes across the battlefield to hop into a [Speeder](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speeder "Speeder"), or when the party's space transport enters the atmosphere of an alien world and must ward off defending [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles").

## The Basics

[Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") and [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") combat is based on the rules for [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat"). In this way, transitioning to space combat is nearly as simple as changing which statistics you're using. In *Saga Edition*, players whose characters hop into an [X-Wing](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/X-Wing "X-Wing") will need to memorize far fewer variant rules to be effective in space combat. Moreover, the changes to the [Skill](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Skill "Skill") system make it easier for a hero to join in the fray even if they didn't invest many character resources in space combat abilities. Of course, certain characters will be better pilots than others, but the game now encourages most players to take an active hand in space combat, rather than waiting around on the ship for the scene to end.

Characters aboard [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") can take [Standard Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Actions "Standard Actions"), [Move Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Move_Actions "Move Actions"), and [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions"), just as they do in [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat"). [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") have their own movement speeds but otherwise move as characters do (though [Attacks of Opportunity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attacks_of_Opportunity "Attacks of Opportunity") are not a standard part of the space combat system). [Vehicle Pilots](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Pilots "Vehicle Pilots") and [Gunners](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gunners "Gunners") can use a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action") to fire [Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons") against targets in range, using their [Base Attack Bonus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Base_Attack_Bonus "Base Attack Bonus") and a bonus provided by the ship's targeting computers (a function of their [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence"), discussed below). Some [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") and [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") Actions, such as [Reroute Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reroute_Power "Reroute Power") and [Recharge Shields](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recharge_Shields "Recharge Shields"), use [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions"), and heroes firing [Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons") at targets with [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover") can use the [Aim](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Aim "Aim") Action to ignore [Cover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Cover "Cover").

In *Saga Edition*, all [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") and [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") have statistics that are very similar to character statistics. For example:

- A Vehicle's [Strength](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Strength "Strength") score measures the power of its engines, its structural integrity, and the general effects of its mass.
- A Vehicle's [Dexterity](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dexterity "Dexterity") score measures its maneuverability, agility, and quickness, contributing to its ability to avoid damage and to the pilot's ability to avoid hazards and perform maneuvers.
- A Vehicle's [Intelligence](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Intelligence "Intelligence") score measures the quality of its onboard computers, including communications and sensor systems as well as the fire control circuits of its weaponry.

Also like characters, [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") and [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") have a [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") and a [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") (but no [Will Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Will_Defense "Will Defense")). The [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") of a [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") works like the [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense") of a character and determines whether the ship is damaged by an incoming attack. Most ships have a base Armor bonus to their [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), but characters with Heroic Levels can replace the ship's Armor bonus with their Heroic Level (much in the same way that Heroic Levels also affects the character's [Defense Scores](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Defense_Scores "Defense Scores")). A ship's [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense") determines its hardiness, and helps in the calculation of the ship's [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold") (equal to the ship's [Fortitude Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Fortitude_Defense "Fortitude Defense"), plus a size bonus).

With regard to the [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") and [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") damage works in much the same way as character damage. The Vehicle's SR and DR reduce the damage, which is then subtracted from the Vehicle's [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"). If a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") takes damage greater than its [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), the [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") moves -1 step down the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track"), and it can't move back up until a character [Reroute Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reroute_Power "Reroute Power") on the ship (discussed below). If a ship falls to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points"), it becomes disabled. If the attack that reduces a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") or [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") to 0 [Hit Points](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hit_Points "Hit Points") also exceeds the target's [Damage Threshold](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Damage_Threshold "Damage Threshold"), the target is destroyed.

## Special Combat Actions

Despite these similarities, [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") and [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") still have some actions and maneuvers that are unique to their design. For example, [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships") and [Airspeeders](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Airspeeders "Airspeeders") can engage in [Dogfights](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dogfights "Dogfights"). When two [Vehicles](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicles "Vehicles") are engaged in a [Dogfight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dogfight "Dogfight"), they remain in place and make opposed [Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilot "Pilot") checks to determine which pilot gets the upper hand in the fight. Breaking out of a [Dogfight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dogfight "Dogfight") can be difficult, but some pilots have special abilities that grant them combat advantages while engaged in a [Dogfight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Dogfight "Dogfight").

Additionally, as a [Standard Action](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Standard_Action "Standard Action"), the pilot of a ship can move the vessel up to its normal [Speed](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Speed "Speed") (a minimum of 2 squares) in a straight line and then make an attack against a target in range. This grants the pilot a +2 bonus to his attack roll but applies a -2 penalty to the Vehicle's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"). The [Attack Run](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack_Run "Attack Run") is similar to a [Charge](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Charge "Charge") in [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat"), except that squares occupied by allies do not prevent you from making an [Attack Run](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Attack_Run "Attack Run") through those squares.

[Vehicle Pilots](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Pilots "Vehicle Pilots") and [System Operators](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/System_Operators "System Operators") aboard a [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") have several options in combat as well. While aboard a [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") with shields, a character can spend three [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") during the same turn (or on consecutive turns) to make a [Mechanics](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Mechanics "Mechanics") check, increasing the ship's [Shield Rating](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Shield_Rating "Shield Rating") by 5 (up to its normal maximum). Similarly, a character can spend three [Swift Actions](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Swift_Actions "Swift Actions") to [Reroute Power](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reroute_Power "Reroute Power"), moving the ship +1 step up the [Condition Track](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Condition_Track "Condition Track")- the equivalent of a character taking the [Recover](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Recover "Recover") Action in [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat").

## Character-Scale Crossover

In an effort to encourage more players to take an active part in [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat"), many [Character Scale](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Scale "Character Scale") abilities have been designed to work well while engaged in [Vehicle](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle "Vehicle") and [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") combat. In *Star Wars*, everyone from princesses to Jedi take an active hand in piloting and shooting aboard a [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship"). The *Saga Edition* rules encourage that without forcing characters to invest large amounts of resources (such as [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats"), and so on) into an aspect of the game that doesn't necessarily come up in every adventure. Instead, the rules allow heroes to use many of the abilities they already have. For example, all [Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons") are considered "[Heavy Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Weapons "Heavy Weapons")," meaning that a character who takes the [Weapon Proficiency (Heavy Weapons)](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Proficiency_(Heavy_Weapons) "Weapon Proficiency (Heavy Weapons)") Feat can hop into a quad laser turret and blast [TIE Fighters](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/TIE_Fighters "TIE Fighters") just as easily as they can use a [Heavy Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Heavy_Repeating_Blaster "Heavy Repeating Blaster") or an [E-Web Repeating Blaster](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/E-Web_Repeating_Blaster "E-Web Repeating Blaster").

This has an added benefit- [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") that affect [Weapon Groups](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Groups "Weapon Groups") (such as [Weapon Focus](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Weapon_Focus "Weapon Focus") or [Double Attack](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Double_Attack "Double Attack")) serve double duty in helping with [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat") and [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat"). For example, most [Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons") can be set to [Autofire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Autofire "Autofire") mode, allowing them to strafe targets on the ground with their very powerful weapons. Feats like [Rapid Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Rapid_Shot "Rapid Shot") and [Burst Fire](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Burst_Fire "Burst Fire"), which affect the way a character makes ranged attacks, work just as well with [Vehicle Weapons](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Weapons "Vehicle Weapons") as they do with character weapons, so heroes don't need to take multiple [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") to become more effective in space combat. Additionally, [Feats](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Feats "Feats") like [Point-Blank Shot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Point-Blank_Shot "Point-Blank Shot") work just as well at [Starship Scale](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship_Scale "Starship Scale") as they do at [Character Scale](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Scale "Character Scale").

Many abilities make use of "[Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight")" as a method of determining targeting. Some [Talents](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talents "Talents"), such as the **[Adept Negotiator](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Adept_Negotiator "Adept Negotiator")** Talent from the [Jedi Consular Talent Tree](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Jedi_Consular_Talent_Tree "Jedi Consular Talent Tree"), require the hero to have "[Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight")" to the target. Seeing a character over a viewscreen now counts as "[Line of Sight](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Line_of_Sight "Line of Sight")," which explains how [Darth Vader](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Darth_Vader "Darth Vader") was able to use *[Force Grip](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Force_Grip "Force Grip")* on Admiral [Kendal Ozzel](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Kendal_Ozzel?action=edit&redlink=1 "Kendal Ozzel (page does not exist)") while communicating with him shortly before the Battle of [Hoth](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Hoth?action=edit&redlink=1 "Hoth (page does not exist)").

Despite these crossovers between [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat") and [Character Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Character_Combat "Character Combat"), some characters are destined to excel at [Vehicle Combat](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Vehicle_Combat "Vehicle Combat"). For example, the [Ace Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Ace_Pilot "Ace Pilot") Prestige Class has access to new [Talent Trees](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Talent_Trees "Talent Trees") that allow those characters to be incredible [Pilots](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Pilots "Pilots") and exceptional [Gunners](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Gunners "Gunners"). They can also apply additional bonuses to the ship's [Reflex Defense](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Reflex_Defense "Reflex Defense"), making the Vehicle harder to hit in the heat of combat. Furthermore, the Prestige Class is designed for use with Ground Vehicles as well as [Airspeeders](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Airspeeders "Airspeeders") and [Starships](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starships "Starships"), which means that heroes who take levels in the class are not restricted to one or the other.

## The 181st Fighter Squadron

Let's look at an example of how a character designed for [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") combat can affect a ship. Presented here are the statistics for a [181st Imperial Pilot](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/181st_Imperial_Pilot "181st Imperial Pilot") from the Empire's elite 181st Fighter Squadron, along with statistics for a [181st TIE Interceptor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/181st_TIE_Interceptor "181st TIE Interceptor") with said pilot at the stick. Note that these statistics don't represent normal [TIE Interceptor](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/TIE_Interceptor "TIE Interceptor") stats, but rather those for an elite [Starfighter](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starfighter "Starfighter") far beyond the rest of the pilots in the Imperial fleet

## *Star Wars*: The Musical Experience

Producing a Soundtrack for your GamesBringing Balance to the MusicMusic from the Outer Rim

*By Patrick Stutzman*

Take a moment and play your copy of *Star Wars*: Episode I *The Phantom Menace*. Fast-forward to the [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") battle between [Obi-Wan Kenobi](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Obi-Wan_Kenobi "Obi-Wan Kenobi") and [Darth Maul](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Darth_Maul "Darth Maul") near the end and watch it. Now that you have your fill of excitement, rewind the battle and watch it again, but this time, mute your television or speakers.

Does it seem different? Is the scene not quite as exciting? Perhaps it's because you're missing the sounds of the [Lightsabers](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsabers "Lightsabers") clashing or the combatants' footsteps. That's definitely part of it, but what really makes this particular scene stand out is the music. Musical soundtracks play a key role in the way that we perceive movies, and music can enhance the atmosphere and add to the enjoyment of our games as well.

To best determine what music to use throughout the game, you need a basic idea of how music works with film to create a spectacular cinematic experience. You do not need to be a musicologist or any kind of musical expert to put together a soundtrack. As long as you have watched any of the *Star Wars* movies, you have a good starting knowledge.

If you listen to any movie soundtrack, whether it's from *Star Wars*, *The Godfather*, or even *Braveheart*, basic themes can be heard throughout the entire score. These themes connect with a particular character, location, or event in the story and may appear multiple times throughout the movie. These themes often spring to mind when we think about that character or place long after the film is done. For example, think of the "Imperial March," first introduced in *The Empire Strikes Back*. Odds are that as you listen to the music in your mind, a scene involving Darth Vader is also playing in your mind. The film score effectively ties the theme to the character and lets us know what is happening in the movie while we watch it.

In addition to themes, the style of music plays an integral part in the scene. The [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") battle we watched earlier has "Duel of the Fates" playing in the background. As we watch Obi-Wan fight for his life against [Darth Maul](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Darth_Maul "Darth Maul"), the fast-paced strings and brass combined with the haunting lines from the chorus pull us to the edge of our seats. The scene would be totally different, not to mention unacceptable, if a slow and melodic theme like "Anakin's Dream" from *Revenge of the Sith* was playing instead.

## Fast Tracks

When you piece together your soundtrack, it's important to know when to use certain tracks. As mentioned above, using the wrong music can destroy the mood and imagery that you intend to create. Unlike movies, having appropriate music playing all the way through our game sessions may not be feasible, so we have to know what potential scenes require specific music.

Fast-paced scenes can be broken down into two basic types: chases and combat. Although this sounds pretty simple, they can be further defined by subtypes. For our purposes, let's break combat down into ground battles, [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") duels, and [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") battles.

### Chase Music

Chases, by definition, are simply one force pursuing another. Whether the chase takes place on foot through the alleys of Mos Eisley, in [Airspeeders](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Airspeeders "Airspeeders") through the skylanes of [Coruscant](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Coruscant "Coruscant"), or in ships through the vastness of space, chase music can be characterized by quick passages and menacing themes that intensify the threat of pursuit.

Examples of good chase music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Escape from Naboo" (*The Phantom Menace* Ultimate Edition, CD1, Track 15)
- "Zam the Assassin/The Chase Through Coruscant" (*Attack of the Clones*, Track 3, Start at 1:03)
- "General Grievous" (*Revenge of the Sith*, Track 5)
- "The *Millennium Falcon*/Imperial Cruiser Pursuit" (*A New Hope*, CD2, Track 2, Start at 1:51)
- "Rescue from Cloud City/Hyperspace" (*The Empire Strikes Back*, CD2, Track 11, Start at 3:51)
- "The Battle of Endor III" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD2, Track 3, End at 1:58)

### Combat Music

It's easy to find music for combat scenes. The name of the saga itself implies lots of battles, and the movies deliver, with fights from orbit around various planets, on planet surfaces, and in some cases, in the ocean's depths. Combat music includes rapid passages that replicate exciting attacks and intimidating themes to accentuate the enemies' actions, in addition to the ever-present heroic Star Wars music for climactic moments.

Examples of good ground combat music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Fighting the Destroyer Droids" (*The Phantom Menace* Ultimate Edition, CD1, Track 5, Start at 0:24)
- "Love Pledge/The Arena" (*Attack of the Clones*, Track 12, Start at 1:53)
- "Grievous and the Droids" (*Revenge of the Sith*, Track 7, Start at 1:12)
- "Shootout in the Cell Bay/Dianoga" (*A New Hope*, CD2, Track 6, End at 2:12)
- "The Battle of Hoth" (*The Empire Strikes Back*, CD1, Track 5, Start at 2:05)
- "The Battle of Endor I" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD2, Track 5)

Examples of good Jedi/[Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") duel music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Duel of the Fates" (*The Phantom Menace*, Track 2)
- "Battle of the Heroes" (*Revenge of the Sith*, Track 3)
- "The Training of a Jedi Knight/The Magic Tree" (*The Empire Strikes Back*, CD1, Track 11, Start at 2:14)
- "The Battle of Endor II" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD2, Track 7, Start at 4:52)

Examples of good [Starship](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Starship "Starship") combat music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Panaka and the Queen's Protectors" (*The Phantom Menace*, Track 12)
- "Bounty Hunter's Pursuit" (*Attack of the Clones*, Track 9, End at 1:16)
- "*Star Wars*/The Revenge of the Sith" (*Revenge of the Sith*, Track 1, Start at 1:24)
- "Ben Kenobi's Death/TIE Fighter Attack" (*A New Hope*, CD2, Track 9, Start at 1:32)
- "The Battle of Yavin" (*A New Hope*, CD2, Track 10)
- "The Asteroid Field" (*The Empire Strikes Back*, CD1, Track 6)
- "The Battle of Endor III" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD2, Track 8, End at 1:57)

## Finding Your Music

This article (and subsequent articles in the series) refers to various passages of music and provides information to help you find and use them in your game. These notes include the name of the movie and/or soundtrack title, the disc within the album, the track number, and any indicators of time within the track.

The soundtracks used for these articles include the Special Edition releases for the original trilogy (first released in 1997), the Ultimate Edition of the Episode I soundtrack (released in 2000), and the standard soundtracks for Episodes II and III. All of these soundtracks are available through Amazon.com, though the Special Edition releases for the original trilogy are now available bundled in one set instead of separated by movie. If you have different versions of the soundtracks, use the track titles and any CD liner notes to help you locate the music you seek.

*By Patrick Stutzman*

Have you ever found yourself sitting in your car, listening to a favorite song, when another car pulls up next to you with the driver's music turned up so loud that you thought it would shatter windows? To say the least, that can be rather annoying and disruptive of your own personal musical enjoyment. Now, ask yourself if you've experienced a similar situation in a *Star Wars* roleplaying game. Probably not- most games don't have a soundtrack built for them, let alone played during the game. However, that doesn't give you the excuse to create a disruption once you have incorporated music into your own game. In this article, we look at the proper use of music in your games as a complementary element and ways to minimize any musical disruptions.

Undeniably, adding music can bring your game to a whole new level. It can evoke emotions and moods while bringing a more cinematic feel to the stories that you and your players create. However, it's important to know how much music is appropriate. As Master [Yoda](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Yoda "Yoda") said, "Control! You must learn control!" Knowing where to apply that control will help keep the music a blessing and not a curse. To that end, we'll focus on three aspects of music that could make it disruptive- volume, style, and quantity- and we'll look at how we can keep it from causing trouble.

## Volume

John Williams did a masterful job of composing the music for the *Star Wars* movies, and you probably enjoy listening to it just as much as the next fan. However, playing the music too loudly during your game can cause problems. The most obvious problem is that you and the other players can't hear each other as well as you should, causing communication breakdowns at the table. Depending on your location, this can occur even if the volume isn't very high. If your gaming room doesn't have a carpeted floor, curtains, or similar furnishings, you may find that your players are competing with the music to be heard. Another problem becomes apparent if you're running a game at the local gaming store or hobby shop. If other groups are present while you're playing music too loud, it can disrupt their games as well and could lead to awkward social situations in which your plastic [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") will not help you very much.

Finding the right volume for your music is vital to keeping it from overpowering the game. The best way to gauge this is to set the volume below the level at which you speak normally. Keep in mind that the right volume may depend on the device being used to deliver the music. If you use a stereo system or portable CD/MP3 player with speakers that face the players, your volume will need to be lower than if you use a laptop computer with the speakers facing you. The important thing is to make sure that you don't drown out your voice or the voices of the other players.

## Style

"Producing a Soundtrack for Your Games" mentioned that the style of music playing in the background can affect the mood of the players. Playing a lighthearted piece of music during the adventure's climactic [Lightsaber](https://swse.miraheze.org/wiki/Lightsaber "Lightsaber") duel will quickly turn the final battle into a slapstick comedy. In fact, studies of mood and its reactions to music showed that people demonstrated differences in spatial reasoning after listening to a Mozart sonata as compared to a relaxation instruction tape. It seems likely that this reaction could carry over to other activities and musical genres.

In the case of *Star Wars* roleplaying, musical styles that contribute to the activity taking place in the game are better than those that might distract from the scene. Do a little homework- find which tracks work best for the scenes you want to have take place during your gaming sessions, and prepare them ahead of time. Create a playlist on your computer or burn a CD with the specific tracks you want, and write notes indicating when to start and stop each track. Setting up your music in advance can keep the game from bogging down or going off the rails.

## Quantity

Determining the right amount of music to use during a game is just as important as having the right selection of tracks. As with just about anything, too much of a good thing can ruin it. For example, avoid playing the same track repeatedly during the same scene. The movies are very good at providing music all the way through a scene. However, a single combat in your game might take longer to unfold than you expected, and most of us don't have a musical composer at the ready to fill in the gaps. To put it simply, don't worry about filling an entire scene with music, especially if it would mean using the same track over and over again. Playing your chosen piece once through should be sufficient to set the mood and help the players focus on what is about to transpire. If a track seems too short, try finding another track with a similar sound and play them back to back.

At the same time, don't use too little music during a game. If you play music only during combat or important events, the players won't have a chance to get used to it, and the tracks might distract them. The idea is to let the music's presence feel natural instead of something you've thrown in. To that end, play music at other times to emphasize other scenes in the adventure that may not be highly intense or combat oriented. For example, playing a quiet track to underscore the importance of arriving at a key destination may help the players get into the scene as you describe the setting.

Using music in your games can be a great tool to help make the experience more exhilarating and memorable. If done incorrectly, it can be more trouble than originally anticipated and become more of a nuisance. When preparing tracks for your next session, keep volume, style, and quantity in mind to help create a good balance, and you may find your players craving more time at the game table.

## (Not So) Fast Tracks

"Producing a Soundtrack for Your Games" focused on musical excerpts that would work well with fast-paced scenes like combat and chases. This time around, we'll look at pieces of *Star Wars* music that might be better suited for scenes that aren't as intense. These not-so-fast scenes can be classified as celebrations, encounters (with aliens or enemies), and views (of establishments and landscapes).

### Celebration Music

Simply put, celebrations are occasions when a group rejoices over an event. In a *Star Wars* game, a celebration may occur when a group of combatants claims victory in battle. As the fanfares proclaim their triumph over the forces of evil, the players can sit back and revel in their glory.

Examples of good celebration music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Hail to the Winner, Anakin Skywalker" (*The Phantom Menace* Ultimate Edition, CD1, Track 34, End at 0:40)
- "The Parade" (*The Phantom Menace* Ultimate Edition, CD2, Track 31)
- "Throne Room/End Title" (*A New Hope*, CD2, Track 11)
- "Victory Celebration/End Title" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD2, Track 10)

### Alien Encounter Music

Encounters deal with meeting others for a variety of reasons. The *Star Wars* universe includes a wide assortment of alien races and cultures, and we want our music to reflect the mystery and occasional humor that can accompany such encounters.

Examples of good alien encounter music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Jar Jar's Introduction and the Swim to Otoh Gunga" (*The Phantom Menace*, Track 4, End at 1:22)
- "The Dune Sea of Tatooine/Jawa Sandcrawler" (*A New Hope*, CD1, Track 4)
- "Arrival on Dagobah" (*The Empire Strikes Back*, CD1, Track 7)
- "Ewok Feast/Part of the Tribe" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD2, Track 11)

### Enemy Encounter Music

In any good gaming experience, the heroes eventually come face to face with the enemy. When that happens, we should have appropriate music ready. To build tension as forces prepare to clash, tracks might feature low, somber tones and slow melodic lines or quick passages with haunting voices or strings rising in volume to a climax.

Examples of good enemy encounter music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Enter Darth Maul" (*The Phantom Menace* Ultimate Edition, CD1, Track 16)
- "Zam the Assassin" (*Attack of the Clones*, Track 3, End at 0:24)
- "Grievous Speaks to Lord Sidious" (*Revenge of the Sith*, Track 13, End at 0:51)
- "Imperial Attack" (*A New Hope*, CD1, Track 3, End at 0:59)
- "Imperial Starfleet Deployed/City in the Clouds" (*The Empire Strikes Back*, CD2, Track 5, End at 1:13)
- "Shuttle Tydirium Approaches Endor" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD1, Track 10)

### Establishment Music

As the characters travel the galaxy, they will surely find an endless number of locales that draw their attention. Using music to emphasize the wonder of a view should help the players tune in to the description of the scene. Many times, the heroes arrive at some sort of establishment or building, typically a cantina or night club, and look around before they enter. The style of music may vary, depending on the type of location.

Examples of good establishment view music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Mos Espa Arena Band" (*The Phantom Menace* Ultimate Edition, CD1, Track 29)
- "Cantina Band" (*A New Hope*, CD1, Track 11)
- "Jabba's Baroque Recital" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD1, Track 13)
- "Jedi Rocks" (*Return of the Jedi*, CD1, Track 14)

### Landscape Music

Landscapes can provide a breathtaking view of nature's beauty, especially when the heroes first arrive on a new planet. As they peer over the area that they are about to enter, such as a busy starport or a misty mountain range, the right music can enhance their appreciation for the scene.

Examples of good landscape view music from the *Star Wars* soundtracks include:

- "Swimming to Otoh Gunga" (*The Phantom Menace* Ultimate Edition, CD1, Track 8)
- "Departing Coruscant" (*Attack of the Clones*, Track 5)
- "Return to Tatooine" (*Attack of the Clones*, Track 10, End at 1:15)
- "Mos Eisley Spaceport" (*A New Hope*, CD1, Track 10)
- "The City in the Clouds" (*The Empire Strikes Back*, CD2, Track 5, Start at 3:44)

*By Patrick Stutzman*

The music of the *Star Wars* saga helps set it apart from other films with its distinct themes and style. In fact, one could say that the soundtrack is a contributing factor that has helped *Star Wars* remain popular as long as it has. Many people believe that, if Lucas had selected contemporary works from the 1970s for Episode IV's soundtrack, the saga would not have made it past the first movie. At the same time, one may wonder what *Star Wars* would have been like if other music had been used. In this installment, we explore that very idea by offering tips and suggestions for you to import music into your game from sources outside of the *Star Wars* movies.

## Using Other Music

When is it appropriate for other music to be used in your game? Many *Star Wars* purists would cry, "Never!" By not using other sources, however, you may limit the potential for a lot of great music to enhance your adventures. Certain scenarios not originally conceived in the saga may present themselves in your game, and music may help bring a sense of cohesiveness. And if the track you seek is not found in the music of the six movies, you have no choice but to look elsewhere.

While you peruse other albums to find the perfect track, keep certain elements in mind. First, focus on strictly instrumental music. Hearing words to a particular song playing in the background can be one of the biggest distractions for you as well as your players. Many players have complained about a fellow gamer or even their GM breaking out into song in the middle of combat because they started singing along with the song that is supposed to augment the game. To put it bluntly, lyrics are bad! The only exception is music with an element of choral voices singing in a foreign or created language. "Duel of the Fates" is a prime example of this type.

Second, be careful of tracks from other movies that include familiar themes. If one of your players recognizes that theme, you may find your group suddenly talking about the movie it came from instead of concentrating on the game. Worse, they might jump universes in the middle of your epic *Star Wars* battle and start talking about a particular scene out of *Star Trek II: The Wrath of Khan*. Although Captain Kirk and his away team won't appear to shoot your Sith Lord with phasers on stun, the interruption created by the theme has already been made and will take a lot of work during that session to fix.

Finally (and probably one of the most important suggestions), strive to find music with a similar sound and feel as the *Star Wars* movie soundtracks. For example, even though John Williams is probably best known for his *Star Wars* compositions, he has written music for other movies that could easily fit into your game without the other players noticing much difference. Some examples that come to mind are the *Harry Potter*, *Indiana Jones*, and *Jurassic Park* movies- just watch out for tracks with overly familiar themes, as noted above.

Other composers have written music for movies that could prove to be valuable in filling your gaming music library. Composers such as Danny Elfman (*Batman*, *Men in Black*, *Spider-Man*), James Horner (*Braveheart*, *Star Trek II: The Wrath of Khan*, *Titanic*), and Graeme Revell (*The Crow*, *Pitch Black*, *Sin City*) have scores that you may find useful for your game's soundtrack. Also, don't discount classical compositions, which run the gamut of styles and can contribute to a colorful soundtrack. Music from the Romantic period (19th century), such as the works of Schubert and Tchaikovsky and the later works of Beethoven, are highly suggested.

Depending on what style of game you run, you might be able to use other sources of *Star Wars* music, such as videogames and computer games. They might allow you to open their music folder without necessarily playing through the game first. In fact, some of the game's music might be available online for you to download. For example, the music from *Star Wars Republic Commando*, once available from LucasArts.com as a free download, is available on fan websites.

## *Dragonheart* Tracks

The possibilities for new music for your *Star Wars* game are virtually endless. But you have to know what to look for before you can find the tracks you want. The tips and selections in the previous articles ("Producing a Soundtrack for Your Games" and "Bringing Balance to the Music") give examples of the type of music to seek when assembling your soundtrack.

To assist you further, let's analyze the soundtrack from another movie- *Dragonheart*, composed by Randy Edelman. As with the examples from the *Star Wars* films, we'll categorize the music according to how the selections might be used, along with time references for easy location of each excerpt. Some of these selections are short, perhaps just 30 to 40 seconds. Although that's not an ideal length of time for a track, you might find that it's all you need for a given scene.

### Chase Music

Examples of good chase music from the *Dragonheart* soundtracks include:

- "Einon" (Track 4, Start at 3:20)
- "A Refreshing Swim" (Track 9)

### Combat Music

Examples of good combat music from the *Dragonheart* soundtracks include:

- "Bowen's Decoy" (Track 11, Start at 1:03, End at 1:51)
- "Kyle, The Wheat Boy" (Track 12, Start at 1:49, End at 2:44)
- "Flight To Avalon" (Track 14, Start at 1:02, End at 1:44)
- "Bowen's Ride" (Track 6, Start at 0:28, End at 0:49); Lightsaber Duels
- "Flight to Avalon" (Track 14, Start at 0:07, End at 0:48); Starship Battles

### Alien Encounter Music

Examples of good alien encounter music from the *Dragonheart* soundtracks include:

- "The Last Dragon Slayer" (Track 5, End at 1:20)

### Enemy Encounter Music
